---
title: "v0.8 - UI/UX & Graphical Fidelity"
weight: 80
---

Seven releases that make the interface the reason to use gPTY. v0.8.0 re-baselines the October 2026 audit, commits the capture harness, and lays down the shared theme, type scale, colour tokens, light theme and accessibility baseline. v0.8.1 is pane chrome, palette and discoverability. v0.8.2 is the pane-interaction and responsiveness fixes that came out of daily use - explicit close affordances, a saved empty workspace, the active-pane and close-fallback repairs, the clamped dialogs, the flood cadence and the workspace-switch cost. v0.8.3 is small windows, sidebar space and the remaining pane responsiveness work. v0.8.4 is pane configuration, launch commands and profile identity, plus the last defects pulled forward from the visual release - a spawn-directory mismatch, a short-scheme palette reset and content-sized cards - and the capture-harness compile fix that makes its evidence honest. v0.8.5 is the repair patch the v0.8.4 tag forced - a tag ruleset that forbade creating tags at all, and a macOS archive whose alias fix never reached the transport - and it carries release-pipeline work only. v0.8.6 is the Godot-native visual layer - shaders, pane transitions, reactive environments and a physical 3D vault graph - and the release capture. v0.8.7 is the finish pass those leave open: one switch behind every animation, a scheme-owned backdrop, elevation for floating surfaces, and a focus change the eye can follow. Every row in this chain is UI/UX work or the capture tooling that records it; build, security, MCP, distribution and promotion work lives in v0.9.

## v0.8.0 - Audit Baseline & Design System

| Done | Area | Item |
|:---:|:---:|---|
| ✅ | GUI | **Re-baseline the audit on the 0.7.2 build** - re-ran the October 2026 report's method on a fresh build of this tree (app version 0.7.2, commit `41d6957`): a private sandbox with a fresh first-run state, the real main scene/autoloads/Rust extension, synthetic clicks and keys through `Input.parse_input_event`, and captures of the app's own viewport at 1920×1080 plus 800×500 in an embedded `SubViewport` (method: AGENTS.md, "UI/UX assessment method"). All nine findings the report carried are still open, none was closed by the 0.7.x work, and one is narrowed: the Pane Settings Environment field is now 60 px tall instead of one line. The two closed v0.7.0 behaviours held: the first terminal is focused at launch (`first_focused: true`), and Close All over a pane running `sleep 30` raised "Stop running processes? … Close anyway?" with Cancel leaving the pane alive. The report's "what worked well" list held too: crisp glyphs, the Phosphor set with one accent blue, ellipsized sidebar names (`Agent Worksp…`), the status bar's active-pane label, profile-activation feedback, the Wiki/Media empty states and tooltips on every icon-only button. The design-system counts moved but not to a scale: 73 `add_theme_*_override` calls (was 160), 37 hard-coded `Color(...)` literals (was 41), 57 of 71 literal font sizes still at 11–12 px, status-bar right text 4.15:1 and Close All red 3.50:1 (both below 4.5:1), no `Theme` resource, no UI-scale setting and no light theme. The pass also found a defect no capture keyed: with an overlay open the keyboard still belongs to the terminal behind it - typing `x` with Settings open reached the shell, and Escape closed neither Settings nor Pane Settings nor the palette - queued below as **An open overlay neither takes keyboard focus nor blocks the terminal behind it**. The ten captures are the `issue-v0.7.2-*` images under Screenshots at the bottom of this page; the run again needed a scratch driver (`godot/tmp_capture.gd`, deleted after the pass), which is what the capture-harness row below removes. |
| ✅ | GUI | **The UI audit has a committed capture harness** - landed 2026-11-03: `godot/tests/bench/ui_capture.gd` stages named states - the original nine (first launch, 4-pane sidebar, settings, pane settings, palette, empty workspace, 1280×760 workspace, 800×500, 560×340) and the five added by **Extend the assessment to the surfaces it did not cover** (wiki vault, wiki graph, concept editor, plugin review, workspace trust) - through the real main scene and autoloads and writes `<out>/<state>.png` from the app's own viewport. Pane sets are seeded through `WorkspaceStore` (the restore path); interactions are synthetic - a click on the sidebar Settings button, a click on the pane title bar's settings button, Ctrl+Shift+P, and Close All plus its confirmation's OK button - and a click that stops opening its overlay fails the run instead of capturing a wrong state. Determinism is in the driver: cursor blink off, the status bar's clock and FPS pinned (new `StatusBar.freeze_readouts()`), the pointer parked, and the capture waits for every grid generation, pane title and the toast queue to settle; all nine states measured pixel-identical across two runs in separate sandboxes. `scripts/ui-capture` owns the sandbox (`scripts/ui-sandbox`) plus `--list`/`--compare`; `scripts/ui-capture-check` asserts the two-run identity and that `--perturb settings` changes exactly that one image; `scripts/screenshot-gpty --state <name>` stages the same states for desktop captures and waits for the harness's ready file. Docs: testing.md's method, `skill://gpty-ui-review`, `skill://release`, and pitfalls (`-s` scripts compile before autoload identifiers exist). Found 2026-10-30 while deciding whether a UI skill could pay for itself: the procedure is what needs committing, not a description of it. |
| ✅ | BUILD | **The release screenshot script captures a doomed second instance when a GUI is running** - fixed 2026-11-03: `scripts/ui-sandbox` creates one 0700 temp root and exports `HOME`, `XDG_DATA_HOME`/`XDG_CONFIG_HOME`/`XDG_STATE_HOME`/`XDG_CACHE_HOME`/`XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, `GPTY_TEST_USER_DIR` and `GPTY_SOCKET`, and clears `GPTY_SECRET` plus `BASH_ENV`/`ENV`/`PROMPT_COMMAND`/`PS1`/`PS2`/`CDPATH`/`HISTFILE` (a pane's shell no longer reads a personal `.bashrc` into the image). `scripts/screenshot-gpty`, `scripts/ui-capture` and `scripts/ui-capture-check` all source it, so a capture never depends on the developer's desktop state. Verified 2026-11-03 while a real gPTY 0.7.2 GUI owned the desktop's control socket: the full capture check (27 staged launches) ran normally, and `--state` waits for the harness's ready file so the frame is captured after staging instead of on whatever frame the window first showed. Found 2026-10-19 while capturing the v0.6.0 screenshot. |
| ✅ | GUI | **Extend the assessment to the surfaces it did not cover** - extended 2026-11-03 on the 0.7.2 build with five committed states (`wiki-vault`, `wiki-graph`, `concept-editor`, `plugin-review`, `workspace-trust`) in `godot/tests/bench/ui_capture.gd`, and with `scripts/ui-capture --audit-focus <state>` printing what keyboard traversal can reach (the focus owner, the visible dialog's own focus owner, whether Escape closes the surface, every visible `FOCUS_NONE` button, and the Tab order). Measured: the Wiki pane's toolbar and link rows (52 `FOCUS_NONE` buttons on the index note), every pane title bar (five buttons per pane) and the link graph's three controls plus its canvas are mouse-only; a keyboard user who opens a note from the list loses the list and the search and cannot go back; the concept editor's canvas is translucent over the Settings panel and the sidebar; the graph draws overlapping note titles at 52 notes / 267 links; the agent-state badge tells failed from needs-attention by colour alone. The trust surfaces themselves held: both dialogs take keyboard focus (Plugin Install Review on Install, Workspace Trust on Restore) and close on Escape. The platform/display half (real OS resizing, 4K/HiDPI, Windows/macOS window behaviour) cannot be staged in the committed harness and is carried to the v0.8.6 recapture. Findings queued as **The keyboard cannot drive pane chrome or the Wiki pane**, **The vault link graph is mouse-only**, **The vault graph's labels overlap at the bundled vault's density** and **The agent-state badge distinguishes failed from needs-attention by colour alone**; the pane-padding, opaque-overlay, dialog and accessibility rows absorbed the rest. Screenshots (under Screenshots below): *Wiki pane with the bundled vault*, *Vault link graph*, *Concept graph editor*, *Plugin Install Review*, *Workspace Trust*. |
| ✅ | GUI | **An open overlay neither takes keyboard focus nor blocks the terminal behind it** - measured 2026-11-03 on 0.7.2: with Settings open and a terminal focused, typing `x` reached the shell and Escape closed neither Settings, Pane Settings nor the command palette, because a focused pane consumes every key in `_gui_input` (`accept_event()` on its fall-through path) after writing the raw ESC to the child, so each overlay's `_unhandled_input` Escape handler never ran; the palette's field also focused nothing (`find_child("*")` returned the palette's `VBoxContainer`). The concept editor was the exception (it grabbed focus and Escape closed it). Fixed 2026-11-04: the four overlays (Settings, Pane Settings, the palette, the concept editor) extend `OverlayPanel` and take the keyboard on open - `open()` remembers the focus owner and focuses a control inside (`_initial_focus()`, else the first reachable descendant), so a typed character goes to the overlay; Escape is handled in `Workspace._input`, before GUI input, with `_keyboard_overlay()` naming the topmost visible overlay; `close()` emits `keyboard_released` only when the overlay still owned the keyboard (a click that already moved focus is never overridden), and the workspace then focuses the overlay underneath (the concept editor returns to Settings) or the active pane; a click behind an open overlay no longer activates a pane. The palette moved to `ui/command_palette.gd`, which fixes its focus target. Measured 2026-11-04 with `scripts/ui-capture --audit-focus`: settings, pane-settings and palette now report the focus owner inside the overlay and `escape ... closed=true` (before: `focus-owner=.../Body` and `closed=false` for pane settings and the palette). Tests: `godot/tests/integration/test_overlay_focus.gd` (a real shell per run: typing never reaches the PTY, Escape leaves the grid unchanged and the next typed command still runs, focus returns to the terminal, the editor returns to Settings, a palette spawn keeps the new pane, a click behind an overlay activates nothing) and `godot/tests/unit/test_overlay_panel.gd`; `scripts/ui-capture-check` is still pixel-identical on the four states. Rule: `docs/content/docs/agents/gdscript.md` ("Overlay keyboard ownership"). |
| ✅ | GUI | **The keyboard cannot drive pane chrome or the Wiki pane** - measured 2026-11-03 on 0.7.2 with `--audit-focus`: every pane title bar's five buttons were `FOCUS_NONE` (20 mouse-only buttons in the four-pane state) and the Wiki pane's Back, New, Graph, Edit/Split/Preview, Rename, Delete, Save and every link row likewise (52 on the index note), and opening a note with Enter hid the list and the search while nothing handled Escape - the editor consumes Tab for indentation, so a note was a hard keyboard trap. Fixed 2026-11-04: the five title-bar buttons are `FOCUS_ALL` with an explicit `focus_next`/`focus_previous` ring (`TerminalManager._wire_chrome_keyboard`), the body's own traversal continues into its first button, `Ctrl+Shift+E` (`Workspace._focus_active_pane_chrome`) is the way in from a terminal (which consumes Tab for its child; it does nothing while an overlay owns the keyboard), Escape hands the keyboard back to the pane (a terminal takes it itself; a read-only pane's first reachable control takes it, `KeyboardFocus.first_reachable`) (`Workspace._escape_pane_chrome`), and a chrome action that leaves the pane on screen returns focus to it. The Wiki pane's controls and link rows are ordinary tab stops, opening a note focuses the buffer (or Back for a note past the 1 MiB cap), leaving focuses the list, and Escape is Back behind the existing Save/Discard/Cancel guard. Measured 2026-11-04: `--audit-focus sidebar` reports `focus-none-buttons=0` (was 20) and a tab-order that enters `TitleBarButtons/{Minimize,SwapPosition,ChangeType,PaneSettings,ClosePane}` and wraps; `--audit-focus wiki-vault` reports `focus-none-buttons=0` (was 52). Godot finding: `_gui_input` is delivered to the focused control only and does not bubble to ancestors on 4.7.2 (measured), so both Escape paths read the input stage and scope themselves with the focus owner. Tests: `test_title_bar_keyboard.gd` (ring, entry, Escape back, overlay guard, restore returns the keyboard), `test_wiki_pane.gd` (Escape from a clean note writes nothing and focuses the list, Escape on a dirty note asks and Cancel keeps it, no visible wiki action is mouse-only), `test_keyboard.gd` (Escape still reaches the child as ESC). Docs: the keyboard-shortcuts wiki page, the GDScript page (pane chrome is a keyboard toolbar) and the pitfalls page (the bubbling trap). |
| ✅ | WIKI | **The note toolbar is unreachable while the buffer has focus** - found 2026-11-04 while closing **The keyboard cannot drive pane chrome or the Wiki pane**, and measured on that fix build: the note view's controls are now tab stops, but a keyboard user who is editing cannot reach them - a focused `CodeEdit` consumes Tab for indentation, Escape leaves the note (Back is the fix for the trap, not a focus move), and the toolbar sits before the editor in tree order, so the only path to Rename/Delete/Edit-Split-Preview while typing is to leave the note, reopen it, or click. Fixed 2026-11-04: the toolbar (Edit/Split/Preview, Rename, Delete, Save) is one explicit traversal ring built from `KeyboardFocus.reachable_children`/`KeyboardFocus.ring` - the pair pane chrome now uses too - so Tab wraps inside the bar; `Ctrl+Shift+K` enters it (`Workspace._focus_note_toolbar`) and Escape returns to the buffer (`WikiPane._return_from_note_toolbar`) before the buffer's own Escape leaves the note, one level out per press. The chord resolves its pane from the focus owner first: `last_body` alone was measured wrong (a wiki pane opened from its own list kept the startup terminal as the active pane, so a `last_body`-only rule never fired from the buffer the chord exists for). `PaneBody.keyboard_entry()` is the new contract behind the row's third half: entering the pane from its chrome (`TerminalManager._return_focus_to_body`) now lands in the note buffer instead of Back. Inert with no buffer on screen (no note open, `preview` mode), where the toolbar is already one Tab from Back. Test: `test_wiki_note_keyboard.gd` - the chord from the focused buffer lands on the first toolbar button with `last_body` pointing elsewhere, Escape returns to the buffer without leaving the note and only the second Escape leaves it, the ring covers modes/Rename/Delete/Save in both directions, the chord is inert in the list view and in preview mode, and chrome Escape aims at the buffer. `--audit-focus wiki-vault` on the built app: 0 mouse-only buttons of 178 controls (its tab-order walk still stops inside the title-bar ring - **The focus audit stops inside the first keyboard ring** below). |
| ✅ | BUILD | **`ui-capture-check` refuses a state subset that leaves out `settings`** - found 2026-11-06 while adding the `file-dialog` capture state: the usage line is `scripts/ui-capture-check [--keep <dir>] [state...]`, but `PERTURB` is fixed to `settings`, so naming any other state alone fails with `perturb state 'settings' is not in the state list` instead of checking the state the caller named - the natural invocation for one new state cannot run. Fix: default `PERTURB` to the first state in the list (or add a `--perturb` flag), so the locality half stays meaningful for any subset. Fixed 2026-11-06: `PERTURB` defaults to `settings` when the list has it and to the first named state otherwise (`--perturb` still overrides), and a perturbed capture of a state with neither an overlay nor a pane no longer silently no-ops - `_perturb(state_name)` fails with `state '<name>' has no overlay or pane to perturb`, and the check reports "no perturbed capture produced an image" instead of restating that as "the perturbed control changed no image". Verified on the development build: `scripts/ui-capture-check --keep <dir> file-dialog` passes (two runs pixel-identical, only `file-dialog` changed, 11 s), `settings sidebar` still perturbs `settings`, `empty-workspace` exits 1 with the harness's own sentence, and `bash -n` is clean. |
| ✅ | WIKI | **The vault link graph is mouse-only** - measured 2026-11-03 in `wiki-graph`: the graph is the only pane surface and it had zero focusable controls - `Arrows`, `Relayout` and `Fit` were `FOCUS_NONE`, and the canvas was a `FOCUS_NONE` `Control` whose drag, wheel zoom, click-to-open and right-click weight menu all lacked a key path, so the Tab order started in the sidebar and the map could not be operated at all without a pointer. Fixed 2026-11-04: the canvas and its three actions are one explicit ring with the canvas first (`KeyboardFocus.ring`), so all four are tab stops and the canvas draws its own accent focus frame (a custom-drawn `Control` has no stylebox to do it); arrows pan (`PAN_STEP` 48 px, Shift x4), `+`/`-` zoom about the crosshair, and Enter acts on what the crosshair covers - a note opens, a ghost resolves, an edge opens its declared-weight menu - through the same `VaultGraphModel.hit_test`/`hit_test_edge` the pointer uses (`VaultGraphView.cursor_hit`, one answer for the draw and the gesture, so the highlight cannot disagree with the action). The crosshair is the canvas centre, drawn only while the canvas holds the keyboard, and the node under it gets its own ring and label. Opening the graph hands the keyboard to the canvas (the Graph button, and `WikiPane.keyboard_entry` for the chrome route), and Escape still leaves for the list. Measured with `--audit-focus wiki-graph`: 3 mouse-only buttons and a mouse-only canvas (35 focusable) before, 0 and 39 after, focus-owner the canvas, tab-order canvas -> Arrows -> Relayout -> Fit. Tests: `test_vault_graph_view.gd` (the ring in both directions, arrow/Shift pan, zoom about the crosshair, Enter on a node, a ghost and an edge), `test_wiki_pane.gd` (the Graph button hands focus over; Escape returns to the list). |
| ✅ | WIKI | **The vault graph's labels overlap at the bundled vault's density** - measured 2026-11-03 on the 52-note / 267-link bundled vault: labels were drawn for every node at the fitted zoom and two titles printed on top of each other at the top of the map (the `wiki-graph` capture), so note identity was unreadable where the map is most useful; the first fit used node positions only (`VaultGraphModel.bounds`), not label extents. Fixed 2026-11-04: labels are placed, not drawn per node - `VaultGraphView.label_placements` orders the candidates by what the user is aiming at (crosshair, pointer, open note), then degree, then id, and `VaultGraphModel.cull_labels` drops every box that would print over one already accepted (or that the view clips anyway), so no two titles stack. Always-on labels still stop at `LABEL_ZOOM`/`MAX_ALWAYS_LABELS`, but past either threshold the aimed-at, pointed-at and open nodes keep their names instead of the map going nameless. `fit_to_view` reserves the widest label (`MAX_LABEL_BLOCK`) out of the usable box, so a fitted map's right-hand labels land inside the padding instead of running off the canvas. Node circles are deliberately not label obstacles: measured on the bundled vault, blocking against them deleted nearly every label in the crowded centre. Tests: `test_vault_graph_model.gd` (disjoint boxes kept, an overlapping one dropped by priority, out-of-view skipped, no candidates), `test_vault_graph_view.gd` (an 8-node cluster keeps fewer labels than nodes and the open note's, no two placed boxes intersect, the fit puts every label inside the view, and a 130-node graph names only the open note). Re-captured `wiki-graph` on the built app: 52 notes, labels legible, nothing stacked. Screenshot (under Screenshots below): *Vault link graph* - the 0.7.2 capture of the stacking this row closed. |
| ✅ | GUI | **The agent-state badge distinguishes failed from needs-attention by colour alone** - `failed` and `needs-attention` both rendered `Icons.WARNING_CIRCLE` and differed only by `BADGE_FAILED_COLOR` (red) and `BADGE_ATTENTION_COLOR` (amber), and the 12 px glyph `Label` carried no text, tooltip or accessible name, so a protan/deutan user could not reliably tell a failed turn from one that wants attention and a screen reader got nothing. Fixed 2026-11-04: `TerminalPane.BADGE_STATES` is the one table per state (`glyph`, `color`, `label`), `failed` is an X-circle and `needs-attention` the warning triangle (`Icons.X_CIRCLE` = U+E4F8, `Icons.WARNING` = U+E4E0 - both codepoints resolved by rendering the bundled Phosphor font, because it has no glyph names), the `label` is at once the hover tooltip and `accessibility_name` (the badge is `MOUSE_FILTER_PASS` because Godot only resolves a tooltip for the control under the pointer - measured on the headed build, a pointer motion over the badge makes `gui_get_hovered_control()` the badge and `get_tooltip()` return `Failed`; headless Godot tracks no pointer at all, even over a plain `Button`, so the suite asserts the hover target and the string rather than the popup; the title bar has no click or drag handler, so no gesture changed), and `accessibility_live` is polite so a change reaches a connected screen reader without focus; idle hides the badge and clears both strings. Tests: `test_every_state_has_its_own_glyph` (four states map to four distinct glyphs, each present in the bundled font - a wrong codepoint renders tofu that no colour difference rescues), `test_badge_carries_text_for_pointer_and_screen_reader` (a Tier 1 `needs-attention` state shows the triangle with tooltip and name; runs on Windows too, unlike the OSC path) and `test_failed_declaration_shows_the_x_circle`. The new `agent-badges` capture state stages all four at once (1280x420, four tiles) and repeats pixel-identically (`scripts/ui-capture-check --perturb agent-badges agent-badges`), which required pinning the badge animation: the spinner cycles at 8 fps and the attention pulse at 1.2 Hz, so the state pins `_badge_anim` to 0 and stops the pane's per-frame work after the poll paints it - later superseded by *The `working` badge is a thin arc that reads as a dim dot at 12 px*: the table moved to `AgentBadge.STATES` in `ui/agent_badge.gd` and the harness freezes the badge's own clock instead. The graph's other signals held: a ghost is a double ring plus an "N missing" count, and the open note is named in the status line. Screenshot (under Screenshots below): *Agent-state badges*. |
| ✅ | GUI | **Shared theme, type scale, colour tokens and UI scale** - closed 2026-11-04. `godot/scenes/ui/ui_tokens.gd` is the one token module (13 chrome colours, a 7-step type scale 11/12/13/14/16/18/36 with 12 the body size, a 7-step spacing scale, one corner radius) carrying `CONTRAST_PAIRS` and `contrast_ratio()`; `ui/ui_theme.gd` builds the one `Theme` from it (`Window`, `AcceptDialog` with `ConfirmationDialog` by class fallback, `GraphEdit`/`GraphNode`, tabs, popups, inputs, lists, scrollbars, tooltips, separators) and `Workspace._ready()` applies it on the scene root, which every pane, overlay, popup and dialog inherits - verified on 4.7.2: an `AcceptDialog` child resolves the themed panel, and `OptionButton`/`MenuButton` resolve `Button`'s items through the class chain. Migrated, not layered: `add_theme_*` overrides 163 -> 109, and the 54 gone are the ones that only restated a theme default; every surviving font-size and spacing call reads a token, so no numeric font-size literal and only two deliberate `0` separations remain, and `Color(...)` literals outside the token module 47 -> 0 (a lighter red is required under 4.5:1 on these backgrounds, so pure red is asserted to *fail* the table). Two measured contrast failures fixed: the status bar's right-hand text 4.15:1 -> 6.35:1 (`TEXT_MUTED`), Close All 3.49:1 -> 6.75:1 (`DANGER`). UI scale: `SettingsManager.cfg_ui_scale` (presets 100/110/125/150/175/200 %, clamped 100-200 on load) drives `get_window().content_scale_factor` - the mechanism that also scales hard-coded `custom_minimum_size` and margins, which a font multiplier cannot - applied on load and on every settings change, with a picker in the Appearance tab; the terminal's own font size stays a separate setting. Tests: `test_ui_tokens.gd` (the 16-pair table, the WCAG reference points, a deliberately failing pair), `test_ui_theme.gd` (opaque surfaces, the type scale, class fallbacks, the checkbox regression, the embedded title bar), `test_ui_scale.gd` (factor, window scale, loader clamp, the workspace applying both). Screenshot (under Screenshots below): *UI scale at 125 %* and *First launch on the design system*. |
| ✅ | GUI | **The app chrome has a light theme, and it follows the system** - closed 2026-11-05. `godot/scenes/ui/ui_palette.gd` is the colour owner: two complete sets (`DARK`, `LIGHT`, 18 tokens each) with the five terminal-facing entries equal in both, because they are drawn over the terminal's own scheme; `UiTokens` keeps the type scale, spacing, radius and `contrast_ratio()`. Every read site is `UiPalette.active()` at draw time (the old `const`s are gone, so a cached copy cannot compile), and 14 token-carrying `add_theme_color_override` sites became four `Theme` type variations (`MutedLabel`, `DangerLabel`, `WarningLabel`, `AccentButton`) plus a palette-read status-bar surface - the sidebar's `Color.WHITE` tint, which is invisible on a light sidebar, went with them. `SettingsManager.cfg_appearance_mode` (`Dark` / `Light` / `Follow system`, a picker at the top of Settings → Appearance) resolves through `system_is_dark`, a seam over `DisplayServer.is_dark_mode()`; `Follow system` registers `DisplayServer.set_system_theme_change_callback` (dispatched deferred, so the switch runs on the main thread) and a platform without the query answers dark. `appearance_changed` fires only when the answer moves; the workspace rebuilds the `Theme`, resets the clear colour, re-pushes the chrome colours and the terminals' overlay colours, and carries every uncustomised chrome colour to the new set (a value equal to either set's default has never been chosen, so it follows instead of pinning). Measured: `LIGHT.TEXT` on `LIGHT.SURFACE` is 16.70:1 and the tightest light pair is `WARNING` on `SURFACE_RAISED` at 4.80:1, so both sets pass every pair (dark is unchanged: 13.07:1 and 8.40:1). Capture states `light-workspace` and `light-settings` are committed for the v0.8.6 recapture. Tests: `test_ui_palette.gd` (set parity, declared-field reflection, both sets through every contrast pair), `test_settings_manager.gd` (round-trip, clamp, the seam, idempotence, carry-vs-override), `test_ui_theme.gd` (a light `Theme`, the role variations), `test_settings_panel.gd` (the picker, reset), `test_workspace_settings.gd` (a live switch moves the panel, clear colour, wrapper, sidebar and status bar). The terminal's own light/dark scheme pair stays with the v0.8.4 row, which consumes the same signal. |
| ✅ | GUI | **Settings and Pane Settings are opaque and readable** - closed 2026-11-04: `Panel`, `PanelContainer`, `PopupPanel`, `AcceptDialog` and the concept editor's `GraphEdit` take an opaque `UiTokens.SURFACE` (`#202128`) with a 1 px `BORDER`, so no pane content, tab or Add Pane grid shows through the Settings cards, the Pane Settings card or the graph canvas (the stock stylebox was `(0.1, 0.1, 0.1, 0.6)` - `1a1a1a99`, the row's 60 %), and `Tree`/`ItemList` take an opaque content background. The panel's `Attachment ID` field gained the sentence that was missing ("The id IPC, MCP and agent commands address this pane by"), and every label and hint in both panels is on the token type scale. Verified by `test_ui_theme.gd` (`bg_color.a == 1.0` for each type, `== UiTokens.SURFACE` for the chrome ones) Screenshots (under Screenshots below): *Settings, opaque* and *Pane Settings, opaque and explained*. |
| ✅ | GUI | **Dialogs and profile forms match the app** - closed 2026-11-04: one builder, `godot/scenes/ui/app_dialog.gd` (`AppDialog.confirm`/`alert`/`set_body`/`body_of`/`group_buttons`), constructs and parents all 19 `ConfirmationDialog`/`AcceptDialog` sites; the four `FileDialog`s inherit the same theme. Buttons group at the right edge: `about_to_popup` collapses Godot's per-button `SIZE_EXPAND_FILL` spacers except the leading one - Restore to Cancel measured ~290 px apart in a 1046 px dialog before, 8 px (the row's own separation) after. Text the user must read and may need to copy moved to a read-only `TextEdit` body (workspace cwd trust, restore trust, profile trust, plugin review, the open-file and open-external-link confirms): the pinned revision and the spawn plan are selectable, and a path containing `[b]` cannot be read as markup. Save Profile: each command field gets a 220 px minimum (was 69 px against `/bin/bash`'s 75), the command scroll area 300 -> 340 px, the name field 320 px, so the path no longer clips to `/bin/ba`; the dialog follows its content's minimum. Tests: `test_app_dialog.gd` (grouping geometry, the leading spacer, a button added after construction, the selectable body, the fallback accessor); `test_workspace_cwd_trust.gd` and `test_pane_file_sinks.gd` read a dialog's text through `AppDialog.body_of`, same assertions. Screenshots (under Screenshots below): *Save Profile* and *Workspace Trust*. |

It is visible exactly when the active workspace has no tiles (`_apply_layout` is the one sync point every create and kill path already reaches) and leaves on the next spawn; the sidebar's `Panes:` heading hides with its list. | ✅ | GUI | **A UI scale above 100 % can push the layout under its own minimum** - reproduced 2026-11-05 on the development build by adding the `ui-scale-125-800x500` capture state: an 800×500 window at a 125 % request lays the interface out in `800 / 1.25` = 640×400, under the Settings card's 540×560. Fixed by capping the applied scale, not by reflowing: `UiTokens.MIN_LAYOUT` (540×560, the same constant the card sizes itself to) and `UiTokens.ui_scale_ceiling()` (the largest whole percent whose layout fits; -1 when the window cannot be measured), applied through `SettingsManager.capped_ui_scale_percent()` / `applied_ui_scale_percent()`. `apply_ui_scale()` writes the applied factor - guarded by `is_equal_approx` against the `size_changed` its own assignment emits - and `_on_window_resized()` re-applies it, so dragging the window smaller steps the scale down and growing it back restores the request; `cfg_ui_scale` and `ui_scale_factor()` keep meaning "what the user asked for". The floor is 100 %: a window already under 540×560 keeps the clipped 100 % layout instead of a sub-100 % interface, which is the v0.8.2 small-window row's work. The Appearance tab's scale row appends `shown at N %` (the tooltip carries the 540×560 reason) while the picker keeps the request. Verified: `test_ui_scale.gd` (ceiling table 89 / 192 / 135 / -1, cap table, applied factor at 800×500, the resize hook both directions) and `test_settings_panel.gd` (hint text, tooltip, `MUTED_LABEL` variation, hover target); the capture logs read `window=1920x1080 ui_scale requested=125% applied=125%` and `window=800x500 ui_scale requested=125% applied=100%`, both states pass `scripts/ui-capture-check` (pixel-identical reruns, perturb local), and the constrained PNG is 800×500 at the 100 % layout (180 px sidebar, one full tile). |
| ✅ | TERM | **Screen-reader accessibility is a product surface, not a flag** - closed 2026-11-05. One owner, `godot/scenes/ui/accessibility.gd` (`A11y`): `enabled()` is true when an accessibility driver is loaded or a reader reports itself attached (the export's `AccessibilityServer.is_supported()` is true, the editor build's is false, and `accessibility_screen_reader_active()` reads 0 on this desktop even with `org.a11y.Status.IsEnabled` set), `set_text` records the visible text and pushes it as the element value, `publish(host, slot, text, live)` replaces a hidden text node, and `announce_host` returns the tile's title bar. Names are enforced at the choke point: `Icons.icon_button(btn, name)` requires the words (a `Button` is named from its text before its tooltip, and that text is a PUA codepoint), `PaneBody` names every pane (`"T1: Terminal"`, or the user's name for it), and the window controls, sidebar, settings, pane-settings and concept-editor glyphs all carry explicit names. Measured limit, left in place on purpose: the engine composes the exposed name as `"<accessibility_name>: <button text>"`, so a glyph-only button still reads its codepoint after the words - moving the glyph out of `Button.text` into a child `Label` is **Pane chrome: one icon-button treatment, signed off by the owner**'s work, not this row's. The terminal publishes its visible grid (`visible_text()` from the damage-tracked cell cache, scroll offset included) and, while unfocused, its last settled output line at most once a second; the vault graph names itself, describes its shape and announces the crosshair's node on keyboard pans; media names its video surface and seek slider; the concept editor names the graph, its rules and its nodes. Verified: `godot/tests/unit/test_accessibility.gd` (59 asserts), `scripts/ui-capture --audit-accessibility first-launch` (glyph-buttons=16, unnamed=0, every name printed) and `scripts/accessibility-probe` against a rebuilt export, which asserts the exposed AT-SPI tree - application `gpty`, window `gPTY`, `T1: Terminal`, `Close All`, `Settings`, `New workspace (max 8)` and terminal text carrying a marker injected through the control socket. Adapter facts the design is built around (4.7.2, measured 2026-11-05): names arrive as the `Name` property, not `GetName`; a `PaneBody` is a leaf, so published text lives on the bar; a name is a snapshot, so dynamic text is a fresh node per change. Element values and descriptions do not reach AT-SPI from GDScript - queued as **Element values do not reach the AT-SPI adapter**. No `accessibility/*` project setting and no launch flag: the engine's `AUTO` remains the policy. |
| ✅ | TERM | **Element values do not reach the AT-SPI adapter** - closed 2026-11-06 with the answer this engine can have. 4.7.2 is the newest release, so there is nothing newer to re-test on: the measurement stands (value and role writes never reach the node, a node's name is the creation snapshot, and the adapter serves `Name` as a property with no role method and no `Text`/`Value` interface on a custom `Control`), and the app-side cost is removed - `A11y.publish` now returns an unchanged slot as it is, so `TerminalPane._a11y_tick`'s once-a-second republish of the same line no longer takes the node a reader is on (`test_publish_leaves_an_unchanged_slot_alone`; a changed live mode still replaces it, `test_publish_replaces_when_the_live_mode_changes`). `scripts/accessibility-probe` prints a report-only `a11y-values:` line (`text-interfaces`, `value-interfaces`, `marker-in-value`, `role-names`), which grades any later engine in one command; the engine-gated half is `docs/content/docs/roadmap/future.md` *Re-test the AT-SPI element-value mapping when an engine release carries it*. |
| ✅ | GUI | **Window controls never swap maximize/restore** - fixed 2026-11-06: the glyph is the shared icon-button treatment (`Icons.icon_button(btn, glyph, name)`, the codepoint in a child `Label`), so `WindowChrome` keeps its maximize control in `_max_btn` and `_sync_max_button()` reads `cfg_window_mode` instead of comparing a `Button.text` that is now always empty - fullscreen shows `Icons.RESTORE_WIN` and "Restore window", every other mode `Icons.MAXIMIZE_WIN` and "Maximize window", with the name moving first so `Icons.set_glyph` mirrors it onto the Label (a node's exposed name is still a creation snapshot, see **Element values do not reach the AT-SPI adapter**). It is driven from `apply_mode()`, so `toggle_fullscreen`, `on_window_mode_selected` and the restore path all reach it; `test_window_chrome.gd` asserts the glyph and the name in modes 0/1/2, one assertion through `apply_mode()`, and that all three controls draw their codepoint in a Label with `text == ""` while keeping the bar's 36 px target. |
| ✅ | GUI | **Global shortcuts stay live while an overlay owns the keyboard** - fixed 2026-11-05: the registry split lives in `ShortcutManager` - `register(..., survives_overlay)` and one injected predicate, `ShortcutManager.overlay_owns_keyboard`, which `Workspace` sets from `_keyboard_overlay()` and resets to `false` in `_exit_tree()` - so the decision is made once instead of by a guard in each callback. Exactly four window actions survive: `app:toggle_palette`, `app:toggle_sidebar`, `app:toggle_fullscreen` and `app:toggle_fullscreen_alt`. Every other chord (the per-type spawns, close, pane-chrome and note-toolbar focus, reset, next/previous workspace, `FocusManager`'s `focus:*`) is refused and the event consumed with `set_input_as_handled()`, so it neither spawns behind the panel nor reaches the terminal (`terminal_pane.gd` already drops a registered chord before sending bytes). Escape is unchanged - `Workspace._input` owns it before GUI. Tests: `test_overlay_focus.gd` shows `Ctrl+Shift+N` while Settings is open spawns nothing and leaves the panel focused, and `Ctrl+Shift+P` still opens the palette over it. |
| ✅ | BUILD | **The capture harness can hang forever with a blank, uncloseable window** - found 2026-11-04 by the operator while `scripts/ui-capture-check` ran: one launch (`window-800x500`, the sixteenth of the run) stalled during engine start-up - its log ends after the Vulkan device line, before any project code runs - and left a blank 800x500 window whose event loop never ran, so the window ignored its close request and the check had made no progress for 23 minutes. It does not reproduce (the same state captures in 3 s, and nothing in the project is executing at that point), so the stall is an engine/display start-up one; what was the harness's own defect is that nothing bounded it. `scripts/ui-capture` now bounds each launch (`CAPTURE_TIMEOUT`, default 120 s) with TERM then KILL, prints `produced no result in Ns - killing the launch`, and exits non-zero, which `ui-capture-check` already reports as a failed state. Verified: a normal capture writes its PNG in about 3 s, `CAPTURE_TIMEOUT=1` kills a launch and exits 1 with no process left behind, and the bound covers both capture and `--audit-focus` launches (`--hold` stays unbounded on purpose: it waits for the desktop grab). |
| ✅ | BUILD | **The focus audit stops inside the first keyboard ring** - closed 2026-11-06: `KeyboardFocus.tab_components(controls)` maps the whole traversal - every control not yet claimed is walked through `find_next_valid_focus()`, each walk stopping at null, at a control another component claimed, or when it returns to its own first member (`closed`, i.e. an explicit ring) - and `_audit_focus` prints the first component as `tab-order` and each later one as `ring[n]`/`flow[n]`, then `components=/rings=/reached=`. Measured on `wiki-vault` with this build: 3 components, 1 closed ring, 83 of 83 focusable controls reached (the old walk printed 5), with the pane-chrome ring and the wiki note toolbar's flow named separately. `test_keyboard_focus.gd` pins two rings as two closed components and a second start inside a ring adding nothing. |

Measured on 4.7.2: that column is not rebuilt by `file_mode`, `access`, a filter add or a re-popup, and `min_size.y` grows by the header's height so the strip sits above the path bar instead of over it. All four sites migrated (workspace directory, terminal font and scheme, Settings font); `test_app_dialog.gd` pins the shape, the close's cancel path and one header across a popup. Escape re-checked: `scripts/ui-capture --audit-focus file-dialog` reports `dialog=Working directory for Workspace 1`, a picker control as its focus owner and `escape overlay=… closed=true`. Screenshot (under Screenshots below): *File picker frame*. |
| ✅ | GUI | **Pane chrome: one icon-button treatment, signed off by the owner** - closed 2026-11-06 with the owner's choice (2026-11-05): the glyph alone at rest on `StyleBoxEmpty`, an `ACCENT`-tinted hover/pressed, the 1 px accent focus ring, one `UiTokens.BUTTON_MIN_WIDTH`/`BUTTON_MIN_HEIGHT` target shared with the sidebar, and text buttons keeping the theme's filled base. It is one mechanism rather than a sweep with per-site choices: `Icons.icon_button(btn, glyph, name, variation)` applies `UiTheme.ICON_BUTTON`, floors the width and draws the codepoint in a child `Label` (`Icons.glyph`/`Icons.set_glyph` read and swap it), which also stops the engine composing `"<name>: <codepoint>"` into the exposed name; all 21 call sites moved, `WindowChrome._make_titlebar_button`'s hand-built Label was deleted in its favour, and the sidebar's set-directory glyph takes `UiTheme.ACCENT_LABEL` (a Button's own font colours cannot reach the child). `test_icons.gd`, `test_ui_theme.gd` (the variation's five styleboxes plus `ACCENT_LABEL`) and the accessibility audit (`glyph-buttons=46 unnamed=0` on the sidebar state) pin it. Screenshot (under Screenshots below): *Icon buttons*. |
| ✅ | BUILD | **The colour-override census is a gate, not a convention** - closed 2026-11-05: `scripts/check-theme-tokens` (python3, the `check-log-hygiene` mould: docstring, exit 0/1, reads only) scans `godot/scenes/**/*.gd` and fails a line that calls `add_theme_color_override` or reads `UiPalette.active()` at class-body level - field and `const` initializers, `@export` defaults, inner-class fields, and a class-level lambda body, decided by an indentation stack that a `func` opener interrupts, so an in-function read (the correct per-call read) passes. `# theme-ok: <why>` on the line or within the three above is the reviewed exception, the way `// log-ok:` works. `scripts/ci-check` runs it as step 3.3 in the fast suite, and `docs/content/docs/agents/gdscript.md` names it next to the grep census. The check starts clean because the seven `settings_manager.gd` chrome field initializers and the seven `load_settings` fallbacks now route through `static func _chrome_default(key)`, which reads the existing `CHROME_COLOR_TOKENS` table, so that table is the one place a setting's default token is written. No allowlist was needed for the two sources the row anticipated: the palette's own tables are built inside functions and the contrast test lives under `godot/tests`, so the `godot/scenes` scan is clean as it stands. Verified: `check-theme-tokens: no cached palette colours in 57 GDScript files`, and a fixture self-test reports the override, the indent-0 field read, an inner-class field and a class-level lambda body while a `# theme-ok:` line and an in-function read report nothing. The gate runs only in the local suite; closing that cloud gap is the v0.9.4 row **The static gates run only in the local pre-push suite, never in CI**. |

Six captures of the finished surfaces ship - `v0.8.0_1.png` (a fresh launch) through `v0.8.0_6.png` (the four agent badges) - every one taken by `scripts/screenshot-gpty` in its sandbox, and `docs/content/blog/v0.8.0/index.md` carries the narrative. |
| ✅ | BUILD | **The screenshot script hung on a capture backend that cannot grab, and `set -e` hid it** - found 2026-11-06 while capturing this release: `scripts/screenshot-gpty` preferred ffmpeg's `x11grab -window_id`, which on this XWayland session attaches to nothing and says nothing (measured: no output in 30 s where ImageMagick's `import` produced the frame immediately), and the call sat bare under `set -e`, so the `timeout` guard that was meant to bound it aborted the script instead of letting the fallback run - a full run ended after the window wait with exit 124 and empty stderr. Fixed 2026-11-06: the backends are a list tried under `CAPTURE_TIMEOUT` (`SCREENSHOT_TIMEOUT`, default 20 s) inside an `if`, so a failing or timed-out backend hands over instead of aborting; `SCREENSHOT_CAPTURE=ffmpeg\|import` pins one; and the failure names every backend tried. Verified by capturing all six `v0.8.0_<n>.png` images on this machine (ffmpeg timed out, `import` captured). Known, not fixed: a hard kill of the script (`timeout`, Ctrl-C at the wrong moment) still leaves the sandboxed app running, because the `EXIT` trap does not run on `SIGTERM`; the leaked window was killed by hand after this release's captures, and the hang that forced those kills is gone. |

## v0.8.1 - Chrome, Palette & Discoverability

| Done | Area | Item |
|:---:|:---:|---|
| ✅ | CORE | **A damaged history store is a dead end: every pane warns on every launch and no scrollback is ever persisted again** - reported 2026-11-10 from a launch log: four `Could not open history store for pane <id>: file is not a database` warnings (`crates/gpty-gdext/src/lib.rs:334`, one per restored pane), while `user://history.db` was 56,938,496 bytes whose every 4096-byte page begins with a b-tree page type byte and which contains no SQLite header and no `CREATE TABLE` anywhere - `sqlite3 .recover` fails with the same `file is not a database`, so the schema page is gone and that file's scrollback is unrecoverable. Nothing in the process could have written it (every history write goes through rusqlite), which is exactly why the code has to survive it: `HistoryStore::open` treated `SQLITE_NOTADB` as a per-pane warning and moved on, so persistence was dead for every pane until the user deleted the file by hand. `open` now reads an unreadable store as a repairable condition: `NotADatabase`/`DatabaseCorrupt` moves the file and its `-wal`/`-shm` siblings to `history.db.corrupt` (siblings first, so a stale WAL cannot replay the damaged file's frames into the replacement), a fresh store takes the path, and the whole open runs under the write guard so the four panes restoring at startup cannot move the replacement each other just created - one warning, one quarantine copy, bounded by the fixed name. Any other code (`SQLITE_CANTOPEN`, busy, read-only) moves nothing, because the file is not what is broken. Verified live in a sandbox with the reported file planted: one warning, `history.db.corrupt` byte-identical at 56,938,496 bytes, a fresh `user_version=2` store, and output from two panes in it; tests `an_unreadable_store_is_moved_aside_and_replaced`, `recovery_moves_the_wal_siblings_with_the_store`, `a_store_that_cannot_be_opened_is_not_moved`, `concurrent_opens_of_a_damaged_store_replace_it_once`. |
| ✅ | CORE | **A damaged vault index is the same dead end, one store over** - found 2026-11-10 while fixing **A damaged history store is a dead end: every pane warns on every launch and no scrollback is ever persisted again**: `VaultStore::open` (`crates/gpty-core/src/vault.rs:1432`) had the shape `HistoryStore::open` had and none of the recovery - `Connection::open`, `busy_timeout`, a `user_version` read, the pragmas - and its six call sites in `crates/gpty-gdext/src/vault_api.rs` (`:125`, `:180`, `:233`, `:286`, `:365`, `:439`) each logged `vault index open failed (<db>): <e>` and answered `vault index unavailable: file is not a database`, so a damaged `<state>/vaults.db` left the Wiki pane's note list, search, link graph and backlinks dead until the user deleted the file by hand. The database is a *cache of the vault* (`VaultStore`'s own doc says so, and `index_vault` re-reads every note into it), so recovery loses nothing. Fixed: `VaultStore::open` now routes through `gpty_core::store::open_with_recovery`, the one helper the scrollback store uses - the process-wide write guard for the whole open, `NotADatabase`/`DatabaseCorrupt` read as repairable, `<path>` and its `-wal`/`-shm` siblings moved to `<path>.corrupt` (siblings first, one fixed name) and the caller's open retried, every other code left alone; `store.rs` also owns `write_guard` and `restrict_to_owner`, and `history.rs`/`vault.rs` import them (no re-exports). A recovered index is empty by construction and the next `index_vault` pass rebuilds it. Tests: `a_damaged_index_is_moved_aside_and_rebuilt_from_the_vault` (two-note vault indexed, store overwritten with `not a database`, reopen leaves a byte-identical `vaults.db.corrupt`, reindex restores 2 notes, a live search hit and the `[[b]]` backlink), `recovery_moves_the_wal_siblings_with_the_store` (now in `store.rs`), and the history store's three recovery tests unchanged. Live: `scripts/smoke_pane_api.py`'s vault section overwrites `state_dir()/vaults.db` and requires the next `gpty vault search` to answer the note and `vaults.db.corrupt` to hold those bytes - recorded as `FAIL [vault]: ... 'error: rpc error -32603: vault index unavailable: file is not a database'` before the fix and PASS after. All six `vault_api.rs` sites and `vault_ipc.rs:134`'s `open_store` inherit it with no call-site edits. |
| ✅ | GUI | **Pane chrome: distinct icons, a compact action menu, padding and consistent empty states** - closed 2026-11-08: the title bar is three controls - **Pane settings**, **More actions**, **Close** - so the type list no longer costs a button; the bar reserves 74 px, was 122. `TerminalManager.show_pane_actions_popup` is the one menu: minimize/restore (the label follows the pane's state), swap position (disabled while one tile has nothing to swap with), and the eight types as radio-check items with the current one marked; the sidebar's pane row opens the same builder, so the two surfaces cannot drift, and choosing the current type is a no-op rather than a same-type rebuild (`swap_pane` does allow one, which a test pins). The overflow glyph is U+E204 `dots-three-outline`, resolved by rasterising the bundled font - `godot/assets/fonts/Phosphor-Regular.ttf` has unnamed `uniXXXX` glyphs at 1024 units/em, and the solid `dots-three` U+E1FE is 96 units tall, three ~1.5 px specks at 16 px - and `Icons.RESET` now means only "reset a value to its default". `PaneBody.CONTENT_INSET` (6 px, via `_fill_content`) insets the five text panes (Inspector, Reasoning, Code Viewer, CLI View, Wiki); the terminal grid, the media surface and the File Tree list keep their own bounds. `PaneBody.empty_state` is the one "nothing to show" surface: Media migrated onto it, and the Code Viewer gained **"No file open"** with "Open a file from the File Tree, or set a path in pane settings." where it used to be a disabled Reload over a blank editor (a routed capture still counts as content, so it is not empty). Three claims in this row were already closed and are recorded here instead of re-fixed: the CLI View copy has used a comma since 0.7.2, Close All has carried `Icons.DELETE` since the UX-02 fix, and the button floors are 22x18 in a 26 px bar. Tests: `test_pane_actions_menu.gd` (type marking, item order, the no-op current type, a real swap, minimize/restore), `test_code_viewer_empty_state.gd` (the empty state, a file replacing it, a capture without a file, the four insets), `test_title_bar_keyboard.gd` and `test_sidebar.gd` updated to three controls. Capture state `pane-empty-states` in `godot/tests/bench/ui_capture.gd`. Screenshots (under Screenshots below): *Pane padding and empty states*. |
| ✅ | GUI | **The File Tree shows an empty list when its root cannot be listed, and says nothing** - fixed 2026-11-08: the pane now renders its shared `PaneBody.empty_state` surface (the type's own `/>` glyph) instead of an empty `Tree` whenever the root cannot be listed, one sentence per state - **No folder open** (an empty `root_path`, or one that is not an absolute path; the message repeats the value), **Not a folder** (the path names a file), **Folder not found** (nothing at that path on this machine), **Cannot list this folder** (the path exists but the open fails) - and a folder that really is empty keeps its own sentence, which also says hidden entries are not listed. Every hint names the resolved path and where the fix is, because a profile or a saved layout can carry a path this machine does not have. One gate, `open_for_listing(path, opener) -> {dir, problem}`, returns `dir` null exactly when `problem` explains why, and the five `*_problem(path)` builders are the same functions the test reads; `open_dir` is the test seam (a Callable the test replaces), so the open-failure state is reached without depending on the suite's privileges. A subfolder that cannot be listed on first expand toasts the same title and hint instead of staying silently blank; an empty subfolder still just has nothing to expand. `test_file_tree.gd` pins all five messages, the rows-visible state, and the recovery path (`apply_settings` with a fixed root swaps the sentence for the rows). Screenshot (under Screenshots below): *File Tree, missing root*. |
| ✅ | TERM | **A fresh pane's child starts at 80×24 and only learns its real size 50 ms later** - fixed 2026-11-08: a pane spawns its child on its first layout pass, at the size the pane is drawn at. `TerminalPane._start_child_if_ready` is the only `start_shell` call site and takes the fitted grid from `_grid_for_size(size)` - one helper, the same `(size - PADDING) / cell` threshold the resize debounce now uses, so a child is born at the size the debounce would settle on and the debounce never resizes a child by its own creation. `crates/gpty-core/src/pty.rs` opens the pty at that size (`openpty(PtySize { rows, cols })`; it used portable-pty's 24×80 and resized afterwards, which is why the child's own first read was 80×24 whatever the grid said). `Workspace._apply_layout` tells each tile's pane it holds the rect it is drawn at (`PaneBody.mark_tile_laid_out` via `TerminalManager.mark_tile_laid_out`), so a pane the grid has never positioned spawns nothing and a tile too small to hold the minimum grid (8×3) still gets a shell at its configured `rows`/`cols`; `_process` covers the tile whose size never changes, one frame after the mark. Input that reaches a pane before its child exists is buffered (`_pending_input`, 64 KiB - `inject`/`broadcast` now go through the pane's own `_send_line_to_term` instead of writing to a terminal with no session) and flushed at start. A terminal with no session answers the documented nine-key `paneStatus` document (the new `gpty_core::engine::unstarted_status_json`, one builder shared with `status_json`) instead of `{}`, because every pane now reports one frame without a session. Measured: pre-fix the test's `bash -c 'stty size'` printed `24 80` where its pane was drawn at `43 59`, post-fix it prints `43 59`; in the app, a startup pane at 1738×1030 answers `60 205` and a split pane at 868×1030 answers `60 102`, unchanged three seconds later, and the capture below (1280×760) shows the child's own `41 rows, 129 cols`. Tests: `test_pane_spawn_size.gd` (born at the fitted size through a real `stty size`; input in the spawn frame survives; a never-positioned pane has no child; a sized pane starts at its own size), `test_pane_settings.gd::test_apply_settings_rows_cols_never_resizes_a_live_grid`, `pty::tests::the_childs_first_view_of_its_size_is_the_requested_size`, `engine::tests::the_unstarted_status_document_has_the_keys_a_live_one_has`. Two behaviour changes: a pane in a workspace that has never been shown starts its child when the workspace is first shown, and a stored or live `rows`/`cols` no longer resizes a grid (the fields are the pre-layout fallback; the pixel fit owns the size). Screenshot (under Screenshots below): *Pane spawn size*. |
| ✅ | GUI | **Every scrollable surface has a 0 px scrollbar** - closed 2026-11-08: the affordance was never styled - `ScrollBar/scroll` and `scroll_focus` were `StyleBoxEmpty` and the grabber boxes were margin-less `StyleBoxFlat`s, and Godot 4.7.2 takes a bar's thickness from the track stylebox's content margins plus `VScrollBar.padding_left/right` and `HScrollBar.padding_top/bottom` (`ScrollBar::get_minimum_size`; the grabber's own margins only set the thumb's minimum length), so every `ScrollContainer` reserved a 0 px column with nothing drawn and nothing to drag - a wheel was the only way to learn that a tab continues. `UiTokens` now owns the geometry (`SCROLLBAR_THICKNESS` 8, `SCROLLBAR_INSET` 1, `SCROLLBAR_MIN_THUMB` 24) and `UiTheme.build` derives the rest from it: a `SURFACE_RAISED` track whose content margins are the 6 px thumb width, an accent-bordered `scroll_focus`, a `TEXT_MUTED` 0.75 thumb inset 1 px inside the track with a 24 px minimum length (3.57:1 against the track in dark, 3.34:1 in light - above WCAG 1.4.11's 3:1 for a UI control), and `ACCENT_HOVER`/`ACCENT` for highlight and pressed. Measured 2026-11-08 on the fix build: the bar's own minimum is (8, 30) vertically and 8 px thick horizontally; Settings → Terminal's bar is (8, 441) where it was (0, 441), page 441 over max 503 - the audit's 62 px overflow - so its thumb is 384 px of the 441 px track (87 %), and the card stays `UiTokens.MIN_LAYOUT` (540×560). Tests: `test_a_scrollbar_reserves_a_column`, `test_the_scrollbar_track_and_thumb_are_visible_controls`, `test_an_overflowing_scroll_container_gets_a_real_bar` (`test_ui_theme.gd`) and `test_the_terminal_tab_shows_a_scrollbar_column` (`test_settings_panel.gd`); the `settings-terminal` and `settings-terminal-150` capture states cover the 100 % and 150 % views. Staging the 150 % view forced a harness fix: `Input.parse_input_event` takes window coordinates while a Control's rect is in layout space, so a synthetic click under a content scale landed 2/3 of the way to its target and the panel never opened - the capture driver now maps a click through the root viewport's final transform (identity at 100 %, where every committed capture came out byte-identical before and after). Screenshot (under Screenshots below): *Settings → Terminal's scrollbar*. |
| ✅ | GUI | **Command palette: population, selection and honest entries** - closed 2026-11-08: opening `Ctrl+Shift+P` lists all 14 entries at once (one "new …" per `PaneTypes.ALL` entry, then the 6 actions; the `Save Command…` entry added with the saved-commands row makes it 15 today) in a 560x480 card centred on the workspace - (320,160) in a 1200x800 workspace, where the old 320x160 panel put its top-left corner on (600,400); at the 560x340 layout floor the card clamps to 528x308 instead of hanging outside its host. Every row shows words rather than the command id, the registry's own glyph (`>_ {} /> @ ? $_ [] \|>`) and the chord it answers, read from the binding registry (`ShortcutManager.chord(action)`) instead of a repeated literal: 6 of the 14 rows carry one (`Ctrl+Shift+N/D/T/O`, `Ctrl+Shift+W`, `Ctrl+Shift+R`). Up/Down move a `UiTheme.SELECTED_ROW` cursor - an accent wash plus a 2 px accent leading edge, so the row Enter would run is identifiable without colour alone - clamped at both ends, and Enter runs the cursor instead of the first match; rows are `FOCUS_NONE` on purpose, because the field is the palette's one keyboard surface and Tab must not park a user on a row that cannot also type the filter. The developer-only `spawn 16 terminals` command is deleted rather than hidden, with its only callers (`Workspace._spawn_bulk`, `TerminalManager.spawn_bulk`), and `save`/`load` are renamed `Save Workspaces`/`Reload Saved Workspaces` - the last two destructive rows (`Close All Panes`, `Reload Saved Workspaces`) wear `DANGER_LABEL`, because reloading destroys the open panes. A miss says `No matching command` instead of showing a blank card. The list is pure data now (`ui/palette_model.gd`: `entries()`, `commands()`, `filter()` with three stable tiers - label prefix, label substring, command/keyword substring - returning copies, so a caller cannot mutate the const), which is the extension point **Saved commands** below appends to. Tests: `test_palette_model.gd`, `test_shortcut_manager.gd` (the round trip `format_chord(_parse_bind(s)) == s`), `test_palette.gd` (populated on open, row name/glyph/chord, no developer command, arrows + Enter + clamp, the empty state, the card at 560x480 and 528x308) and `test_ui_theme.gd`'s `SELECTED_ROW` case; a live smoke drove `Input.parse_input_event` through the real main scene (Ctrl+Shift+P, typed `new `, Down twice, Enter spawned the selected file-tree pane, Escape closed it with no command). Audits: `--audit-accessibility palette` lists all 14 rows by name, `unnamed=0`; `--audit-focus palette` - the field is the focus owner, Escape closes the overlay, `components=3 rings=1 reached=31` (the rows are `FOCUS_NONE` by design and reached with the arrow cursor, not Tab; the count grew from 30 when the sidebar's Help button joined its ring), and the `palette` capture repeats pixel-identically (`ui-capture-check palette` PASS). Screenshot (under Screenshots below): *Command palette on open*. |
| ✅ | GUI | **Saved commands: insert or run a frequently used command from the palette** - closed 2026-11-09: `godot/scenes/autoloads/saved_command_store.gd` (`SavedCommandStore`, `user://commands.json`, `{commands: [{name, command, run}]}`) is the one place a saved command lives - at most 200 entries, a 64-character name and a 1024-character command, LF/CR/TAB folded to one space with every other control dropped (a Godot `String` cannot hold a NUL at all: measured, both a literal and `String.chr(0)` read back as U+FFFD), names compared case-insensitively with the first of a duplicate keeping its row on load, and `add`/`update`/`remove`/`move` the only writers, so a profile, layout, plugin or IPC call has no path in. The seventh Settings tab (**Commands**, between Concepts and About) is the authoring surface: an `ItemList` of `Name  (Insert\|Run)` rows with the command as tooltip, `Add…`/`Edit…` through an `AppDialog` that does **not** hide on OK (`dialog_hide_on_ok = false`, so a refusal keeps what was typed), `Delete`, `Up`/`Down`, an empty-state sentence, and no reset footer because a reset would delete the one thing the tab holds; `open_commands(add_now)` is the palette's entry. The palette lists them under one **Saved commands** heading after the fixed rows, in store order, each hinting `Insert` or `Run`, and `Save Command…` (after `Open Settings`) opens that tab with the form already up. Delivery is one function: `run` goes through `_send_line_to_term` (the line path carries Enter), an insert through `_send_to_term` (raw bytes), then `_snap_to_bottom` - only to the **active terminal pane**, and `run` is off on a new entry. `Workspace._saved_command_refusal()` returns one sentence for the three no-target cases (no pane, the active pane is not a terminal, it is minimized); the palette uses it as the refused rows' tooltip and accessible description (rows `disabled`, cursor steps over them, Enter does nothing) and the dispatcher toasts the same sentence, so a hint and the behaviour cannot disagree. The seventh tab needed room: measured on this tree the seven tabs want 520 px of the card's 500 px bar at the theme default (12 px text, 8 px tab padding), and Godot hides the overflow behind a dropdown (a hidden tab's `get_tab_rect` comes back zero-based, which is why the guard reads the rects as one contiguous row and sums them). `UiTheme` now sets `font_size` for `TabBar` **and** `TabContainer` at `FS_CAPTION` (11) - the container's item is the one the engine copies into the bar it creates, so the `TabBar` entry alone left the live bar at the theme default - with 6 px tab padding: 490 px of 500, all seven tabs on the bar. Tests: `test_saved_command_store.gd` (round trip, `run` default off, sanitizing, the 1024 cap, case-insensitive duplicate, the 200 cap, a junk/duplicate/oversize file, move/no-op bounds, copies handed out), `test_palette_model.gd` (saved rows after the fixed list, exact entry shape, heading, filtering by name), `test_palette.gd` (one heading, refused rows with the reason, a recording pane proving the exact bytes and the insert-vs-line split, a real shell proving an insert does not run and a run does - judged by a file the command would create, since a redrawing shell can echo a pending line more than once - a deleted name writing nothing, `save command` opening the tab and the form), `test_settings_panel.gd` (list text and tooltip, add with `run` off, duplicate keeping the dialog, edit in place, delete, reorder keeping the selection, button enablement, empty state, the seven tabs contiguous and inside the bar) and `test_ui_theme.gd` (both tab font items). GUI-local: no IPC method, CLI verb or MCP tool, no plugin-supplied entries, no variable expansion, nothing at spawn (startup commands stay the `launch` key). Docs: wiki `settings.md` and `keyboard-shortcuts.md`, the `AGENTS.md` autoload tree, and two capture states (`commands` for the tab, `palette-commands` for the section) for the release screenshot. |
| ✅ | GUI | **Discoverability: pane names, shortcuts and the window-mode control** - closed 2026-11-09: the Add Pane grid stays glyph-only and names itself three ways instead. Its header names the tile under the pointer (`Add a Terminal Pane`), falling back to the tile the keyboard cursor is on and reverting to `Add Pane:` when both leave; the same header carries the section's **Help** button (tooltip and accessible name `Help`, `Icons.QUESTION` U+E3E8), whose press opens an `AppDialog` table of **glyph / action / chord** for all eight pane types. The affordance is one generic module, `godot/scenes/ui/section_help.gd` (`SectionHelp.attach(header, host, title, intro, rows_of)`), so the workspace, profile and pane headers can adopt it without a second implementation; `host` is the sidebar's unclipped background rect, and the rows provider is called at press time because a chord only exists once the workspace has registered it. The window-mode dropdown reads `System Frame` / `Borderless` / `Fullscreen` (it was `OS` / `Windowed` / `Windowless`), the label is unchanged in `user://settings.json` because the stored value is the index, and both dropdowns (sidebar and Settings → System) carry `Window Mode` as their accessible name and `SettingsManager.WINDOW_MODE_HINT` as their tooltip: one sentence per mode saying what it draws. Settings → About gained a **Keyboard Shortcuts** table grouped Workspace, New Panes, Panes, Keyboard Focus and Terminal, rebuilt from `ShortcutManager.chord(action)` on every show (never literals), with the new palette row `Keyboard Shortcuts` landing on that tab through the `shortcuts` dispatch arm and `SettingsPanel.open_about()`. The bindings got one owner to make that possible: `ShortcutManager.declare(action, bind)` binds an action with no workspace callback, `term:search`/`term:copy`/`term:paste` are declared in `_ready()`, `TerminalPane` matches them with `InputEvent.is_action(…, true)` instead of keycode+modifier tests (so a declared chord can never become a workspace action and still costs the pane its key first), and the workspace's `register(...)` block moved above `_build_sidebar()` - it used to run after it, so every chord the sidebar printed was empty. The two hardcoded sidebar tooltips (`Ctrl+Shift+F`, `Ctrl+Shift+R`) are registry reads now. Tests: `test_shortcut_catalog.gd` and `test_section_help.gd` (new), `test_shortcut_manager.gd` (declared ≠ registered), `test_sidebar.gd` (heading hover/focus, Help table), `test_settings_panel.gd` (`open_about` tab, registry spelling, rebuild not append, caption/rows structure), `test_keyboard.gd` (each `term:*` event derived from `ShortcutManager._parse_bind(chord(action))` still copies/pastes/searches), and `test_discoverability.gd` (a real workspace: sidebar tooltips, Help chords, both window-mode dropdowns, the palette row's About list). Screenshots (under Screenshots below): *Add Pane, named*, *Add Pane help* and *Keyboard shortcuts*. |

Its right-hand end reads `n/m` (`SearchCount`: `0/m` before the first jump, `Invalid pattern` in `DANGER_LABEL` with the engine's own sentence in the tooltip - the old `_search_error` was set and never shown), and `SearchPrev`/`SearchNext` are `Icons.COLLAPSE`/`Icons.EXPAND` icon buttons (`FOCUS_NONE`, tooltips naming the keys) that hand focus back to the field; Shift+Enter steps back while Enter stays the LineEdit's submit. `_refresh_search_count` is the one derivation, called from both search paths, both jumps, the scope toggle and the strip's open/close, and the History scope's results panel hangs below the strip on the same surface. A toast is now a `PanelContainer` per message, not a bare `Label`: `TOAST_PANEL`/`TOAST_WARN_PANEL`/`TOAST_ERROR_PANEL` give it an opaque `SURFACE_RAISED` card with 6 px padding, and the level is a 3 px leading edge as well as the label's ink, so severity never rests on colour alone; one 0.2 s fade-in replaces the pair that flashed alpha 1 for a frame, the text autowraps, and the message is both the accessible name and `accessibility_live = LIVE_POLITE`. `ToastManager.info("%s spawned")`, `"Pane closed"` and `"Swapped to …"` are deleted (with the dead `_add_body_to_grid` wrapper), and the rule - a toast reports a result the user cannot otherwise see - is stated in `toast_manager.gd`'s header; the refusals, clipboard copy, restore warnings and profile/workspace messages stay. `godot/scenes/ui/color_swatch.gd` (`ColorSwatch.describe`/`label_text`/`tooltip`/`attach`/`sync`) is the one owner of the swatch reading: Settings → Appearance now says `Focus (30%)` and `Selection (40%)`, Pane Settings says `Cursor color (70%)` and `(unset)` for a fully transparent colour a layout or profile carried in, and the tooltip names the checkerboard instead of leaving it unexplained. | ✅ | GUI | **The history search results are mouse-only** - closed 2026-11-09: the History scope is keyboard-operable end to end. `_rebuild_history_panel` builds each hit as a `FOCUS_ALL` toggle Button with the palette's `SELECTED_ROW` treatment and an index-bound `pressed`, so `_history_cursor` names the same row whether the keyboard walked to it, Tab focused it or it was clicked; `Up`/`Down` from the field move that cursor (`KeyboardFocus.next_index`, clamped) and are consumed by the strip - they used to reach `_handle_keyboard`, which sent an arrow sequence to the child and snapped the view to the bottom; `Enter` in the History scope copies the cursor's line through the one `_on_history_result_clicked(index)` path, so the toast always names the row the cursor shows; `Escape` closes and returns the keyboard to the pane. The scope toggle is a tab stop again and `_on_scope_toggled` hands the field the keyboard back, so the scope needs no pointer; the two step buttons stay `FOCUS_NONE` and now declare their keyboard path (`KeyboardFocus.declare_mouse_only`). `_clear_history_list` removes children before freeing them, so a same-frame cursor move cannot count a row on its way out. Tests: five in `test_history_search.gd` driven through `Input.parse_input_event` (rows are tab stops, the arrows move the cursor and stop at the ends, Enter copies with a toast, Escape closes and restores focus, a focused row walks focus, and the arrows never reach the child - pinned by the view offset, whose sentinel first waits for the child to stop printing, because a scrolled-back pane anchors its view to late output; `windows-smoke` measured the offset doubling from 44 to 88 when a line landed between the scroll and the key), plus the new `search-history` capture state. Measured: `--audit-focus search` prints `focus-none-buttons=2 declared=2 unaccounted=0`; `--audit-focus search-history` prints `focus-none-buttons=0 unaccounted=0` - the two step buttons are hidden in the History scope (`_refresh_search_count` shows them only for live matches), so the only buttons there are the focusable result rows: `focusable=36` against the Live scope's 33. |
| ✅ | GUI | **The sidebar offers a feedback path that opens a prefilled GitHub issue** - closed 2026-11-09: **Feedback** is the fourth footer action, directly below **Close All** (`sidebar.gd`, `_make_icon_text_button(Icons.FEEDBACK, "Feedback")`, U+E16C chat-circle-dots - a new glyph, because the plugin-catalogue hint already owns arrow-square-out), and it opens `https://github.com/godot-pty/gpty/issues/new?title=…&body=…` through an `open_external` Callable seam (`OS.shell_open` by default). The text is one pure module, `godot/scenes/ui/feedback_text.gd` (`FeedbackText`, statics, no scene tree): three prompts - what happened, what I expected, steps to reproduce - then an "About this build" block carrying `GptyTerminal.get_app_version()`, `OS.get_name()` + `OS.get_version()` and `SettingsManager.window_mode_label()` (the new getter that reads the same `WINDOW_MODE_LABELS` table the dropdown fills from), and nothing else - `body()` takes four strings, so no pane output, command, selection, path, environment value or log can reach the form. Every field is control-character-folded onto one line and capped at 96 characters, so an odd OS version cannot forge a block line; `MAX_URL = 8000` guards the URL (GitHub answers 414 past its limit) and a body that would exceed it is cut by bisection with the build block kept whole and `[truncated by gPTY]` appended. No `labels=`, `assignees=`, `template=` or `projects=`: the first two are dropped for users without write access, and `template=` would route into a YAML issue form, which ignores the generic `body=` parameter - and the repo's `bug_report.yml`/`feature_request.yml` do exist (they prefill only their own field ids), so this row's "no issue templates exist today" is corrected here instead of being kept. A failed `shell_open` (offline, headless, no handler) copies the same text through the `copy_text` seam and raises a WARN toast, so the report is one paste away and the app still submits nothing and holds no token. Tests: `godot/tests/unit/test_feedback_text.gd` (the exact template, placeholder fields, newline folding, URL parameter set, a 20 000-character field capped with the block intact, an over-cap trim with its marker) and three `test_sidebar.gd` cases (the row sits directly below Close All with its own glyph, the prefilled open asserted through the seam, a failed open → clipboard + WARN toast). `--audit-accessibility sidebar` reports `unnamed=0` with the fourth row, and the `window-chrome-borderless` capture shows it in place. |
| ✅ | GUI | **A failed external link is silent at the app's other three link sites** - closed 2026-11-10: one helper, `godot/scenes/ui/external_link.gd` (`ExternalLink`), is now the only path from an in-app URL to the OS. `open(url, fallback_text, message, source)` returns `OS.shell_open`'s `Error`; on anything but `OK` it copies the fallback text (the URL by default) and raises a WARN toast for 6 s whose sentence names the URL - "Couldn't open a browser - copied <url>" - with the URL shortened to 120 characters for the one-line toast only (`shown`), while the clipboard always gets it whole. `open_external`/`copy_text` are the replaceable seams, asked by `test_external_link.gd`'s four cases: a reachable link opens once and copies nothing, a refused one copies the exact URL and warns, a custom fallback/message/source replace the defaults (the Feedback row pastes a whole issue body, not the URL), and a 400-character URL stays whole on the clipboard and under 200 characters in the toast. The catalogue hint (`Sidebar._make_catalog_hint_row`), `MarkdownView._confirm_link`'s confirmed branch and the Settings About tab all go through it; the About line is now a flat `ACCENT_BUTTON` `Button` (`RepoUrl`, tooltip "Open the gPTY repository in your browser") instead of a `Label`, and the feedback row's own `open_external`/`copy_text` pair moved out of `Sidebar` into the helper, its wording and body unchanged. `test_sidebar.gd` injects `ERR_CANT_OPEN` and pins the catalogue row's fallback: the URL is copied and one WARN toast names it. The file tree's own file-opener seam was the last `OS.shell_open` site, closed by the row below. |

The pane's `open_external` seam is deleted; the helper's own `open_external`/`copy_text` are the one seam, so the in-app viewer/media route is asserted against the same recorder and still never reaches the helper or the clipboard. | ✅ | IPC | **`new-pane --command` answers with a pane whose child never starts** - fixed 2026-11-10. `command` on a terminal is a command line run through the configured shell — `shell_args = PaneTypes.shell_run_args(cfg_shell_command, command)` (`-c`, `/c` for `cmd.exe`), the shape `paneRun` and the spawn-size capture already use — so the pane's program is the user's shell and the line is its argument. Measured before and after in one sandbox: `-c "echo hi"` answered a `pane_id` with `pid: null` and `pane-read: ""` (the log said `Failed to spawn PTY for 'echo hi'`), while `-c /usr/bin/cat` had a live child; after the fix the same call answers a pane whose status reads `exit_code: 0, exit_reason: exited`, and `-c "echo A; echo B"` reads back `"A\nB"`. The success is no longer claimed before the spawn: a dispatch answer carrying a `pane_id` (so `newPane` and `paneRun` today, and a future pane-creating handler by shape) is held by `Workspace._hold_pane_answer` until the pane's own start reports — `TerminalPane.child_started`/`child_start_failed`, emitted one idle frame after the attempt and outside the container sort that caused it — with `Workspace._poll_pending_pane_starts` answering `Pane '<id>' never started` at 5 s for a pane closed before its first layout. A refusal answers the flat `{code, message}` error naming the program, raises the same sentence as a WARN toast, and closes the tile with `_kill_now`; `crates/gpty-gdext/src/lib.rs`'s `start_shell` now returns that sentence (`GString`, `""` = started) instead of only logging it. The seven `GptyTerminal.respond_ipc` call sites in `workspace.gd` fold into one `send_ipc_answer` Callable seam (the `ExternalLink.open_external` pattern), so the drain's deferred answers are testable with no socket. Docs corrected where they called the flag a program (`cli.md`, `control-api.md`, `ipc.md`, `foundations/shell.md`'s list of program sources, `SKILL.md`, the CLI's `--command` help): a caller can no longer name a pane's program at all — a profile or a saved tile is the only surface that owns one — and a *command line* that fails is the shell's business (the shell's own message stays in the pane, exactly like `pane-run`), while a **spawn** failure is the call's error. Tests: `test_new_pane_command_runs_a_compound_command_line` (held answer, `["-c", "echo NP_ONE; echo NP_TWO"]`, both markers in `paneRead`) and `test_a_failed_spawn_answers_an_error_and_leaves_no_pane` (a nonexistent `cfg_shell_command` answers one `false` naming the program, leaves the tile count unchanged and raises one toast) in `test_ipc_dispatch_contract.gd`, plus the `new-pane --command` step in `scripts/smoke_pane_api.py`. |
| ✅ | IPC | **`new-pane -t cli_view` answers success over a program that never starts** - fixed 2026-11-10.
