Reproduced first: `gpty new-pane -t cli_view -c /nonexistent/gpty-no-such-program` answered the pane document `{pane_id, label: V1, type: cli_view}` (a JSON-RPC success), `pane-read` `""` and `pane-status` answering `no status primitives` — the only trace was the pane's own `_status` label, which no API caller can read; after the fix the same call answers `-32000 cannot stat executable /nonexistent/gpty-no-such-program: No such file or directory (os error 2)` and `list-panes` holds no cli_view pane. `CliViewPane` now keeps `_cli.start`'s refusal in `_start_error` (cleared on a successful start) and exposes it through `start_error()`, and the `cli_view` arm of `newPane` checks it right after the spawn — the start is synchronous in `_ready`, so no held answer is needed — toasts that exact sentence and closes the tile with `_kill_now` instead of answering the pane document. The in-pane label stays as the surface for a *restart* that fails after the pane exists. Test: `test_new_pane_cli_view_refuses_a_program_that_cannot_start` (an error naming the program, tile count unchanged, one toast) in `test_ipc_dispatch_contract.gd`; `control-api.md` now says which arm of `command` is a shell line and which is a program. |
| ✅ | TERM | **A scrolled-back pane returns to live output in one click** - closed 2026-11-10: `TerminalPane` carries a floating chevron (`Button` named `ScrollToBottom`, 24x24, anchored to the pane's bottom-right at offsets -30..-6, `Icons.CARET_DOWN` U+E136) whose tooltip and accessible name read **Return to live output**. It is shown exactly while `get_scroll_offset() > 0` (`_sync_scroll_end_button`), and the sync sits on every path that moves the view rather than only in the frame sync: the wheel branch, PageUp/PageDown, `_snap_to_bottom` (typing, Enter, the click) and a search jump - the frame-sync call alone left the control up to a sync interval stale after a wheel (measured while staging the capture: offset 15, button still hidden four frames later). The click calls `_snap_to_bottom()`, then `_refresh_selection()`; the selection is grid-space, so the jump carries the highlight with its text and copy still works, and the existing `[Scroll: N/M lines]` readout stays the position. It is a real `Button` child, so Godot's picking delivers the click before `_handle_mouse` reaches the mouse-reporting branch: in a TUI that owns the mouse the chevron stays pane chrome rather than becoming a click report. It never takes focus or sends input - `FOCUS_NONE` with a declared reason ("PageDown, the wheel or typing return to live output"), which the focus audit counts as answered (`unaccounted=0`), and `Ctrl+End` parity can ride the configurable-keybindings item in v0.9.5. Tests: `test_scrollback_return.gd` - the chevron follows the offset through the frame sync (shown above 0, hidden at the bottom), a click resets the offset to 0 with the selection text unchanged, and a `ScrollProbe` pane reporting DECSET 1000 keeps `MOUSE_FILTER_STOP`/`FOCUS_NONE`, sends nothing (`sent == []`) and keeps its selection. Screenshot (under Screenshots below): *Scrollback return*. |
| ✅ | IPC | **The pane API's bad-parameter refusals answer two different codes** - fixed 2026-11-10. The split was real and no rule decided it: measured before the fix, 14 refusal rows answered `-32000` where the request's own value was the problem (both `paneWait` patterns, `Unknown pane type`, `Command required` and `Invalid command`, `Pane target required` from `paneRead`/`killPane`, the 64 KiB inject cap, `Invalid broadcast request`, `Profile name required`/`too long`, `Concept name required`, `Unknown plugin action`, `Malformed plugin id`) while `newPane`'s `focus`/`split`/`command` checks and a non-object `params` already answered `-32602`. The rule is now stated once, on two constructors: `WorkspaceIpcHandlers.bad_param` (`-32602` - a value the request named and the handler cannot accept) and `refused` (`-32000` - a well-formed request naming app state it cannot satisfy); `error(msg, code)` has no default code, so an arm cannot inherit a kind it never decided, and `_resolve_pane_target` returns its own `code` (`target_refusal` forwards it) because a missing target is a malformed request while an unknown or ambiguous one is app state. Every other arm keeps `-32000`, deliberately: not-found/ambiguous, a pane of the wrong kind (`is not a terminal`, `has no readable output`, `has no status primitives`), the pane ceiling, a `cli_view` that cannot start, an unsaved pane, an unapproved or unknown profile, and the deferred answers (`Pane gone`, `never started`, too many reviews). `test_ipc_dispatch_contract.gd::test_every_refusal_arm_answers_the_code_its_kind_owns` runs 26 rows (one spawned terminal and one `code_viewer`, so the two placeholder targets cost one pane each), `smoke_pane_api.py`'s `handler error shape` now proves both codes over the real socket (`paneRead {"pane_id": ""}` answers `-32602 Pane target required` beside the existing `-32000 Pane 'nosuchpane' not found`), and the contract is stated in `crates/gpty-ipc/README.md`, `security.md`, `pitfalls.md`, `wiki/gpty/ipc.md`, `wiki/gpty/control-api.md`, `wiki/gpty/cli.md` and the `gpty-public-surface` skill. |
| ✅ | IPC | **`conceptToggle` invents a concept for a name that does not exist** - found 2026-11-10 while applying the refusal-code rule to `ipc_handlers.gd`: the arm passed the caller's `name` straight to `ConceptManager.toggle_concept`, whose not-found branch appended `{name, enabled}` to the user store and saved it, so one IPC call per entry grew `user://concepts.json` without bound and `conceptList` reported the phantom rows. The settings panel could not reach that branch (it passes names it read from the merged set), so the IPC arm was the only caller with a name that may not exist. Fixed 2026-11-10: `toggle_concept` answers false, and writes nothing, when the merged set holds no rule by that name, and the arm answers `-32602 Unknown concept: <name>` (an unknown enum member, the rule this release established) with the toggle still owned by `ConceptManager`. Measured live in a sandbox before and after: before, `gpty concept toggle no-such-concept` answered `{"success": true, "name": "no-such-concept"}` and `concepts.json` held `{"concepts":[{"enabled":false,"name":"no-such-concept"}]}` with no trigger, listed by `concept list`; after, the same call answers `-32602` with `Unknown concept: no-such-concept`, `concept list` holds no such row, and `concepts.json` is untouched. Tests: `test_toggle_refuses_a_name_the_merged_set_does_not_hold` (the manager: false, no append, absent from the merged set) and `test_an_unknown_concept_toggle_is_refused_and_writes_nothing` (the arm and the store: `-32602`, the store unchanged, no phantom row in `conceptList`, a known name still toggles), plus the new row in `test_every_refusal_arm_answers_the_code_its_kind_owns`; `crates/gpty-ipc/README.md` and the `control-api` wiki page state the refusal. |
| ✅ | IPC | **`layoutSave` answers the name the caller asked for, not the one the store kept** - found 2026-11-10 in the same file: the arm answered `{"success": true, "name": profile_name}` while `ProfileManager.add_profile` de-duplicated through `_name_exists` and stored `name (2)`, so a script that saved twice under one name believed both answers addressed its profile and `layoutLoad name=x` silently loaded the first. Fixed 2026-11-10: `add_profile` returns the stored name (the same return `rename_profile` gives callers; `""` for a request that names nothing, which stores nothing) and the arm answers that, so the answer is the row `layoutList` reports and `layoutLoad` resolves; the sidebar's save toast uses the same return, so a deduped save says `Profile 'x (2)' saved`. Measured live in a sandbox before and after: before, two `gpty layout save dup` calls both answered `"name": "dup"` while `layout list` held `dup` and `dup (2)`; after, the second answers `"name": "dup (2)"` and `layout load "dup (2)"` succeeds. Tests: `test_add_profile_answers_the_name_the_store_kept` (unit: `Dev` / `Dev (2)` / `Dev (3)` / `""`) and `test_layout_save_answers_the_name_the_store_kept` (two saves in one run over an emptied workspace: the second answer differs, is listed by `layoutList`, and loads); both docs tables state the stored-name rule. |
| ✅ | GUI | **The `working` badge is a thin arc that reads as a dim dot at 12 px** - found 2026-11-06 while taking the release images: in the `agent-badges` state (`godot/tests/bench/ui_capture.gd`) `completed` (a filled check circle), `failed` (X-circle) and `needs-attention` (warning triangle) all read as solid silhouettes, while `working` is `Icons.SPINNER_GAP` (`terminal_pane.gd:708`) - one thin arc of a ring - so the fourth state is the one a reader has to hunt for, in a surface whose whole point is that the four are distinguishable. In `docs/static/images/v0.8.0_6.png` the arc's stroke is mostly partial coverage (`#344B62`-`#6186BE` against the `#2E2E33` title bar, 1.50:1 to 3.64:1) although `ACCENT` itself (`#7fb4ff`) is 6.35:1 on that surface, so the defect is stroke weight, not colour. Fixed 2026-11-10, both remedies at once: `working` is a filled silhouette *and* the badge is raised to the icon-button floor. The badge is now its own control, `ui/agent_badge.gd` (`AgentBadge`), which owns the state table, the tint role, the tooltip, the accessible name and its own animation clock; `working` is painted rather than drawn - a 1.75 px band on a 6 px radius with a 1.75 rad notch that turns once per 0.9 s, so the notch carries the motion and the band is the silhouette - while the other three states stay Phosphor glyphs, all four drawn at `UiTokens.FS_HEADING` (16 px) in the `BUTTON_MIN_WIDTH` x `BUTTON_MIN_HEIGHT` box. `TerminalPane` now only hands the badge a state (`_apply_badge` calls `AgentBadge.show_state`), and `BADGE_STATES`, `BADGE_SPINNER_FRAMES`, `_animate_badge`, `_badge_label` and the `SPINNER`/`SPINNER_GAP`/`CIRCLE_NOTCH` codepoints are deleted. Measured with the same projection on the same four tiles of the same `agent-badges` state (1280x420): `working` went from 11.7 to 58.3 ink px, and at full opacity the four read 58.3 / 48.2 / 51.4 / 48.4 (working / completed / failed / needs-attention) - a 21 % spread where the gap was 2.1-2.3x. The capture's `needs-attention` tile measures 26.6 because that state pulses and the harness freezes it at its 0.55 alpha frame (the pre-existing pulse; at full opacity it is 48.4). The 2 px band the mock used measured 67 px - bolder than its siblings rather than equal - which is why the shipped stroke is 1.75. In greyscale the four are a notched ring, a ring with a check, a ring with a cross and a triangle with an exclamation point. Tests: `test_the_working_ring_is_a_closed_band_with_one_notch`, `test_the_notch_advances_with_the_animation_phase`, `test_only_the_two_animated_states_tick` and `test_a_state_names_itself_for_the_pointer_and_the_reader` (new, `godot/tests/unit/test_agent_badge.gd`), plus `test_every_state_has_its_own_silhouette` and the state-swap assertions in `godot/tests/integration/test_agent_badges.gd`; the harness stops the badge's own clock, which `scripts/ui-capture-check agent-badges` proves by two pixel-identical runs. Screenshot (under Screenshots below): *Agent-state badges, silhouette weight*. |
| ✅ | IPC | **`pane-read --lines N` returns the screen's last N rows, so a small N is trailing blanks** - fixed 2026-11-10: `TermGrid::plain_text` (`crates/gpty-core/src/term.rs`) drops trailing empty rows before it applies `limit`, so the limit bounds the answer instead of spending itself on screen padding; leading and interior blanks stay (a blank row inside a program's output is content), the per-row right-trim and the byte-exact grid walk are unchanged, and a grid with no text answers no lines, which `get_plain_text` joins to `""`. `plain_text_drops_trailing_blank_rows_before_the_limit` pins it: a grid holding `one` returns `["one"]` at limits 1, 5 and 200, a program that prints `a`, a blank line, `b` keeps all three rows, and an untouched grid is empty. The FFI signature and `paneRead`'s GDScript call are unchanged, so a fresh pane now answers `pane-read --lines 5` with its prompt line instead of `"\n\n\n\n"`. |
| ✅ | GUI | **The focus audit cannot see an open overlay's own mouse-only controls** - fixed 2026-11-09: `_audit_focus` snapshots the open overlay's visible, enabled `FOCUS_NONE` buttons - text, tooltip, declared reason and path, all as strings - before the Escape that closes it, reports each one as `mouse-only button scope=overlay`, and only then closes and prints the escape result; the workspace's own are collected after the close and deduped by path, so a control a failed Escape left visible is not named twice. The summary splits the count: `focus-none-buttons=N declared=M unaccounted=U`. `KeyboardFocus.declare_mouse_only(control, reason)` + `mouse_only_reason` are the declaration, the palette's rows and the search strip's step buttons use it, and `docs/content/docs/agents/testing.md` states the contract. Measured: `--audit-focus palette` names all 15 rows (8 pane types and 7 actions) before the Escape and still reports `closed=true`, `declared=15 unaccounted=0` - the same state reported `focus-none-buttons=0` before, because it counted after closing, which is also why this row's original "13 rows" figure was stale. |
| ✅ | GUI | **The window's own titlebar controls are mouse-only** - fixed 2026-11-09: `WindowChrome._make_titlebar_button` no longer sets `FOCUS_NONE`, and `build()` joins the three controls into one explicit `KeyboardFocus.ring` after the bar enters the tree (`get_path_to` is empty before that, which is why the call sits there). `Ctrl+Shift+A` (`app:focus_window_chrome`, `Workspace._focus_window_chrome`) moves the keyboard in through `WindowChrome.focus_controls()`, which answers false unless the bar is on screen and has a stop - so the chord is inert in `System Frame` mode, where `apply_mode` leaves the bar hidden - and Escape goes one level out: `_escape_window_chrome` recognises a focus owner under `titlebar` and hands the keyboard back through `TerminalManager._return_focus_to_body`, matching the pane-chrome step and running after it in `Workspace._input`'s ladder. Discoverability: the chord is in `ShortcutCatalog`'s Workspace group and in the wiki's shortcut table. Evidence, all measured on this build: the new `window-chrome-borderless` capture state (`settings: {cfg_window_mode: 1}`, applied before the scene exists, so it is the real borderless window) takes the audit from `focus-none-buttons=3 unaccounted=3` to `focus-none-buttons=0 unaccounted=0` and makes the ring visible to it - `ring[1]=GlobalTitleBar/WinControls/Minimize, Maximize, Close`; the image shows the bar with its three glyphs. Tests: `godot/tests/integration/test_window_chrome_keyboard.gd` - the ring in both directions with no tree-order fallback, the chord entering at Minimize and Escape returning to the active pane, the chord inert in the decorated mode, and Escape outside the bar not swallowed (a terminal keeps the raw ESC byte). The row's own claims held: nothing else in it needed correcting. |
| ✅ | GUI | **The view panes' toolbars are mouse-only** - fixed 2026-11-09: the two toolbars are keyboard rings, entered by `Ctrl+Shift+L` (`app:focus_pane_toolbar`) and left by Escape. The contract moved onto the base class rather than being spelled per pane: `PaneBody._action_bar` (the pane's own row), `focus_toolbar()` (first reachable stop, false when the bar is hidden or empty), `toolbar_has_focus(owner)` (the scope test) and `leave_toolbar()` (one level out to `keyboard_entry()`, and never back into the bar), so a third view pane joins by assigning one field. `CodeViewerPane` rings Reload + view toggle from `_refresh_view` (a non-Markdown file hides the toggle, which is then a one-stop bar) and aims `keyboard_entry()` at the text; `CliViewPane` rings Restart + Stop from `_sync_buttons()` and aims at its output. `KeyboardFocus.ring` grew the one-member case: a single stop keeps `FOCUS_ALL` and gets no `"."` self-path, and it clears the paths a larger ring left behind - measured as a real defect, since `Tab` from a lone Restart still walked onto the just-exited run's disabled Stop. A disabled button also stops being engine-reachable at all (`focus_mode` follows `disabled` in `_sync_buttons`), because the engine's own traversal does not skip one. The Wiki pane's note toolbar joins the same ladder: `_action_bar` is its `_toolbar`, `_return_from_note_toolbar()` is deleted, its `_input` declines Escape when the owner is in the bar, and `Workspace._escape_pane_toolbar` performs the step - `Ctrl+Shift+K` and its preconditions are untouched. Correction folded in: this row said neither pane holds a text field - both hold a read-only `CodeEdit` (a focusable stop, and it keeps Tab for indentation), so the defect was the `FOCUS_NONE` flags, not a missing text field; the fix direction stood. Evidence, measured: the new `code-viewer-content` state (a Markdown fixture written into the sandbox, since `TextRead` refuses `res://`) and `cli-view-content` state (`/bin/sh -c "printf 'ready\n'"`, which exits at once so the capture is repeatable) take both audits to `focus-none-buttons=0 unaccounted=0` from `unaccounted=2` and `unaccounted=1` (the CLI state counted only Restart: Stop is disabled after the exit) and show the rings to the audit - `ring[1]=…/Toolbar/ReloadButton, ViewToggle` and `…/Header/RestartButton, StopButton`. Tests: `godot/tests/integration/test_view_toolbar_keyboard.gd` (both rings and their wrap, the chord entering each and Escape returning to the editor, the chord inert on an empty viewer, and the CLI ring following `disabled`), `test_keyboard_focus.gd::test_a_one_member_ring_is_a_stop_without_a_wrap` + `::test_shrinking_a_ring_clears_the_path_to_the_gone_member`, and the existing `test_wiki_note_keyboard.gd` passes unmodified. |
| ✅ | GUI | **The capture harness approves the plan the Workspace Trust state exists to have reviewed** - found and fixed 2026-11-09 while running the full `ui-capture-check` sweep for *Discoverability: pane names, shortcuts and the window-mode control*: `godot/tests/bench/ui_capture.gd` records every tile a state seeds as approved (`TrustedStore.approve_builtin("ui-capture", …)`) so a state that needs a non-default program for an unrelated reason (`pane-spawn-size` prints its own PTY size) does not trip the restore gate. That block landed 2026-11-08 with the spawn-size row (commit `01d5859`) and silenced a state that had existed since 2026-11-03 (commit `5778aeb`): `workspace-trust` *is* the prompt, so `_show_multi_trust_dialog` never ran and the capture failed with "the untrusted tile did not raise the Workspace Trust dialog" on every run - reproduced in a pristine `git archive HEAD` checkout in a fresh sandbox, so the dialog had stopped being captured by any state. Fix: an `approve_seeded_plans: false` flag on that one state, checked by the staging block; nothing else in the harness changes. Evidence: `scripts/ui-capture workspace-trust` writes the dialog again (Workspace Trust, the `/bin/sh` plan against `/bin/bash`, Restore and Cancel) and the full sweep has no failure. |
| ✅ | BUILD | **A CLI test depended on a process-global version-probe latch, so the parallel cargo pass failed** - found 2026-11-09 while running the full gate for the history-search keyboard work: `cargo test (parallel)` failed in `crates/gpty-cli/src/tests.rs::plugin_commands_probe_the_gui_version_first` with `probes == 0`. `daemon::VERSION_OBSERVED` was one process-wide `AtomicBool`, so whichever test read a `version` response first silenced every later probe; `reset_version_latch` cleared it, but a concurrently running test could set it again between that reset and the probe, and the assertion (`== 1`) then saw zero. The latch is now keyed by endpoint (`Mutex<Vec<String>>`, the path every call site already passes): production has one endpoint and still probes once, while a test's own socket cannot be observed by another test's probe, so the test is deterministic. `VERSION_WARNED` stays process-global - the mismatch is a property of the binaries, not of the command. Evidence: `cargo test -p gpty --bin gpty` passes 112/112 with default parallelism three runs in a row (and 112/112 with `--test-threads=1`); the serial pass already passed, only the parallel one failed before the fix. |
| ✅ | GUI | **The close confirmation names the workspace and the pane, not just `T1`** - reported 2026-11-10: with `lazygit` running in the `git` workspace's T1, closing the workspace asked "Closing will stop processes still running in: T1" - the label alone names neither the workspace nor what T1 is. The dialog was introduced in `b8bf5f9` with `pane_label`s only and the text has never changed (`git log -S'Closing will stop processes'` finds that commit alone), so the recollection of an earlier fix is the v0.7.0 row that added the confirmation itself. `_ask_running_then` now takes the caller's action phrase and a qualification flag: a workspace-scoped action names the workspace in the intro ("Closing the 'git' workspace will stop a running process in 1 of 2 panes:"), every other action qualifies each line with its workspace ("'git': T1 (Terminal)"), and `PaneBody.display_name()` supplies the user's own pane name or the type's name. The intro reports busy panes against the panes the action destroys, with singular, all and count forms. Tests: `test_running_ask_intro_counts_busy_panes_against_the_action_set`, `test_running_ask_entries_name_the_pane_and_its_workspace`, `test_closing_a_workspace_with_a_running_terminal_names_it`; `./scripts/gut-check` reports 101 scripts, 1214 tests, all passed, and `./scripts/ci-check` passes. |
| ✅ | BUILD | **Back-to-back docs pushes make the Docs workflow fail at its deploy step** - fixed 2026-11-10. Found 2026-11-09 while closing the history-search keyboard work: three docs pushes in one session produced two red `Docs` runs, both failing only at `Deploy to GitHub Pages` while every content step passed (`check markdown`, the knowledge-bundle check, the Hugo build, the three render verifications). Two distinct causes, one per failure: the newer run was refused with `Deployment request failed … due to in progress deployment. Please cancel <sha> first or wait for it to complete` (400), and the earlier one asks for artifact metadata immediately after `upload-pages-artifact` reports success and gets `Found 0 artifact(s)` (runs 42688595589 on `ac5fd0a`, 42690667476 on `71c04e8`). `gh run rerun --failed` was not a recovery path either: re-running the job uploaded a second `github-pages` artifact into the same run, and the deploy then died with `Multiple artifacts named "github-pages" were unexpectedly found … Artifact count is 2` (run 42690667476 on `71c04e8`); only a fresh run deployed. Fix: a workflow-level `concurrency: group: pages, cancel-in-progress: false` queues runs instead of racing them; the content steps and `Upload pages artifact` stay in `build` under `contents: read`, and the new `deploy` job (`needs: build`, `timeout-minutes: 15`, `actions: read` + `contents: read` + `pages: write` + `id-token: write`, `environment: github-pages`) holds only `actions/deploy-pages`, so a `--failed` rerun touches it alone; a wait step polls the run's artifact list (30 × 2 s) for exactly one `github-pages` artifact first, which is the 0.39 s case handled instead of met by luck - run 42688595589 was the only live run when its deploy queried at 19:59:37.693 and answered `Found 0 artifact(s)` at 19:59:37.790, the next run being created at 19:59:42. `workflow_dispatch` was already present (added in `7d09e0c`), so no retry switch was added; both Pages actions keep their pinned SHAs. Measured on the acceptance pair, pushed 10 s apart: run 42908063437 (`9cd8b13`) created 21:45:11Z, build 21:45:14→21:45:28, deploy 21:45:36→21:45:49, `success`; run 42908091183 (`64866cb`) created 21:45:21Z but queued until the first finished, its build starting 21:45:52 - two seconds after the first run ended - then deploy 21:46:09→21:46:56, `success`. No job of the two runs overlapped, the `github-pages` deployments were recorded in order (`9cd8b13` at 21:45:28, `64866cb` at 21:46:04), so the newer sha deployed last. |
| ✅ | SEC | **The MCP registry publish job executed the released tag with its OIDC identity** - fixed 2026-11-10 (code scanning alert 13, `actions/untrusted-checkout/critical`, open on `.github/workflows/mcp-registry.yml:84-95`): the publish job holds `id-token: write` and publishes under the project's name, yet it checked out `steps.release.outputs.tag \|\| github.ref` on a `workflow_run` and then ran `python3 scripts/mcp-registry check` from that tree. The job now runs only default-branch code - a literal `ref: main` checkout with `persist-credentials: false` - and reads the tag as data: `server.json` and `crates/gpty-cli/{Cargo.toml,README.md}` through the contents API into `$RUNNER_TEMP/descriptor`, validated by `scripts/mcp-registry check [ROOT]` (the optional root is new, with missing-file guards; `check` with no argument is unchanged for `scripts/ci-check`), and `mcp-publisher` is downloaded into and run from the staging directory, so the released descriptor is validated and published while the released tree is never executed. The ref is a literal because all three checkout queries key on `PRHeadCheckoutStep`, whose heuristics match event-derived refs (step ids, needed-job ids, JSON paths, fields matching `.*(head\|branch\|ref).*`) and taint from any `github.*` context. Verified locally against the staged v0.8.0 tree (pass, tag-mismatch refusal, crates.io 404 refusal) and by the re-scan: `gh api repos/godot-pty/gpty/code-scanning/alerts/13` reads `state: fixed` at 2026-11-10T21:46:05Z, and no `actions/untrusted-checkout/*` alert is open. The release skill's "fires on `release: published`" claim was corrected to the Release workflow's completion. |
| ✅ | DOCS | **The v0.8.1 changelog records the chrome, palette and discoverability work** - released 2026-11-10: `docs/content/docs/changelog/v0.8.1.md` (linked from `CHANGELOG.md`; the weight ladder moved up one step for every existing page - 190 down to 20 - so the new page takes 10) covers the three-control pane bar and its one action menu, the 15-entry palette with its glyphs, chords and keyboard cursor, the saved-command store and its Settings tab, the Add Pane header and help table with the About shortcut list, the search strip's `n/m` count and the toast contrast, the Feedback row, the return-to-live-output chevron, the window and toolbar keyboard rings, the scrollbar geometry, the `ExternalLink` refusal path, both stores' `.corrupt` recovery, the fitted spawn size, and the IPC refusal-code rule; the captures are `v0.8.1_1.png` (home), `v0.8.1_2.png` (the palette with saved commands), `v0.8.1_3.png` (the search strip), `v0.8.1_4.png` (the Add Pane help table), `v0.8.1_5.png` (the four badges) and `v0.8.1_6.png` (return to live output). |

## v0.8.2 - Pane Interaction & Responsiveness

| Done | Area | Item |
|:---:|:---:|---|
| ✅ | GUI | **Workspace and pane close is explicit, and the last workspace can be closed** - the sidebar's footer **Close All** is removed; **Workspaces:** and **Panes:** carry a trash that swaps to an inline check/cross before the running-work walk, every workspace row carries a **More** menu (**Set Directory**, whose tooltip is the current path, and **Clear Workspace**, which clears a hidden workspace without switching), and workspace/pane close uses the trash glyph (the pane title bar included). Closing the last workspace is legal: first run and "close all workspaces" land on zero workspaces with the guide, and the first pane created there creates a **Default** workspace. Tests: `test_sidebar` (row/title confirms, menu signal), `test_workspace_settings` (zero state, Default spawn, close last, header close-alls, menu, hidden clear), `test_pane_close_guard` (clear asks), `test_workspace_store` (zero round-trip); `scripts/ui-capture first-launch`/`empty-workspace` and `scripts/accessibility-probe`. |
| ✅ | GUI | **A workspace the user emptied restores a terminal on the next launch** - reported 2026-11-11 from daily use: close every pane of a workspace, quit, relaunch - the workspace has a terminal again. The save side is faithful (`_exit_tree` saves on quit, `workspace.gd:314-322`; `WorkspaceStore.save` keeps `layout: []`, `workspace_store.gd:41-52`) and an empty workspace is a supported visible state (`_apply_layout` shows `EmptyState`), so only the restore disagrees. Cause: `_restore_into` spawns one terminal whenever the restored set is empty and the pane ceiling skipped nothing (`workspace.gd:1417-1421`), a first-run rule applied to every empty layout - an empty store, a legacy import and a layout the user emptied are indistinguishable there. Fixed 2026-11-11: `_init_workspaces` derives `fresh` from an empty stored workspace set (a legacy import clears it) and passes it through `_build_workspaces(entries, active, seed_first)` into `_restore_into(ws, tiles, seed_terminal)`, where the seed now requires the flag; every other caller (profile activation, the tests) keeps the default `false`, so a profile that restores nothing stops inventing a terminal too. Tests: `test_an_empty_saved_layout_restores_no_panes` and `test_a_junk_layout_restores_nothing_instead_of_inventing_a_pane` in `test_layout_restore.gd` (the second replaces `test_restoring_nothing_usable_still_leaves_a_terminal`), `test_a_workspace_the_store_kept_empty_stays_empty` in `test_workspace_settings.gd` (a store whose workspace carries `layout: []`), and `test_pane_ceiling.gd`'s restored-workspace test now seeds a real tile instead of leaning on the fallback. Verified 2026-11-11: `test_layout_restore` 21 tests, `test_pane_ceiling` 8, `test_workspace_settings` 25, `test_startup_focus` 3, all green. Superseded 2026-11-11 by **Workspace and pane close is explicit, and the last workspace can be closed**: a first run now has no workspace or pane, and the first pane creates **Default**; the empty-layout rule stands. |
| ✅ | GUI | **A spawned pane is not the active pane, and a close falls back to the wrong one** - reported 2026-11-11 from daily use: a workspace's first terminal does not take the keyboard until it is clicked, and closing the newest of three panes activates the oldest instead of the middle one. Both are the activation decision, not the focus plumbing: the sidebar's Add Pane is connected straight to `_spawn_pane` (`workspace.gd:2019-2020`) and `_spawn_pane_into` never activates, so a fresh workspace keeps `last_body == null` and the keyboard on the sidebar button; `_kill_now` scans `ws.tm.tiles` forward and takes the first `TerminalPane` (`workspace.gd:621-647`), so T1,T2,T3 minus T3 focuses T1 although the tiling logic's survivor is T2. Fixed 2026-11-11: `_spawn_pane_into(..., activate := true)` is the one activation point and is gated on `ws.tm == _tm`, so a restore or a spawn into a hidden workspace records nothing; the shortcut closure, the palette's `new ` arm and `_request_pane_into` lost their hand-written `activate_pane` calls, `newPane` passes its `focus` flag through, and `paneRun` passes `false`. `_kill_now` takes `held_focus = ws.tm.last_body == body or _focus_owner_in(body)` and activates `_last_pane_body(ws)` (reverse tile order, any pane type) before `_list()`. Tests: `test_a_spawned_pane_is_the_active_pane` (the sidebar's `request_new_pane` signal leaves the new pane as `last_body` and the viewport focus owner), `test_killing_the_focused_pane_hands_focus_to_the_last_survivor` (T1,T2,T3 minus T3 leaves T2), `test_closing_the_active_pane_records_a_read_only_last_pane` (active with no focus owner); `test_wiki_note_keyboard` and `test_startup_focus`'s unvisited-workspace case now spawn with `activate := false`, the restore route. Verified 2026-11-11: `test_workspace_settings` 25, `test_startup_focus` 3, `test_wiki_note_keyboard` 4, `test_ipc_dispatch_contract` 31, and the activation-sensitive files `test_palette`, `test_pane_open_request`, `test_overlay_focus`, `test_pane_settings_panel`, `test_pane_close_guard`, `test_title_bar_keyboard`, `test_agent_state_contract`, all green. |
| ✅ | GUI | **A workspace switch costs about a second on the daily-use set** - reported 2026-11-11 from daily use (two workspaces - `omp` terminal + Reasoning, and `btop` in a terminal - window 2053x1382, window mode 0, `max_fps` 165): every switch, especially into `btop`, takes about a second, while the same set measured 63-75 ms of synchronous GDScript on 2026-11-09 (see **The sidebar rebuilds every list on a workspace switch**). The first fix removed the switch-time settings re-apply (`_on_settings_changed`) and the unconditional window-mode round trip, and the committed bench then measured 36.6-37.4 ms - but the reporter still saw the second, so the bench was not covering the expensive path. Re-measured 2026-11-11 against the reporter's own store, copied into `scripts/ui-sandbox`, with `btop` running in both panes (windowed 2053x1382, `--disable-vsync`): `_switch_workspace` 0->1 cost 992-1,020 ms and the first painted frame 1.5-1.6 s, while 1->0 cost 37-39 ms. The view steps account for 37 ms of that (workspace UI 16-17 ms, profiles 10 ms, `_list()` 11 ms, save 0.25 ms, layout/titlebars/status under 0.1 ms). The second is the focus path: `TerminalPane._on_a11y_focus_entered` -> `_push_accessibility_value` -> `A11y.publish`, which created a `Label` whose `text` was the whole screen - 221x78 = 17 315 characters cost 992 308 us in that one call, against 3 362 us for the `omp` pane's 134-character screen. `Label` shapes and measures its `text`; the name a driver reads comes from `Control.accessibility_name` (`Control::_get_accessibility_name`) and `publish` already sets it, while the value `Label.text` feeds does not reach AT-SPI on 4.7.2. Fix: `A11y.publish` no longer sets `node.text`, and the slot-reuse check compares `accessibility_name` (against `text` it could never match, so the slot would churn on every push). After: the 17 315-character publish is 53 us, 0->1 is 40.6 ms, 1->0 is 38.9 ms, and the first frame is 78 ms. `test_publish_carries_text_name_and_live_mode` and `test_publish_replaces_the_slot` pin the name channel and the empty `text`. |
| ✅ | GUI | **A dialog can grow past the window it opens in** - reported 2026-11-11 from daily use: the "Stop running processes?" confirmation for a workspace whose panes are all running extends beyond the app viewport, and "Close anyway" can end up below the bottom edge. `AppDialog` never clamps a modal: it sets no `max_size` and every site centres with a bare `popup_centered()` (`workspace.gd:578` and 25 more), and the autowrap body `set_text` builds has no width floor (`app_dialog.gd:141-148`), so the engine shapes it one word per line before the first layout and the card's minimum size is unbounded in both axes - the running-work list (`RUNNING_ASK_MAX_NAMES = 8`, `workspace.gd:10`) is the app's longest dialog text. Settings is the counter-example: an in-tree card at `UiTokens.MIN_LAYOUT` (540x560) with a `ScrollContainer` per tab, so content can never grow it. Related: **Small windows adapt instead of clipping**. Fixed 2026-11-11: `AppDialog.modal_limit`/`text_width`/`available_size` are the pure maths (0.75 of the window less 24 px, floored at 240x160), `AppDialog.popup(dialog, ratio)` sets `max_size` and centres with `popup_centered_clamped`, `set_text` gives its autowrap label a width floor (it was shaped one word per line before its first layout, which is what made the card tall) and `set_body` clamps its requested minimum; the hidden engine label that carries `dialog_text` clips, so a long line there cannot set the card's width again. All 25 modal sites migrated (`popup_centered()` -> `AppDialog.popup`; the cwd picker keeps its 0.6 ratio). Tests: `test_app_dialog.gd` 18 green (limit maths, `max_size` at the limit, the label floor, the clamped body, the clipped engine label) and a new `running-work` capture state at 800x500 over four busy panes, whose workspace-scoped card lists four names and sits wholly inside the frame. Screenshot (under Screenshots below): *Stop running processes? at 800x500*. |
| ✅ | GUI | **Typing into a pane that is also streaming is throttled to 10 Hz by the flood back-off** - found 2026-11-11 from daily use (typing into the OMP pane in T1 while btop runs in T2), measured in a sandbox on the v0.8.1 source at the reporter's shape (165 fps, two panes, 102x78 pane): a 60-line/s coloured stream in the pane being typed into pegs `_sync_backoff` at 93.9 ms - the `MAX_SYNC_INTERVAL` ceiling - so the grid is refreshed 2-3 times per 300 ms (7-10 Hz) and a keystroke's echo arrives at the next refresh, key-to-next-grid-change p50 15 ms and max 114.8 ms; btop's 18-39 ms repaints in the other pane drop the frame rate from 165 to 145-155 and widen that tail. Cause: the budget check `_fetch_ms + _draw_ms > PANE_WORK_BUDGET_MS` runs once per sync with no regard for the stretched cadence, so a pane whose fetch+draw is 8-10 ms keeps re-arming the back-off although that is 9 % of a 100 ms window, and only view input (scroll, search) was exempt (`user_driving` reads `_view_input_ms`), never typing - the pane the user types into was the one being throttled. Fixed: `_note_view_input` zeroes `_sync_backoff` and both send helpers (`_send_to_term`, `_send_line_to_term`) call it, so input reclaims the frame cadence for `VIEW_INPUT_GRACE_MS` (500 ms). Verified in the same sandbox: back-off 0.0 in every phase, 14-19 grid refreshes per 300 ms (47-63 Hz), key-to-grid p50 14.2-14.3 ms and max 63-70 ms; `test_typed_input_reclaims_the_frame_cadence` added to `test_flood_cadence.gd`. |
| ✅ | GUI | **Re-applying unchanged settings repacks the whole grid** - measured 2026-11-09 on the same run: the incoming terminal's first `_process` after a switch reports `_fetch_ms` 5-6 ms with `is_full = true` on the 145x78 `omp` pane, although it produced no output while hidden. Cause: `_switch_workspace` calls `_on_settings_changed()`, which passes `color_scheme_path` into `apply_to_terminal` on every broadcast; `PaneBody.apply_settings` sets every accepted key unconditionally, and `TerminalPane.color_scheme_path`'s setter calls `_apply_stored_scheme()`, which with no scheme calls `set_palette("")`; `set_palette` sets `palette_changed = true` and bumps the generation even when the palette already is the system default (`crates/gpty-gdext/src/lib.rs:509-513`), and `TermGrid::get_grid_updates` answers any `palette_changed` with `GridUpdate::Full` (`crates/gpty-core/src/term.rs:386-392`), so all 11 310 cells are repacked into new arrays and the pane's whole cell cache is replaced. Fix: return from the `color_scheme_path` setter when the value is unchanged and make `set_palette` flag only a real change. Tests: re-applying identical settings leaves `get_grid_generation()` unmoved, a changed scheme still repaints, and a settings save that touches no pane setting leaves every generation alone. Fixed 2026-11-11 from the switch bench: the `color_scheme_path`, `font_path` and `max_fps` setters return on an unchanged value, so the palette bump, the font reload with its cell-metric recompute, and the double refresh-rate probe are skipped, and the switch path no longer broadcasts settings at all - a settings save reaches every workspace once (`test_a_settings_save_reaches_a_hidden_workspace`). `set_palette` itself was left alone: its one caller reaches it only after the guard, so the Rust-side `palette_changed` change the row first proposed was not needed. Measured on the same bench: the incoming `btop` pane's first frame after a switch dropped from 18.6-18.7 ms to 6.4 ms, and its `_fetch_ms` from 7-9 to 1-2. |
| ✅ | DOCS | **The v0.8.2 changelog records the interaction and responsiveness fixes** - released 2026-11-12: `docs/content/docs/changelog/v0.8.2.md` (linked from `CHANGELOG.md`; the weight ladder moved up one step for every existing page - now 200 down to 20 - so the new page takes 10) covers the empty-workspace shortcut guide and its keycap action, the explicit workspace and pane close affordances with the zero-workspace first run, the seeded default layouts and plugin-profile uninstall, the active-pane, close-fallback and empty-restore repairs, the clamped dialogs, the flood cadence under typing, the workspace-switch cost and its measured fixes, the FPS-only status bar, and the sidebar rename and centering repairs. |

## v0.8.3 - Windows, Panes & Responsiveness

| Done | Area | Item |
|:---:|:---:|---|
| ✅ | BUILD | **Rebuilding with a GUI open kills it with SIGBUS** - found 2026-11-13, twice in one session: `scripts/build` copied the new `libgpty_gdext` over `godot/bin/` with `cp`, and a running `godot --path godot` that had that library mapped died on the write (`fish: Job 1, 'godot --path godot' terminated by signal SIGBUS (Misaligned address error)`), taking every pane in it down. Fix: the install writes `$LIB_DST.new`, chmods it and renames it over the old name, so a process that already mapped the library keeps its inode and no build can kill a GUI. Verified by rebuilding with a dev GUI running. |
| ✅ | BUILD | **The export still rewrites `dist/gpty` under a running export** - fixed 2026-11-13, found while fixing **Rebuilding with a GUI open kills it with SIGBUS**: the library install was atomic, but `scripts/build`'s Godot export wrote `dist/gpty`, `dist/gpty.pck`, the exported `libgpty_gdext.linux.x86_64.so` and the bundled FFmpeg libraries in place, so a `dist/gpty` instance (the accessibility probe's, a demo recording's, a user's copy) that had them mapped died with the same SIGBUS. `scripts/build` now exports into `dist/.stage.XXXXXX` (on `dist/`'s own filesystem, so every later `mv` is a rename), bundles the media runtime there, and installs the finished set entry by entry through one `install_atomic` helper - a file renamed over the old name, a directory (`dist/licenses`) renamed aside first and the old one then removed - which the `godot/bin/` install uses too, so a failed export leaves the previous build untouched. `dist/` stays a mixed directory (`aur/` tracked, `ffmpeg/` the pinned inputs), which is why the set is installed entry by entry instead of swapping the directory. Verified against a sandboxed running export, its maps read with `GPTY_ALLOW_DEBUG=1` (the app clears `PR_SET_DUMPABLE`): one pane created, then `./scripts/build` under it - rc 0, process alive, `list-panes` still answering with that pane, all 7 files it had mapped (`dist/gpty`, `libgpty_gdext`, the five `libav*`/`libsw*`) reading `(deleted)` in `/proc/&lt;pid&gt;/maps` while the on-disk inodes moved (gpty 808996 → 809031, pck 808997 → 809032, gdext 808999 → 809034), no `dist/.stage.*` left, `dist/aur` and `dist/ffmpeg` untouched; the installed bundle then launched fresh, spawned a pane, injected and matched a marker through `pane-wait`, and `daemon stop` exited cleanly with no error in its log. pitfalls.md carries the trap. |
| ✅ | GUI | **Opening Settings with a non-terminal first tile raises a script error and empties the Concepts section** - found 2026-11-13 from a crash report: `Workspace.get_terminal_for_ffi` walked every tile reading `body._terminal`, which on a `ReasoningPane` is `Invalid access to property or key '_terminal' on a base object of type 'Control (ReasoningPane)'` (`workspace.gd:3256`); the function aborts, answers null, and `settings_panel.gd`'s `_add_concept_section` then disables Add Concept and lists nothing even when a terminal sits in another tile. Fix: the walk answers only a `TerminalPane`'s live backend (`body is TerminalPane and body._terminal != null`). Tests: `test_workspace_concept_terminal.gd` (a reasoning tile first and a terminal second must find the terminal; a workspace with no terminal answers null without raising the error, which GUT would fail the test on). |
| ✅ | GUI | **The workspace store drops the grid unit it is handed** - fixed 2026-11-12: `PaneTypes` now owns the unit vocabulary - `GRID_LEGACY` (12) beside `GRID`, and `sanitize_grid_unit(raw)`, which returns a declared unit only when it is one this build can scale and 0 ("unknown, infer") otherwise - and the field round-trips both ways: `WorkspaceStore.save` writes it (absent in the dict = this build's `PaneTypes.GRID`), `WorkspaceStore.load` reads it through `_as_int` (absent = unknown), and `_init_workspaces`'s entry dicts carry it into `_build_workspaces`'s existing `scale_layout` call, including through the trust dialog's approve/cancel paths. Tests: `test_grid_unit_round_trips_and_unknown_units_are_dropped` (store) and `test_a_store_written_in_a_coarser_unit_restores_the_same_shares` (integration; the tiles stop at half the 12-unit grid so inference alone rescales them, verified by reverting the restore read - the same tiles measure 510x389 px instead of 255x194.5). |
| ✅ | GUI | **The engine rejects the accessible value writes, and every launch prints two errors** - fixed 2026-11-12: `A11y` no longer calls the server at the call site. `set_value`/`set_text` record `VALUE_META` and, when a driver exists, queue the pair in `PENDING_VALUE_META`/`PENDING_ROLE_META` and ask for `queue_accessibility_update()`; `A11y.apply_update(control)` is the only writer and runs from the host's own `NOTIFICATION_ACCESSIBILITY_UPDATE` - `TerminalPane._notification` forwards it, the only `PaneBody` that records a value. The engine side is why: 4.7.2 refuses `update_set_role`/`update_set_value` unless `in_accessibility_update` is set (`drivers/accesskit/accessibility_server_accesskit.cpp:746`, `:834`), and that flag is true only inside `_accessibility_build_tree_update`'s `update_cb` (`:649-653`) -> `SceneTree::_process_accessibility_changes` -> `node->notification(NOTIFICATION_ACCESSIBILITY_UPDATE)` (`scene/main/scene_tree.cpp:263`). Verified: a rebuilt export launched with `--accessibility always --accessibility-driver accesskit --verbose` logs `AccessKit driver loaded` + `adapter created` and **zero** refusal lines (no `ERROR` line at all; the v0.8.1 build printed two per launch). Tests: `test_set_value_records_the_value_without_a_driver` (headless queues nothing), `test_apply_update_consumes_a_queued_value_once`, `test_a_terminal_forwards_its_accessibility_update`. `scripts/accessibility-probe` now fails a run whose app log carries the refusal, before its tree walk so another assertion cannot mask it - proven by re-introducing the illegal call, rebuilding, and watching the probe print it and exit 1. The value still does not reach AT-SPI on 4.7.2 (the adapter maps neither value nor role); the Future row **Re-test the AT-SPI element-value mapping when an engine release carries it** now says the call side is fixed, so that re-test grades the mapping. |
| ✅ | GUI | **A pane cannot be moved to another workspace without closing it** - shipped 2026-11-13 (CI run 43453896318 green). Workspaces are live parallel grids (`_workspaces`, `workspace.gd`), so a move is a reparent: the PTY, scrollback and pane env are keyed by `attachment_id` (all workspace-independent) and `_resolve_pane_target` already addresses panes across workspaces, so nothing is re-keyed. Blocker found in the feasibility pass: `reparent` fires `_exit_tree`, which `cli_view`, `media` and `inspector` treat as close (`_cli.stop`, `_stop_backend`, `_close_session`), so a naive move kills the backend; `PaneBody.backend_preserved` guards it. Policy A moves a terminal with its follow set (`source_attachment_id` in its own workspace, today Reasoning only); a follower moved alone is warned that it will observe nothing; the Inspector has no follow link and moves only when named, warned when `auto_run` starts answering the target workspace's captures. Also fixes, all found in the same pass: the three `ws`-capturing closures in `_attach_pane_into`/`_wire_pane_activation` (a moved terminal's badge freezes because `_agent_state_primed` still matches, a moved file-tree pane spawns into the old workspace, and a stale `last_body` lets "close active" close a pane in another workspace); `pane_label` collisions on adopt, which make label-addressed IPC permanently ambiguous; and atomicity (capacity is planned before anything detaches, so a refusal mutates nothing). Fix: `TerminalManager.detach/plan_adopt/adopt` (reusing `kill`'s rect absorption and `_split_for`), `Workspace._move_panes_into`, the pane-menu submenu, `movePane` + `workspaceList` IPC, `move-pane` + `workspace-list` CLI/MCP tools. Tests: 9 in `test_workspace_pane_move.gd` (same pane instances and ids, PTY session and child alive, rects tile the grid exactly, a full-grid refusal mutates nothing, label unique, the active pane stays, `request_pane` lands in the target, the badge resumes, the move notes), 3 IPC contract tests, and the pane-menu submenu test. Measured live: the moved PTY kept pid 660430 and its scrollback marker, W1 0 / W2 3 panes after the move and 2/1 after the move back, and `workspaces.json` round-tripped the membership. |
| ✅ | GUI | **A restored terminal publishes no text to the AT-SPI tree** - fixed 2026-11-13, and the row was two defects. (1) *The push was starved*: `_a11y_tick` gated `_push_accessibility_value` on a settle window keyed to the grid's damage counter, so a pane whose generation moved faster than `A11Y_SETTLE_MS` never pushed - a GUT tick right after `echo A11Y_MARKER` published nothing, and the export trace (`enabled=true`) showed the value pushed twice at start-up with an empty screen and never again. The value now follows the screen text, limited only by `A11Y_PUSH_MS`; the settle window delays the one-line announcement alone, and the publish host is re-resolved per push so a rebuilt tile cannot leave a body pointing at a freed bar. (2) *The published node's name was empty*: a `Label`'s AT-SPI name is its display text, not `accessibility_name` (proved by publishing a literal label text - the tree showed that literal), so the content node arrived unnamed; a bare `Control` is dropped from the tree entirely (container role). New `A11yContent` (`godot/scenes/ui/a11y_content.gd`) is a `Control` that writes the role `A11y.set_text` queues from its own update notification, so the node carries role 79 and the whole screen in its name with no text shaping (the 992 ms cost of 2026-11-11). Tests: `test_a_restored_terminal_publishes_its_screen_on_its_own_host` (the body's own `_a11y_host`, the node's name, `VALUE_META`), `test_a_churning_grid_still_publishes_the_value`, `test_the_value_push_is_rate_limited`, `test_the_announcement_waits_for_a_quiet_grid`, and `test_publish_carries_text_name_and_live_mode` now pins that the node is not a Label; the gate is reachable headless through the new `A11y.force_enabled` seam. `scripts/accessibility-probe` also stops asserting a marker it never typed: it resolves a CLI (`GPTY_CLI`, else `gpty`, else `target/release/gpty`), exits 3 without one, and prints its first non-empty `Text` answers. Verified end to end: `./scripts/accessibility-probe` exits 0 and the marker appears in the name dump as `[neilp@cachyos-x8664 ~]$ echo A11Y_PROBE_MARKER \| A11Y_PROBE_MARKER \| ...`. |
| ✅ | GUI | **A streaming pane nobody is typing into still refreshes at 10 Hz** - cadence half fixed 2026-11-13; the paint half is split out as **A terminal repaint rebuilds the whole canvas when one row changed** (next row). The budget is now a share of the wall clock, `PANE_WORK_BUDGET_MS * (_sync_interval + _sync_backoff) / _sync_interval` - a quarter of a 60 Hz frame, ~24 % at any cadence - so a sync over its share stretches the cadence and one under it calls the new `_relax_sync_backoff()`, which is the half the old code lacked: a streaming child has damage every sync, so the found-nothing-new branch that used to relax the cadence never ran and the pane parked at `MAX_SYNC_INTERVAL`. User input still resets it (`_note_view_input`). Measured with `BENCH_FLOOD=1 draw_bench --max-fps 60` on the row's own grid, 3 reps per side at 102x78 and 2 at 120x60: 102x78 went 10.5-13.3 syncs/s with `_sync_backoff` pegged at 83.3 ms to 33.5-39.5 syncs/s at `backoff_ms=0.0`; 120x60 went 16.5-23.2 to 34.6-39.5. `draw_bench` gained `syncs`/`sync_hz`/`backoff_ms` (its `gen_changes` counts grid damage, not the pane's syncs) and both its header and the flood helper now say `--max-fps 60` is required for a cadence question - unthrottled, the loop outruns the back-off and every frame syncs, which measures cost but not rate. Tests: `test_a_sync_that_fits_the_stretched_window_relaxes_the_cadence` and `test_the_same_cost_still_stretches_at_the_frame_cadence` pin both directions with the same 9 ms cost; `test_backoff_never_exceeds_the_cadence_ceiling` now uses a cost above the ceiling's own scaled budget (40 ms) because a 24 ms sync legitimately fits a 100 ms window; the probe helper is `_run_sync_with_draw_cost`. Gate: 1333 GUT tests pass. |
| ✅ | GUI | **A terminal repaint rebuilds the whole canvas when one row changed** - fixed 2026-11-13: `TerminalPane`'s paint is split across child CanvasItems - one background band and one text band per row (a `Layer` Node2D, drawn in the order the two passes used), then an overlay for the focus border, cursor, scrollback indicator, selection and search highlights. A partial grid update queues only the rows its damage covers (`_painted_rows`, ascending); a full update queues everything; a cursor blink, a selection change and a search change now queue the overlay alone. The bands keep an identity transform and draw at the grid's absolute pen positions: the text server picks the glyph raster from the position argument, not from the composed item transform, so a per-band `position` repainted every glyph with a different antialiasing (73 357 differing pixels in the bench PNG). `_draw_ms` is now a float sum of the bands a frame drew, so the cadence budget reads a one-row update as one row. Measured on the release gdext (`draw_bench`, 2026-11-13): a full repaint 2976 → 2834 µs at 102x78 and 2913 → 2630 µs at 120x60 (idle 241/264 µs), one row 60 µs and 32 µs over idle, a cursor blink 0-3 µs; `BENCH_SPARSE=1 --max-fps 60` (one line per 0.2 s) 2286 → 190 µs per damage sync at 102x78 and 2286 → 120 µs at 120x60, `avg_rows` 2, `backoff_ms` 0; `BENCH_FLOOD=1` still repaints every row (`avg_rows` 78/60) at 3262 → 2957 µs and 2646 → 2434 µs. The render is pixel-identical: `ui-capture --compare` reports identical PNGs for the `draw_bench` pair at both grids and for the staged `workspace-1280x760`, `search` and `scrollback-return` captures (the search capture is what caught the band-order bug below). Tests: `test_terminal_repaint.gd` - a full repaint lays one background and one text band per row at the origin (a band transform is the antialiasing bug), a smaller grid frees the extra bands and keeps the overlay above them, a partial update queues only its damaged rows, and a typed line in a real shell repaints at most 4 of ≥ 10 rows and covers every row whose text changed, with every band below the search strip. The end-to-end assertion first read "the repainted rows are the cursor's", which Windows CI turned red on 2026-11-13: ConPTY rewrites the rows it relays, so one typed line damaged rows 0, 3, 4 and 6 with the cursor on row 6, so the test asserts the platform-neutral direction (text changed ⇒ repainted) and `pitfalls.md` carries the trap. Both paint traps are recorded in `pitfalls.md`; the ordering one is the reason a real-pane test exists at all - a synthetic probe (no strip) passed with the bands in the wrong order, and only the `search` capture diff showed the grid painting over the strip's panel, because moving the background band and its text band in one interleaved `move_child` pass leaves every text band *after* the strip whenever the strip sits between the two groups. |
| ✅ | GUI | **Small windows adapt instead of clipping** - fixed 2026-11-13 (build 0.8.3). The Settings and Pane Settings cards clamp to the window instead of keeping a fixed size: `OverlayPanel.fit_card` sets `custom_minimum_size` to `min(design, host - 2*edge)`, shared with the command palette (which already did this inline), so the 540×560 card is 540×484 at 800×500 - inside the window, where it used to be centred at y=-30 with 60 px past the bottom - and 540×324 at 560×340 where it was clipped at (10, -110); the height is what clamps first - the 540 px design width fits a 560 px window - and every tab was already a `ScrollContainer`, so a shorter card scrolls its content. A pane title stops at the button column (`TitleLabel` inset 30 px from the left, clearing the agent badge, and 76 px from the right against the 74 px button reserve) and ellipsizes with `OVERRUN_TRIM_ELLIPSIS`, the full title on its tooltip; both `title_changed` handlers write through the new `TerminalManager.set_title_label`. The sidebar scrolls as one column (`SidebarScroll` between the margin and `SidebarContent`) and its pane list takes the five-row cap `SECTION_MAX_VISIBLE_ROWS` already claimed for all three lists, so the stack overflows the 488 px content area of an 800×500 window and every row is reachable by scrolling instead of the list being squeezed into the 33 px the fixed blocks above it left; `Sidebar.WIDTH`/`COLLAPSED_WIDTH` replace the `180`/`20` literals in the workspace and in the fold. `UiTokens.MIN_LAYOUT` drops from 540×560 to 560×340 - the smallest layout this row asserts, and the size at which the card still keeps all seven tabs - so `UiTokens.ui_scale_ceiling(Vector2i(800, 500))` is 142 % where it was 89 %: a 125 % request in an 800×500 window is honoured instead of forced to 100 %, and the cap now only refuses a scale that would squeeze the layout below the floor. Tests: `test_small_window.gd` (the Settings card at 800×500 and 560×340 and its re-clamp on a resize while open, the Pane Settings card at 560×340, a title's right edge at or before its buttons' left edge with a title measured wider than the label, and the sidebar's last pane row reachable after scrolling, with the pane list still filling a tall window) plus the re-pinned floor in `test_ui_scale.gd`, `test_settings_panel.gd` and `test_workspace_settings.gd`. Screenshots (under Screenshots below): *Small window, 800×500* and *Small window, Settings*. |
| ✅ | GUI | **Resizing a pane is smooth: the resize cursor always shows, no gray band while dragging, no hop on release** - fixed 2026-11-13, four defects in one row, all reproduced on the 0.8.2 build. (1) *The cursor recovers.* `TileDrag.drive_edge_drag` ends the drag on the left release and now returns false for it, so `Workspace._input` no longer consumes the release of the press the grab band took and the viewport sees its matching release; the drag publishes `divider_drag_started(axis)`/`divider_drag_finished`, and the workspace answers with `Input.set_default_cursor_shape` (HSIZE/VSIZE for the axis, ARROW after) because the pointer leaves the handle on the first motion. (2) *No grey band.* A terminal now fills itself with the grid's own background - `palette[0]`, which `NamedColor::Background` resolves every cell's background to - read back through the new `GptyTerminal.get_background_color()`, instead of a per-pane `default_bg` no cell ever painted; `default_bg` is now the terminal's background (`set_background_color` writes `palette[0]` and forces the full repack that re-resolves every cell, default black, and a scheme's first colour wins while one is set). The grid also follows the divider: `_on_resize` stops restarting its 50 ms quiet period while a drag is in flight, and `TerminalPane.set_drag_resizing` turns it into a 10 Hz throttle (`RESIZE_DRAG_INTERVAL`) plus one final reflow on release - measured mid-drag, the grown pane went 59 to 63 columns with the button still down. (3) *No hop.* `_preview_edge_drag` rounds the pointer travel to whole grid units before moving anything, the same integer `_commit_edge_drag` applies: a 40 px drag on a 17 px unit previews and commits 34 px. (4) *One handle per divider line.* The four 6 px strips per pane are gone (`EDGE_STRIP`, `EdgeHost`, `_make_edge_strip`, `_sync_edge_strips`): `godot/scenes/terminal/divider_handles.gd` (`DividerHandles`) puts one 12 px band per divider segment - exactly the stretch where a tile ends on the line and another starts on it, so a spanning pane never has a handle over its own content - inside the workspace grid, topmost, with a 2 px ACCENT highlight on hover; the old pair of strips left the two 1 px wrapper borders dead inside a 14 px target, and a divider with no travel (one pane, or both sides at `MIN_TILE`) shows an arrow and starts nothing. Drags are started by line (`TileDrag.begin_line_drag`/`line_limits`, `_tiles_on_divider` reading the live model before a press). Tests: `test_divider_handles.gd` (segment geometry: side-by-side, the Agent Workspace L, 2x2, a spanning tile, band rects) and `test_pane_edge_drag.gd` rewritten (one band per line and its cursor, no band for a single or immovable divider, GUI picking, preview == commit, the release not consumed, absolute backtracking, the `MIN_TILE` clamp, one line moving every tile, and the mid-drag reflow plus the final one); `test_the_ground_is_the_grid_background` in `test_terminal_repaint.gd` pins `ground_color() == get_background_color()` across a `default_bg` change and a scheme. Screenshot (under Screenshots below): *Divider handle* and *Resize drag*. |
| ✅ | GUI | **The sidebar's three sections cannot be folded** - fixed 2026-11-13 (build 0.8.3); requested from daily use, measured 2026-11-05 on the 0.8.0 development build: `sidebar.gd` builds Workspaces (`_add_workspace_section`), Profiles (`_add_profile_section`) and Panes (`_add_pane_list_ui`) as permanently open sections, and each reserves vertical space the user cannot give back - the workspace and profile lists grow to `SECTION_MAX_VISIBLE_ROWS` (5) measured rows through `_measured_section_height`, and the pane list takes every remaining pixel (`size_flags_vertical = SIZE_EXPAND_FILL`); the only fold in the app is the whole 180 px sidebar (`_toggle_sidebar`, `Ctrl+Shift+B`). Panes is also the one section with no header row: a bare `Label` whose text carries a leading space (" Panes:", against "Workspaces:" and "Profiles:"), hidden while the list is empty, holding no button. At 800x500 the pane list is already cut after one row - the state *Small windows adapt instead of clipping* measures - so folding the two lists the user is not switching right now is what buys that space back, and the pane list's `SIZE_EXPAND_FILL` gives the freed height to it with no layout code. Fix: one header builder for all three sections - the heading label (`SIZE_EXPAND_FILL`), the section's existing add button where it has one (`AddWorkspaceBtn`, `SaveProfileBtn`), then the fold toggle as the header's right-most control, which leaves Panes with the toggle alone; `Icons.style_button`, `focus_mode = FOCUS_ALL` (the v0.8.0 keyboard pass made every sidebar control a tab stop) and a `tooltip_text` naming the section and the direction. The label's glyph becomes the state: caret-down while open, caret-up while folded; the set has only caret-left/right (`Icons.COLLAPSE` `E138`, `Icons.EXPAND` `E13A`, what the whole-sidebar arrow draws), so add caret-down `E136` and caret-up `E13C` - resolved by rendering the bundled Phosphor font, where `E136` is 704 units wide at the top of its box and 46 at the bottom and `E13C` the reverse. Folding hides the section's `ScrollContainer`, which container sorting then skips - measured on 4.7.2, laid out over frames: a `VBoxContainer` of heading, `ScrollContainer` (`custom_minimum_size.y = 150`) and a following label reports a 216 px combined minimum and places that label at y=187, and 62 px / y=33 with the scroll container hidden, back to 216 / 187 when it is shown again; the `ScrollContainer` reserves nothing of its own even holding five 30 px rows (a 66 px minimum with it shown against 62 hidden - the 4 px separation), so `_measured_section_height` is the only thing reserving list space and the refresh that runs it (`update_workspace_list` / `update_profile_list` / `update_pane_list`) cannot re-open a folded section. The header row inherits `_pane_heading`'s empty-list visibility rule, so a Pane section with no tiles still shows no heading. Fold state persists per section in `user://settings.json` (a closed `sidebar_folded` list of the three section ids, `SettingsManager`), so a user who folds Profiles to reach Panes keeps it across launches; the whole-sidebar collapse stays non-persisted, which `docs/content/wiki/gpty/sidebar.md` documents. The engine `FoldableContainer` already folds the Reasoning pane's turns, but it owns its own header row and toggle, which is not the placement requested here. Docs: that page's "Shape and collapse" section. Tests: `godot/tests/integration/test_sidebar.gd` - a fold hides the list and drops the section's combined minimum to the header, an update while folded does not re-open it, the glyph and tooltip swap, and only a registered section id is accepted from the settings key. Shipped 2026-11-13 (build 0.8.3): each section is one header row plus its list, built by `Sidebar._add_section_header` (heading label, the section's own controls in order, then the fold toggle right-most), so the Panes section gains a header row and stops being a bare " Panes:" label; the caret is `Icons.CARET_DOWN` while open and `CARET_UP` while folded, and the tooltip and accessible name carry the verb and the section (`Fold the Profiles section` / `Show the Profiles section`). The state is `SettingsManager.cfg_sidebar_folded`, a `sidebar_folded` list in `user://settings.json` reduced on load to `SIDEBAR_SECTIONS` ids in registry order and written by `set_sidebar_folded` without the `settings_changed` broadcast, because nothing a pane draws reads a fold and that broadcast re-applies terminal settings to every pane; the whole-sidebar collapse still is not saved. A list refresh never touches the fold, and the Panes host gives up its `SIZE_EXPAND_FILL` while folded so a closed section does not hold an empty band open. Docs: `docs/content/wiki/gpty/sidebar.md` ("Shape and collapse") and the sidebar bullet in `docs/content/docs/agents/gdscript.md`. Implemented tests: `test_sidebar.gd` (43/43) and `test_settings_manager.gd` (17/17), plus the `sidebar-folded` capture state. Screenshot (under Screenshots below): *Foldable sidebar sections*. |
| ✅ | GUI | **Creating a pane gets slower as the window fills** - fixed 2026-11-14 (build 0.8.3). The growth was the sidebar, not the tiling: `Sidebar._render_panes` cleared and rebuilt every row on any pane-list change, at the recorded 7-10.4 ms per row (five Phosphor glyph Labels), so a spawn at 64 panes rebuilt 64 rows it already had. `_sync_pane_rows` mutates the list in place from `_pane_rows` (body instance id -> row): a closed row is detached in the same call (not queued), a spawn builds only its own row, a renumbered label is rewritten on its row, and a row's actions swap for its check/cross in place (`_apply_row_pending`), so the close-confirm paths do not rebuild either. The create path also stopped paying a whole-window title-bar sweep (the new bar already follows `cfg_show_titlebar`) and now defers `DividerHandles.refresh()` one idle (`_apply_layout(false)` plus `_queue_divider_refresh`), whose O(grid x tiles) scan was 5.1 ms at 64 panes. The check the row asked for is committed: `godot/tests/bench/spawn_bench.gd` (POSIX, needs a display; per-spawn `sync_us`, child-resolved `total_us`, RSS, child count, and per-pass marks at 1/8/16/32/48/64 panes). Measured 2026-11-14, 1920x1080 windowed X11, debug build, 64 panes: `sync_us` 35 382 -> 34 747 at pane 1 and 761 655 -> 25 016 at pane 64, slope +11 528 -> -154 us per live pane, `total_us` 810 825 -> 51 435, RSS 1198-1235 -> 1192-1211 MiB, children 64 against 64 tiles. Tests: `test_a_spawn_adds_one_row_and_keeps_the_attached_ones`, `test_a_closed_pane_row_leaves_in_the_same_call`, `test_a_renumbered_pane_rewrites_its_row_label`, `test_a_close_confirm_swaps_only_its_rows_actions`. |
| ✅ | GUI | **The sidebar rebuilds every list on a workspace switch, 62 of 74 ms** - fixed 2026-11-13 (build 0.8.3); measured 2026-11-09 on the 0.8.1 development build (a windowed X11 run of the reporting user's own workspace set: `omp` = terminal + Reasoning, `git` = lazygit, plus `kb`, `omp2`, `work`): `_switch_workspace` spends 63-75 ms of synchronous GDScript, and the breakdown is `_update_workspace_ui` 34.5-36 ms (5 workspace rows), `_refresh_profile_buttons` 17 ms (2 built-in profiles + the catalog hint) and `_list()` 10.2-10.8 ms (1-2 pane rows), against `_save_workspaces_to_store` 0.3 ms, `_apply_layout` 0.02 ms, `_apply_ui_colors` 0.04 ms and the settings re-apply to the incoming panes 0.1 ms - the panes are not the cost. The cost is control creation: building a row is 0.09 ms, but each Phosphor glyph Label costs 3.3-3.5 ms to enter the tree (an ASCII Label with the same font override costs 0.09 ms, and putting the glyph in `Button.text` - the pre-v0.8.0 shape - costs the same 3.4 ms, so the a11y child Label did not introduce it), and every row carries 1-3 of them: a workspace row (cwd + close) 7 ms, a profile row 7 ms, a pane row (settings/menu/close) 10.4 ms. Switching to the `omp` workspace costs 74-75 ms against 63-65 ms for the single-pane ones, which is its extra Reasoning row. The first switch after launch measured 551 ms once (unexplained outlier; every later switch 63-75 ms). Not newly introduced: the path has rebuilt the lists since workspaces landed (`124d432`, v0.5, `_ws_tabs.update` + `_list()`), and the cost grew with each control a row gained (the workspace cwd button, `aae08f0` 2026-10-21; the profile catalog hint and new-workspace glyph; the pane action glyphs). Fix: do not rebuild a list that did not change - keep the rows, compare a content signature, and move only the state (`_apply_row_accent` for a row, `Sidebar.set_active_pane` already does it for panes), and reuse a row's glyph Label instead of freeing it (re-assigning `Label.text` on an attached Label measured 0.00 ms). Tests: a switch with unchanged lists allocates no rows and its accents still follow the active workspace; a switch after a spawn or close still shows the new pane set. Shipped 2026-11-13 (build 0.8.3): the three `update_*_list` entry points compare `_workspace_signature`/`_profile_signature`/`_pane_signature` (one string per row, fields joined with U+001F) and, when it matches and no close question is open, call `_apply_*_state` - the active accent and the section header - with nothing built or freed; a changed model still rebuilds, and the first draw is forced by `_ws_drawn`/`_profile_drawn`/`_pane_drawn` because an empty model matches an empty signature. Measured with `godot/tests/bench/switch_bench.gd` (two restored workspaces, 5 reps, defaults, 2053x1382): median 0->1 sync_us 43210 -> 12338 and median 1->0 43311 -> 12309, a 71 % cut on the same machine in the same session - the Workspaces and Profiles halves of the switch no longer create a control, and the remaining 12 ms is the incoming Pane list's own rows plus `activate_pane`. That pane half of the signature was replaced in v0.8.3 by the in-place `_sync_pane_rows` (**Creating a pane gets slower as the window fills**), so a switch with an unchanged pane set now touches no row either. The 551 ms first-switch outlier is its own row now (**The first workspace switch after launch takes 551 ms**). |
| ✅ | GUI | **The workspace-switch bench never fills the incoming pane, so it misses focus-time work** - fixed 2026-11-13: the warm-up now settles each workspace's pane while it is on screen (`_settle_pane`: non-empty `visible_text()`, its grid quiet for 8 frames, or filled for 1.5 s when the payload never goes quiet, 20 s deadline), and the measured loop starts from the same workspace afterwards. Every `_switch` records the incoming pane's character count, each rep prints `chars=`, and a switch into an empty pane fails the run - with one exception, the warm-up's first arrival, which is the one switch that may land before the pane has painted (`require_screen=false`). The bench also forces `A11y.force_enabled = 1`: the focus-time half only runs with the app's a11y gate open, and a developer session usually has no screen reader, so the bench would measure a switch with that work switched off (`switch_bench: seeded (a11y forced on)`). The default `QUIET` payload gained `echo quiet` so it has a screen to arrive at. Measured 2026-11-13 at 1900x1000, 3 reps, `DISPLAY=:0`: both panes settle (`chars=2429` for the flood payload, `5` for the quiet one), every rep prints a non-zero `chars=` (2429 / 5), `sync_us` 42.4-46.6 ms for `_switch_workspace` and `frame_us` 4.5-5.5 ms - against the 36.6-37.4 ms the empty-pane bench reported. The ~1 s the reporter saw does not reproduce on this build and is not what is left to measure: its two halves are closed elsewhere in v0.8.3 - the 992 ms was the old Label-based `A11y.publish` shaping the whole screen (**A restored terminal publishes no text to the AT-SPI tree**), and the residual synchronous switch cost is the sidebar rebuild (**The sidebar rebuilds every list on a workspace switch**). `SWITCH_CMD_B=btop` settles at only 24 characters in the sandbox (btop does not reach a full screen there), so the recorded numbers use the bench's own QUIET/FLOOD pair. |
| ✅ | GUI | **The switch bench's `draw_ms` readout is always 0** - fixed 2026-11-14. `_switch` baselines the incoming pane's own sync counter (`_last_grid_gen`, which stands still while the pane is hidden) before the switch and waits, bounded to `SYNC_WAIT_MAX_FRAMES` (120 frames, longer than the flood's 0.4 s interval), for the frame it moves: `first_sync_frame` (1-based; 0 means the pane needed no sync in the window), `first_sync_us` (wall time from the switch) and `fetch_ms`/`draw_ms`/`sync_work_ms` read at that frame, per direction, with a median `first_sync_us`. Measured 2026-11-14 (2053x1382, quiet in A and the flood in B, 5 reps): the flood direction reports `first_sync_frame` 1-2, `first_sync_us` 32.9-43.5 ms, `draw_ms` 5.28-7.92 and `sync_work_ms` 13.3-17.9 (was `draw_ms=0.00` on every rep), while the quiet direction reports `first_sync_frame=0` with `draw_ms=0.00` - a stated fact (nothing to repaint) instead of the old silent zero. |
| ✅ | GUI | **The toast queue never queues** - fixed 2026-11-14: `_enqueue` no longer dismisses the visible toast. It appends and returns while one is up, the toast's own tween callback starts the next, and the queue is bounded at `MAX_QUEUE` = 8 (oldest dropped, newest kept) so a caller in a loop cannot grow it. `_dismiss_current` is gone - its only caller was the preempt. A burst now reads in order: the three restore warnings show one after another, each for its own lifetime, and a refused loop's newest sentences are what survive the cap. Tests: `test_a_second_message_waits_for_the_first`, `test_the_dismissal_starts_the_queued_message`, `test_the_queue_is_bounded_and_keeps_the_newest`. `ui_capture`'s `TOAST_TIMEOUT_MS` is 70 s because a queued drain costs the sum of its messages (eight 8 s errors is the cap), not the longest. |
| ✅ | GUI | **A concept row's provenance tooltip can never show** - fixed 2026-11-13. The row carried the provenance twice: on its `HBox` (a hover target) and on the `Label` inside it, whose tooltip could never show because a `Label`'s default `mouse_filter` is `MOUSE_FILTER_IGNORE`. The provenance now has one owner, the label, which is set to `MOUSE_FILTER_STOP` - so the sentence is reachable over the text a reader is pointing at - and the row builder is its own function (`SettingsPanel._make_concept_row`), which is what makes the contract testable without a terminal. The audit the row asked for found two more unreachable ones and fixed them the same way: the Terminal tab's on-disk size label (its tooltip is the store path) and the concept editor's error label (its tooltip is the full error list); `AgentBadge` is `MOUSE_FILTER_PASS` and was already reachable, and every other `tooltip_text` site is a `Button`, an `OptionButton` or the swatch rows, hover targets by construction. Tests: `test_settings_concepts.gd` (a plugin concept's row carries the provenance and stops the mouse; a rule with no source carries none), `test_settings_panel.gd` (the on-disk label's hover target, and a walk over every label in the panel asserting that one carrying a tooltip never ignores the mouse) and `test_concept_graph_editor.gd` (the error label's). |
| ✅ | GUI | **A scrolled-back terminal offers a jump to the oldest line as well as back to live output** - delivered 2026-11-12: the bottom chevron (v0.8.1) gained its counterpart in the top-right corner, and both moved into one shared widget (`godot/scenes/ui/scroll_hints.gd`). The terminal binds it with `bind_custom`: up while `get_scroll_offset() < get_history_size()`, down while `> 0`, neither at the ends, and the up chevron hides while the search strip covers the pane's top edge. The jump is one `scroll_up(get_history_size())` - the call measured 0.01 µs and the forced full render that follows 79 µs at 102x78 / 68 µs at 120x60 (release `gpty-core`, 200 reps), identical at 179 / 1,179 / 10,000 lines of scrollback because `display_iter` is viewport-bounded - so depth never enters the cost and nothing animates (one frame, no tween). The bespoke `ScrollToBottom` button is deleted. Tests: `test_scrollback_return.gd` (the up chevron reaches the oldest line and hides; the down one still returns to live output; the click stays chrome in a mouse-reporting pane; the up chevron hides with the search strip open). |
| ✅ | GUI | **Every scrolling pane carries the same two scroll chevrons** - delivered 2026-11-12: `ScrollHints` drives a pane's own scroller through one of three bindings - `bind_bar(bar)` for a `ScrollBar` (`ScrollContainer`, `TextEdit`/`CodeEdit`, `RichTextLabel`), `bind_tree(tree)` for a `Tree` (`get_scroll()` up, `get_item_area_rect` of the last visible row down, `scroll_to_item(first/last, false)` to jump), `bind_custom(...)` for the terminal grid - and each chevron shows only while its direction has content, so a body that fits its viewport shows neither. Wired: the code viewer (rebound to the visible body on a source/rendered toggle), the CLI view, the Inspector's answer, the Reasoning turn list, both halves of the wiki note view, and the file tree; media and the wiki link graph are exempt by design. The pair sits clear of the 8 px scrollbar column (14 px right inset) and keeps the terminal's 6 px corner. Tests: `test_scroll_hints.gd` (fits-none, each direction at both ends and mid-range, the two jump actions, a hidden body, tree first/last row, a custom binding, mouse-only/named chrome, the file-tree and code-viewer wirings). |
| ✅ | BUILD | **The sandbox cleanup leaves the caller's shell without `HOME`** - fixed 2026-11-13: `gpty_sandbox_init` records every variable it overrides or unsets (`HOME`, the five XDG paths, `GPTY_TEST_USER_DIR`, `GPTY_SOCKET`, and the secret/prompt list), keeping the "was unset" case, and `gpty_sandbox_cleanup` puts each back instead of unsetting it — so a shell that sandboxed and cleaned up no longer meets `HOME: unbound variable` in the next `set -u` script (measured 2026-11-13: `./scripts/build` died at its export-template check, and the failed build is what left `godot/bin` half-installed). Verified by a round trip that asserts `HOME` and `XDG_DATA_HOME` come back exactly as found, including the unset case. |
