| ✅ | IPC | **The wiki pane's view state had no programmatic face** - the Graph button, opening a note and the three mode buttons were GUI-only: `new-pane` carries no type-specific settings, so a script could create a wiki pane but not point it at a note or open its link graph. Delivered 2026-11-12 as `gpty pane-view <pane> [--graph] [--note <path>] [--mode edit\|split\|preview]`, one verb for the three gestures, applied note then mode then graph so `--note index.md --graph` selects the note the graph's Back returns to. Display-only and focus-free: it never calls `activate_pane`, so an agent can open a graph while a person types in another pane. The answer reads the pane back (`graph`, `note`, `view_mode`) instead of echoing the request, so `--graph` fails rather than reports success when the canvas did not come up; an unsaved buffer, a missing vault and an unbuildable graph are refused (-32000) and a malformed value is -32602, the split the other pane arms keep. Free over MCP (the manifest is generated from clap; `wire.rs` needed one sample row). Tests: the CLI roundtrip plus the pre-socket refusal, six dispatch-contract rows including the unsaved-buffer refusal, and a live smoke step that crosses clap, the registry and the pane. |
| ✅ | WIKI | **Opening the link graph blocks the frame, and `pane-view --graph` inherits it** - fixed 2026-11-14 (build 0.8.3). The query, the model and the layout now run in one `WorkerThreadPool` task: `WikiPane.request_graph` sets `_graph_wanted`, queues `WikiPane._build_graph_document` (the Rust `vault_graph` call, `VaultGraphModel.parse` and `VaultGraphModel.layout` — nothing there touches a node or an autoload, and the weights and the view's positions are copied in on the main thread) and returns; `_poll_graph_build` applies the result on the frame it lands, and `graph_built(ok, reason)` reports it once. A build that is no longer wanted (`_hide_graph`, which every surface that leaves the canvas now calls) or that read a previous vault is dropped, and a wanted graph whose build read the wrong root is rebuilt, so Back cannot be undone by a late result. `VaultGraphView.show_graph` takes the worker's `prepared_positions`, so a drag still survives a re-query, and `_get_layout_state` reports a pending build as the graph the user asked for. The IPC side is the third deferred-answer method: `paneView` moved from `handle` into `dispatch_request` (the one place that holds the request id), and a request naming `graph` is booked in `_pending_graph_answers` and answered from the pane's `graph_built` via the shared `WorkspaceIpcHandlers.view_answer`; the deadline sweep answers 250 ms before the request's own fallback, so a caller reads "still being built" or "left the link graph" instead of a generic timeout. Measured with `godot/tests/bench/graph_bench.gd` (`BENCH_NOTES=600` and `=2000`, links=5): 600 notes/3,000 edges was 382 ms of frame (query 36, parse 28, layout 319) and is now 45 µs of frame with the canvas 399 ms later on the worker; 2,000 notes/10,000 edges was 1,250 ms of frame (query 124, parse 124, layout 1,001) and is now 49 µs with the canvas 1.32 s later; a warm re-query at 2,000 notes is 255 ms of work that no longer touches the frame. Tests: `test_opening_the_graph_returns_before_the_canvas_is_built`, `test_leaving_the_graph_before_it_lands_keeps_the_canvas_down`, `test_a_build_that_lands_after_a_vault_change_is_rebuilt_for_the_new_vault`, `test_a_dragged_node_keeps_its_place_across_a_build`, and in the contract file `test_pane_view_opens_the_graph_then_a_note` (now dispatch + settle), `test_a_graph_request_is_refused_when_the_pane_left_the_graph`, `test_a_graph_request_is_answered_before_its_own_deadline`. Changelog sentence for **The v0.8.3 changelog records the remaining window and pane work**: opening the link graph no longer blocks the window, and `pane-view --graph` answers when the canvas is up. |
| ✅ | IPC | **The control surface cannot set a pane's own settings** - shipped 2026-11-14 (build 0.8.3). Decided as **one generic vocabulary, not per-type factory flags**: `newPane` gained a `settings` object (so a media pane, code viewer or wiki pane is built already pointed at its file or vault) and a new `paneSet` applies the same object to a live pane, both through `PaneTypes.sanitize_settings` at a third source, `SOURCE_IPC` - the table stays the only vocabulary, so a `pane-set` and a saved tile cannot describe the same key differently. The table grew an `ipc: false` marker for the values that are not a caller's to set: the pane's identity (`type`, `attachment_id`), its geometry (`rows`/`cols`), the user's own env (`shell_env`) and Inspector aim (`system_prompt`, `auto_run`, `accept_concept_captures`), and the wiki's restore-only gestures (`open_note`, `graph`, which `pane-view` owns). Those keys refuse over the socket while the pane settings panel keeps `SOURCE_RUNTIME` unchanged, and neither verb writes `PaneEnvStore`/`PaneInspectorStore`/`VaultRegistry` - `pane-set --setting vault_path=…` shows a vault in a pane, it does not register one for the socket's vault verbs. All-or-nothing: any refused key answers `-32602` naming every one and applies nothing; a `vault_path` change over an unsaved note answers `-32000` (the guard `pane-view`'s note swap already had, through a new `PaneBody.would_discard_unsaved` plus the `WikiPane` override); and the answer is the pane read back - `changed` names the tile keys whose value moved, `settings` is the tile document - so a value the pane normalizes is visible instead of a success a caller cannot check. A read-only `paneSettings` publishes the table itself (`kind`, `min`/`max`, `file`, `ipc` per key, projected from `PaneTypes.settings_schema`), which is what makes the vocabulary discoverable without reading source. The CLI spells it `--setting KEY=VALUE` (a value is JSON when it parses, else a string; `commands/settings_args::object_from` is the one parser, shared with the MCP translation, which also accepts the object form), `gpty pane-set`/`pane-settings` never auto-spawn a GUI, and a rename now reaches every pane's title bar (`title_changed` moved from `spawn_pane`'s terminal-only branch to `_attach_pane_into`, the choke point every entry route uses). Tests: `pane_set_roundtrip_carries_the_settings_object`, `pane_settings_asks_for_the_table_with_no_arguments`, `pane_set_without_a_setting_never_reaches_the_socket`, `settings_accept_the_object_and_the_pair_list`, `test_ipc_settings_refuse_the_user_owned_keys`, `test_pane_set_applies_settings_and_reads_them_back` (including the title-bar text), `test_pane_set_refuses_keys_the_table_reserves`, `test_pane_set_refuses_to_discard_an_unsaved_note`, `test_new_pane_carries_settings_into_the_spawn`, `test_pane_settings_reports_the_closed_table`, plus four rows in `test_every_refusal_arm_answers_the_code_its_kind_owns`; the smoke's `pane-set / pane-settings` step crosses CLI → Rust params → GUI registry on a real socket. Two follow-ups found while reading the table are their own rows under v0.8.4 (**A settings value the pane normalizes is accepted as applied**, **A code viewer re-reads its file on every settings edit**). |
| ✅ | GUI | **The pane menu's type list reads as a silent rebuild** - the More menu carried the eight types as a top-level radio list, so a press on another type discarded the pane with no wording that said so. The menu is now three named actions with icons - Minimize/Restore pane (U+E32A minus / U+E0A2 arrows-out), Swap pane (U+E0A0 arrows-left-right, no ellipsis), Switch pane type (U+E464 squares-four) - and Switch opens a second popup listing every type with its Phosphor icon and the current one radio-checked; choosing the current type stays a no-op. Both the workspace rows and the pane menus build items through `MenuItems.add`/`MenuItems.add_radio` (`godot/scenes/ui/menu_items.gd`), one icon+text+tooltip+metadata path. Tests: `test_pane_actions_menu.gd` pins the three-item menu, every icon, the switch list's order/icons/current mark, and both type-press outcomes. |
| ✅ | TERM | **The typed-line repaint test counts paint calls across syncs** - fixed 2026-11-14: the test diffed `_cell_cache` between the settle and the end of the test but asserted against the *last* `_repaint_rows` call, so a typed line whose echo and command output land in two syncs failed - 3 of 3 runs of that file alone on 2026-11-13 (row 0 changed while `_painted_rows` held [1, 2]), and on `windows-smoke` in run 43533705570, which put `main` red with row 3 changed against [4, 6]. It now samples every frame: a sync is a frame whose generation moved, the rows it changed are the diff against the previous sample, and the paint set is `_painted_rows` as that sync left it, so every changed row is judged against the sync that changed it and the four-row budget holds per sync (a full repaint still fails the run). Measured after the fix: 5 of 5 in that file on three consecutive runs, where it used to fail 3 of 3. |
| ✅ | GUI | **The File Tree's first double-click on a folder folds the rows it just listed** - reported 2026-11-14 from daily use: the first activation made the expand arrow appear but left the folder closed, so opening a directory took a second double-click. Measured on the repo's Godot 4.7.2: a new `TreeItem` starts with `collapsed == false` and `Tree` never expands by itself (`item_activated` is only a signal), so `file_tree.gd`'s handler listed the children and then ran its unconditional `item.collapsed = not item.collapsed`, flipping `false` to `true` in the same event; the second activation was the first real expand. Fix: population is the first expand (`_populate`, then `collapsed = false`), and the toggle stays on later activations and still only for a folder with rows. Test: `test_file_tree.gd::test_the_first_activation_expands_a_folder` (first activation lists the row and stays open, second folds). The wiki already stated this rule - `panes/file-tree.md` says a directory is listed "the first time and then expands or collapses it". |
| ✅ | GUI | **The first workspace switch after launch takes 551 ms** - fixed 2026-11-14 (build 0.8.3). The one-shot was never in the switch's GDScript: `_restore_into` attaches every restored workspace's bodies, but `_apply_layout` positions the active workspace's tiles alone, so a never-visited workspace had tiles at rect (0,0), unsized bodies and terminals whose children had never started (`_start_child_if_ready` runs from `_ready`, `_on_resize` and `_process` only, and a hidden grid processes neither of the last two) - the first visit to one laid its tiles out and spawned their programs on its arrival frame. `Workspace._prime_restored_workspaces` now supplies one restored workspace per frame after the launch frame: `_layout_tiles(grid, tm)` (the pass extracted out of `_apply_layout`) gives its tiles the rect they will be drawn at, and `TerminalPane._start_child_if_ready(size_hint)` starts each terminal's child at that rect - the body's own size wins once the container has sorted it, and the `_started` guard keeps the primer idempotent. Measured windowed (2053x1382, X11, debug gdext, two restored workspaces with one pane each, `A11y.force_enabled=1`, three launches before and after, `SWITCH_REPS=3`): the arriving workspace's started panes went 0/1 to 1/1, the arrival frame `frame_us` 29.4 ms (19.8-34.8) to 4.4 ms (4.3-4.4), and switch-to-first-sync `first_sync_us` 49.5 ms (49.0-59.0) to 18.5 ms (18.1-19.4) against a steady 33.4 ms - so the first switch is now 0.53-0.58x a warm one, and `sync_us` (14.2 ms on both sides) says the synchronous switch was never the cost. The 551 ms itself was one observation on the 0.8.1 build over a five-workspace store with `omp`/`lazygit` panes and does not reproduce as an 8x one-shot on today's build. `switch_bench.gd` reports the first switch on its own (`cold … started=n/m`, then a `first_switch … ratio=` line) instead of swallowing it as the warm-up. Consequences: every restored workspace's programs start at launch; a saved pane whose program cannot start warns and closes its tile then rather than on first visit; and closing a never-visited restored workspace now asks the running-work question, because its programs are live. Tests: `test_a_restored_workspace_starts_its_panes_before_it_is_viewed` and `test_priming_does_not_restart_a_running_child`, both of which fail with the primer's call removed. |
| ✅ | AGENT | **The OMP event channel is capped at four events a second, so the Reasoning pane and the agent badge run seconds behind the session** - found 2026-11-14 in daily use and measured against the live event socket: the extension's one-connection-per-event client (`extensions/gpty-omp-events/src/transport.js` `sendOne`) wrote its request, half-closed, and never read the answer, and a socket only reaches `end`/`close` once its readable side has been consumed - so every event sat out the full `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS` (250 ms) and the forwarder pumped 4.0 events/s, on Node 26.13.1 and Bun 1.4.4 alike. Reasoning deltas flush every 16 ms, so a thinking turn outran the channel by ~15x: the 128-slot outbox dropped the oldest chunks and `omp.agent.settled` sat behind the whole backlog, which is why the pane kept printing after OMP had finished and the badge flipped only when it caught up. Fix: `socket.resume?.()` at connect (the answer is discarded; the channel stays fire-and-forget), measured ~4 800 events/s on Node and 5 769/s on Bun. Test: `test/extension.test.js` "takes the answer off the wire so a connection can finish" - a socket double that closes only after its bytes are read, which times out without the fix. A running OMP keeps the module it loaded, so the session must restart (as the README already says for plugin changes). |
| ✅ | DOCS | **The IPC page says event subscribers receive agent state and the OMP event set** - fixed 2026-11-14 (build 0.8.3). `docs/content/wiki/gpty/ipc.md`'s second-socket paragraph now names the three types `GptyTerminal.emit_event` actually fans out - pane spawn, pane kill and concept matches, a notify-only match included - and says `state.declared` and the `omp.*` submissions are consumed by the GUI's drain (Reasoning pane, agent-state badge) and never queued for a subscriber, leaving the fan-out extension to the v0.14 row **Nothing streams workspace, pane and agent state to a consumer**. `docs/content/wiki/gpty/omp-events.md`'s Vocabulary section carries the same clarification where the event names are listed, so a reader of either page gets one answer. Gates: `./scripts/build-wiki --check` (bundle rebuilt, 52 notes) and `python3 scripts/docs-check`. |
| ✅ | DOCS | **The v0.8.3 changelog records the remaining window and pane work** - shipped 2026-11-14 with the release. `docs/content/docs/changelog/v0.8.3.md` is linked from `CHANGELOG.md` and covers every item this row named: the small-window layout (cards clamp, titles ellipsize, the sidebar scrolls, floor 560x340), the foldable Workspaces/Profiles/Panes sections (persisted in `sidebar_folded`), the resize cursor and drag band, the spawn-cost and workspace-switch work, the toast queue (every message in turn, bounded at eight), the two scroll chevrons and the terminal's jump-to-start arrow, the pane-configuration verbs (`new-pane --setting` / `pane-set` / `pane-settings`, one vocabulary, user-owned keys refused, the answer reads the pane back), and the row-granular repaint - plus the pane move, `pane-view`, the Inspector backend dropdown, the first-switch primer and the OMP channel fix the commit range added. The blog entry `docs/content/blog/v0.8.3/index.md` carries four captures: `v0.8.3_1.png` (fresh launch), `_2.png` (Workspaces and Profiles folded), `_3.png` (four panes at 560x340) and `_4.png` (the terminal's two chevrons). The changelog weight ladder was shifted one slot so the new page sorts first (v0.8.2/v0.8.1/v0.8.0 now 20/30/40; unique 10..210 across 21 pages). Gates: `python3 scripts/docs-check`, `./scripts/build-wiki --check` and `./scripts/ci-check`. |

| Done | Area | Item |
|:---:|:---:|---|
| ✅ | GUI | **The Inspector's backend is free text, so a typo fails only at session open** - fixed 2026-11-12: Pane Settings' Backend field is an `OptionButton` built on every panel open from `GptyAi.list_backends()`, and the vocabulary lives in one pure module, `godot/scenes/panes/backend_choice.gd` (`BackendChoice`): it mirrors `BackendKind::parse` (`mock`, `omp`/`oh-my-pi`/`pi`, `cli`, trimmed and case-folded), so a profile that names an alias selects the canonical row and re-saves its kind. A program the machine does not have stays listed and selectable, labelled with a `(not found)` suffix and the install-or-`GPTY_OMP` sentence, and a value the list does not know is kept as `Custom: <value>` instead of being reset. The Command row and its empty-command hint appear only for `cli`; the model placeholder no longer names omp. Tests: `test_backend_choice.gd` (alias normalization, empty and malformed rosters, custom-row retention, missing-program annotation, duplicate and unknown entries) and `test_inspector_backend.gd` (the real extension roster, selection applied and persisted through `_get_layout_state`, an alias selecting the omp row, an unknown value staying custom, a stub-reported missing omp annotated but not disabled, a fresh roster read per open, the cli-only Command row). No capture change: the `pane-settings` state opens a terminal's panel. |
| ✅ | GUI | **A pane's launch command is honoured but no UI can set or see it** - shipped 2026-11-15 (build 0.8.4). Pane Settings' Shell section gained **Launch command** (`LaunchCommand`, one `LineEdit` plus a caption): it reads and writes the tile's `launch` line and nothing else, its placeholder is `Your shell (<cfg shell>)` while empty, and the caption says it runs at this pane's next start and does not change the program already running. Save Profile's per-pane field became a launch line: it is prefilled from `settings.launch`, labelled with the pane's name (or its tile number), writes through `PaneTypes.apply_launch_field` and no longer writes `shell` or `shell_args` - typing `lazygit` in it runs `lazygit` through the shell exactly as the pane does, a non-terminal tile gets no row (the dialog says "geometry only" when none exists), and nothing is written for the other types. A saved launch tile carries the line alone (`_get_layout_state` emits `launch` instead of `shell` + `shell_args`), so clearing the field falls back to the pane's own program. Tests: `test_pane_settings.gd` (`test_launch_command_round_trips_through_the_pane_settings_ui`, `test_clearing_the_launch_command_goes_back_to_the_pane_program`), `test_layout_restore.gd` (`test_save_profile_dialog_keeps_the_launch_line`), and the `new-pane --setting launch` smoke probe, which spawns a two-command line and reads both halves back. |
| ✅ | GUI | **A profile cannot ship a portable launch command: `omp update && omp` needs a shell runner** - shipped 2026-11-15 (build 0.8.4). A terminal tile may carry `launch`: one line, ≤1024 characters, trimmed, no control characters and no U+FFFD (`PaneTypes.sanitize_launch`; rule kind `launch` in `TERMINAL_SETTINGS`, accepted from files, the pane panel and IPC). The expansion is one pure helper, `PaneTypes.spawn_argv`, called at the single `start_shell` site (`TerminalPane._start_child_if_ready`), so restore, spawn, a profile activation and a restored workspace all reach it and no second spawn path exists - `_restore_into` needed no expansion code, and a launch line wins over `command`/`shell_args`. Its plan key is `PaneTypes.LAUNCH_KEY_MARK` (U+FFFD) + the line: U+FFFD is refused by every sanitizer, so no program or argv can forge a launch key, and the runner is deliberately absent because it is the user's configured shell rather than a file's choice (and the install review runs in the CLI before any shell is known) - so the GUI and `plugin.rs::profile_plan_keys` derive the same key. The Rust mirror gained the launch branch, `profile_programs` names a launch line first (then `command`, `settings.shell`, the legacy `shell`) so the install review shows what the record covers, `plugin_manifest::validate_tile` validates `settings.launch`, and `launch_plan_key_marker_matches_the_gui` reads the GUI source through `include_str!` so the marker cannot drift. The Workspace Trust dialog prints `program:` (the runner) and `launch:` (the line). Tests: `test_pane_hardening.gd` (`test_a_launch_line_is_one_trimmed_line`, `test_a_launch_tile_is_untrusted_and_keyed_by_its_line`, `test_spawn_argv_expands_a_launch_line_through_the_configured_shell` - POSIX, `cmd.exe`, `pwsh`, the both-keys winner and the refused line - `test_untrusted_plan_names_the_runner_and_the_launch_line`, `test_a_launch_line_is_a_terminal_setting_a_file_may_ship`), `test_layout_restore.gd::test_a_launch_tile_restores_as_a_portable_line`, `plugin.rs::review_summary_lists_the_plan_keys_each_profile_would_run` (launch keys deduplicated; the same text as a plain argv is a different plan), `review_summary_lists_a_launch_line_as_what_the_profile_starts`, `launch_plan_key_marker_matches_the_gui`, `plugin_manifest::a_launch_line_is_validated_as_one_line`, and the smoke's end-to-end run of a two-command line. `security.md` carries the verbatim rule; the v0.7.1 cwd-trust dependency is closed (`tile_plan_key` already appends the workspace directory), so a builtin may carry a launch line - no builtin does yet (row **gpty-omp ships the OMP-branded workspace** is where `omp update && exec omp` lands). |
| ✅ | GUI | **A pane can choose its own directory, with the workspace directory as the fallback** - shipped 2026-11-15 (build 0.8.4). A pane's own working directory is `user://pane_cwd.json` (`PaneCwdStore`, the `PaneEnvStore` pattern: owner-only atomic writes, typed reads, id-pattern keys, ≤4096 absolute real paths, 256 entries, one bad entry costs itself), keyed `<workspace id>/<attachment id>` — attachment ids are unique across the window, so an entry keyed by the pane id alone would follow a profile from repo X into repo Y. That needed a stable per-workspace id, so `workspaces.json` entries now carry `id` (`PaneTypes.new_workspace_id`: `ws-` plus 12 hex), minted on the first load of a store that lacks one and written straight back, surviving renames and resaves; `sanitize_absolute_dir` (on `BasePersistenceManager`) is now the one directory sanitizer, shared by `WorkspaceStore.sanitize_cwd`, the store and the new `dir_path` settings rule. Resolution is `PaneBody.spawn_cwd()` — the pane's own directory when it exists, else the workspace's, else `""` (gpty's own) — resolved at the moment of the spawn (so a `cli_view` Restart re-checks) with one toast per stale key and the fallback done in GDScript, because the PTY layer drops an unusable cwd silently and that is the layer a stale override must not reach. Pane Settings gained a **Directory** block on terminals and on `cli_view` (picker, **Use workspace directory**, the inherited value named while empty, a caption saying it applies at the next start); `pane_cwd` is in both types' tables as `file: false, ipc: false` and never appears in `_get_layout_state`, so no profile, layout, plugin or `pane-set` caller can supply one. `Workspace`'s `settings_applied` handler is the only writer and moves the entry on a user rename (a collision rename leaves it: the key names a workspace, so the other instance keeps its own); `_close_workspace_now` and `_close_all_workspaces_now` drop a workspace's entries, and `_init_workspaces` retains only the surviving ids. The row's second half is closed too: `CliProcess::spawn` takes `cwd` (refusing a relative or missing one, and setting `PWD` on Unix because `chdir` does not), `GptyCliView.start` parses the optional `"cwd"`, `TerminalManager.spawn_pane` and `_restore_into` hand the workspace directory to any body carrying `workspace_cwd`, and `scripts/record-demo` lost its `git -C "$HOME"` compensation. Tests: `test_pane_cwd_store.gd` (sanitizing, key grammar, load caps, move/drop/retain, resolve order, the stale fallback), `test_pane_cwd.gd` (`test_same_profile_id_in_two_workspaces_keeps_two_directories`, `test_a_pane_rename_carries_the_entry`, `test_closing_a_workspace_drops_its_entries`, `test_a_stale_override_falls_back_to_the_workspace_directory`, `test_a_usable_override_wins_over_the_workspace_directory`, `test_a_profile_tile_cannot_carry_a_directory`, `test_clearing_the_directory_inherits_again`), `test_workspace_store.gd`'s three id tests, `test_cli_view.gd::test_the_child_starts_in_the_panes_directory`, `cli.rs`'s `spawn_starts_the_child_in_the_given_directory`/`spawn_sets_pwd_for_a_direct_program`/`spawn_refuses_a_missing_directory`/`spawn_refuses_a_relative_directory`, and the `pane_cwd` markers in `test_pane_settings_reports_the_closed_table`. Behaviour worth stating: it applies to the next spawn, not the live shell, and a pane's directory sits outside `tile_plan_key` exactly as its env does (user-owned, keyed by a locally minted workspace id, unreachable from a file). Original plan, for the record: each workspace now owns one directory (`workspace_store.gd:78-89` sanitizes it, `workspace.gd:1003-1004` hands it to every terminal, `terminal_manager.gd:63-78` and `terminal_pane.gd:280` carry it to `pty.rs:421-423`), and that is exactly the wrong granularity when one profile is activated into two repositories: the pane that should start in the second repo has no say. Fix: a user-owned per-pane directory in a new `user://pane_cwd.json` (the `PaneEnvStore` pattern - `BasePersistenceManager`, owner-only, absolute-only and ≤4096, id-pattern key, empty means inherit, written only by Pane Settings), resolved in GDScript as pane override, then workspace directory, then gpty's own directory. It must NOT be a tile field: the v0.7.1 cwd trust row's acceptance is that a profile or plugin tile still cannot carry a cwd, and the v0.9.5 project-workspaces row states the directory is user-originated, never a profile or plugin field. The key cannot be `attachment_id` alone: ids are unique across all workspaces (`workspace.gd:576-585`) and a builtin such as `agent-terminal` reappears in every workspace that activates the profile, so an override pinned to repo X in one workspace would silently follow the profile into repo Y and defeat the workspace setting. Key it `<workspace_id>/<attachment_id>`, which needs a stable per-workspace id in `workspace_store.gd:48-52` (entries are positional today and renames are allowed), generated on first load and backfilled on migration. Resolve the path in GDScript, not in Rust: `crates/gpty-gdext/src/lib.rs:250-269` drops an unusable cwd and the child then inherits gpty's own directory, so a stale override would land in the wrong layer - instead warn once and fall back to the workspace directory, and apply the same existence check to a restored workspace path, which today is only validated when the picker sets it (`workspace.gd:848-856`) and silently degrades after a repo move. UI: a Directory row in Pane Settings with the existing directory picker (`FILE_MODE_OPEN_DIR`, `workspace.gd:818-843`) and a `Use workspace directory` action that clears the entry, showing the inherited value when empty; the debounce/gather pattern is `terminal_pane.gd:1373-1393`. Migrate the entry on an `attachment_id` rename the way env already does (`workspace.gd:59-63`), cap the store, and drop entries for deleted workspaces. It applies to the next spawn, not the live shell, and only terminal panes receive a cwd today (`workspace.gd:1003`), so decide explicitly whether `cli_view` follows. Tests: store sanitizing (relative, oversized, control characters, the cap); the same profile id in two workspaces keeps two directories; a rename carries the entry; a stale override warns and starts in the workspace directory, not gpty's; a tile that carries `cwd` is still ignored. - and the same resolution must reach `cli_view`: a cli_view child runs in the GUI process's cwd (Godot's project directory), because `CliProcess::spawn` (`crates/gpty-core/src/cli.rs`) takes no cwd while terminals go through `PtyHandle::spawn_with_cwd` (`pty.rs:400`). Found 2026-11-06 while recording `demo-cli-view`: `gpty new-pane -t cli_view -c git --arg log` in the sandbox listed gPTY's own history instead of the sandbox repo's four commits, while the same `git log` in a terminal pane showed the sandbox. The clip now compensates with `git -C "$HOME"`; when this row ships, a cli_view pane prints the pane override and then the workspace directory (a test runs `pwd` through it) and that `-C` comes out of `scripts/record-demo`. |

| ✅ | CORE | **The PTY input queue is unbounded, so a pane whose child stops reading grows the process** - fixed 2026-11-15 (build 0.8.4). Two bounded stages replace the unbounded ones: `STDIN_QUEUE_CAP` (256 messages) in `crates/gpty-core/src/engine.rs` and `WRITER_QUEUE_CHUNKS` (16 chunks) in `crates/gpty-core/src/pty.rs`. `PtyHandle::send` answers `WouldBlock` instead of blocking a tokio worker, and the task retries with a 1 ms async sleep, so a pane close can still abort it and drop the handle that kills the child; `GptyTerminal.send_text`/`send_line` now return `bool`, `TerminalPane._send_*` propagate it, `inject` answers a `refused` error when the queue is full and `broadcast` reports a `refused` count. Test `a_child_that_stops_reading_refuses_input_instead_of_growing_the_queue`: a `stty raw` child that never reads refuses the 64 KiB injects within the 288-call loop and accepts at most 256 + 16 + 2; it passed in 0.07 s. The unbounded build accepted all 288 sends before any refusal. |

| ✅ | GUI | **A profile's declared grid unit is dropped on load** - found 2026-11-12 while closing the v0.8.3 row **The workspace store drops the grid unit it is handed**: `workspace.gd` read `profile.get("grid_unit", 0)` before `scale_layout`, but no producer set the field, so the read was dead code and a profile authored in a coarser grid relied on `scale_layout`'s inference (correct only while the tiles touch the grid's far edge). Fixed 2026-11-15 (build 0.8.4): every producer writes the unit through `PaneTypes.sanitize_grid_unit` - `ProfileManager.add_profile`/`update_profile` stamp `PaneTypes.GRID` (their tiles come from live tiles, so a save cannot be in another unit; the Save Profile dialog and `profileSave` inherit the stamp), `_load_defaults` copies a declared unit and stamps `GRID` on a file that declares none (the shipped file is this build's app content), `load_profiles` normalizes a hand-edited store (12 and 60 survive, anything else becomes 0 = infer), and `_derive_plugin_profiles` copies the plugin record's unit. The one consumer, `Workspace._activate_now`, reads through the same sanitizer. The plugin chain carries it end to end: `[[profiles]]` may declare `grid_unit` (12 or 60; geometry is validated against it and `cspan`/`rspan` default to it, anything else is refused by name), `PluginProfileRecord` stores it (`Option<i64>`, absent for a record written before the field existed), `profile_records` writes the manifest's unit, and `profiles_json` forwards it - a plugin declaring one must set `min_gpty_version` to `0.8.4`, because an older GUI ignores the field and infers. Tests: `test_profile_grid_unit_round_trips_and_unknown_units_are_dropped` (all four producers + the four store shapes), `test_a_profile_written_in_a_coarser_unit_activates_the_same_shares` (installed-plugin JSON -> `find_profile` -> activation; a quarter of a 12-unit grid stays a quarter), `a_profile_declares_the_grid_unit_its_tiles_are_written_in`, `profile_grid_unit_round_trips_and_a_non_integer_is_rejected`, `profiles_json_forwards_a_declared_grid_unit`; measured on the running app in a sandbox, `gpty profile save` wrote `"grid_unit": 60` (the seeded defaults too) and a hand-written 12-unit profile loaded both of its panes over `profileLoad`. Docs: `agents/plugins.md`, wiki `plugins.md` and `profile.md`. |
| ✅ | GUI | **A settings value the pane normalizes is accepted as applied** - fixed 2026-11-15 (build 0.8.4). A rule may carry `values`, a closed set checked in `PaneTypes._value_for_rule` after the rule's length check, so an unknown member is refused at every source: `pane-set`/`new-pane --setting` answer `-32602` naming the key, a saved tile drops it and `Workspace._restore_into` reports it once, and the pane keeps the value it had. `paneSettings` publishes `values` beside `kind`/`file`/`ipc`. The vocabularies moved to one owner in `PaneTypes` - `CODE_VIEWER_LANGUAGES`, `CODE_VIEWER_VIEW_MODES`, `FILE_TREE_OPEN_MODES`/`FILE_TREE_DEFAULT_OPEN_MODE`, `WIKI_VIEW_MODES` (also `paneView`'s `mode`) - and the wiki, code-viewer, file-tree and Inspector apply-time re-derivations are deleted. `inspector.backend` is the one canonicalizing rule, `kind: "backend"`: `BackendChoice.selected_kind` runs in the table, `BackendChoice.KINDS`/`ALIASES` are data (the list `pane-settings` publishes), an alias folds to its kind and an unknown name is kept verbatim so the shipped, tested `Custom:` row still shows it - closing that key to a set would have reset a user's file instead. Measured 2026-11-15 on a sandboxed headless build: `pane-set <code_viewer> --setting view_mode=bogus --json` answers `-32602` with "cannot be applied to this pane: view_mode (an unknown key, a value of the wrong type or outside a closed set, ...)" and exits 1, `--setting view_mode=source` answers `changed: ["view_mode"]` with `settings.view_mode: "source"` and exits 0, and `pane-settings` prints `wiki.view_mode = {kind: text, max: 32, values: [edit, split, preview]}` and `inspector.backend = {kind: backend, max: 32, values: [mock, omp, cli]}`. Tests: `test_a_closed_set_refuses_a_member_it_does_not_know`, `test_a_saved_tile_names_a_refused_closed_set_member`, `test_a_backend_alias_folds_and_an_unknown_name_is_kept`, `test_a_closed_set_member_never_reaches_the_pane`, `test_pane_set_refuses_a_member_outside_a_closed_set`, and the live smoke's `pane-settings`/`pane-set` steps. Screenshot: none - the dropdowns render the same, only a value that used to be silently ignored is now refused. |
| ✅ | PLUGIN | **A plugin store written by this build is unreadable to an older CLI** - fixed 2026-11-16 (build 0.8.4): the reader's forward-compatibility policy lives in `crates/gpty-plugin/src/store.rs` and is one rule at every level - an unknown *sibling* key is ignored (file root, record, profile entry, concept entry) while a required field, a type, a shape, a cap and the id/revision formats are still refused, so a renamed or removed field still fails through its required-field check and tolerance covers additive drift only. The writer still refuses an unknown concept key: the closed set (`CONCEPT_KEYS`, the manifest's vocabulary) moved out of the shared shape check into `validate_concepts`, so the store never emits what the validator would not admit, while the reader keeps an additive key the engine ignores (`gpty_core::concept::concepts_from_json` reads the keys it knows, and a legacy `cmd` is explicitly data). A rewrite converges the file to this build's keys: profile entries are re-serialized from `PluginProfileRecord`, so a newer profile field is dropped (the GUI then re-infers, as it does for an absent `grid_unit`), while concept entries travel as raw JSON and keep theirs. A per-record shape failure is the new `StoreError::mismatched` - the file, the failing field, `different gpty release`, `run the CLI and the GUI from the same release` - and `is corrupt` is now reserved for JSON syntax, a missing `plugins` array, an IO error and the 256-record cap; the `plugin` surface's stale-binary probe still warns first. Tests: `additive_keys_read_and_a_rewrite_keeps_only_this_builds_keys`, `an_unknown_concept_key_reads_but_is_never_written` (the write path still refuses `unknown key`), `a_record_that_does_not_match_names_the_version_skew`, and the two shape tables lost their `unknown-key` cases. Measured in a sandbox (`XDG_STATE_HOME`/`XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`/`GPTY_SOCKET` under one temp dir, 2026-11-16 build): a store carrying `future_record_key`, a profile `color` and a concept `future` lists as `enabled owner/x @aaaaaaa`; `plugin disable owner/x` succeeds and the rewritten file holds exactly the six record keys, `name`/`grid_unit`/`tiles`, and the concept's `future`; a record missing `enabled` prints the skew sentence naming `record 0` and exits 1, with no `is corrupt` in it. |
| ✅ | IPC | **`paneSet` answers `changed: []` when a setting is cleared** - found 2026-11-15 while adding the launch field: `ipc_handlers.gd`'s `paneSet` derived `changed` by walking `pset_after` only, so a key that was in `pset_before` and gone from the read-back was never named - `pane-set <pane> --setting launch=` (an empty value is the documented clear - `settings_args.rs`) answered success with `changed: []` while the tile lost its launch line, and setting one dropped `shell`/`shell_args` from the read-back without saying so. Fixed 2026-11-15: the arm walks the union of `pset_before` and `pset_after` and names every key present in one and absent or different in the other, in before-then-after order. Measured on the running app in a sandbox: `pane-set @pane --setting launch=lazygit` answered `changed: ['shell', 'shell_args', 'launch']` with the read-back holding only `launch`, and `--setting launch=` answered `changed: ['launch', 'shell', 'shell_args']` with the read-back holding `shell`/`shell_args` again. Test `test_pane_set_names_a_key_the_read_back_dropped`; `docs/content/wiki/gpty/cli.md`'s `pane-set` row now says `changed` names every key that changed or left the read-back. |
| ✅ | GUI | **A code viewer re-reads its file on every settings edit** - found 2026-11-14 while wiring pane settings over the API: `CodeViewerPane.apply_settings` called `load_file(settings["file_path"])` whenever the key was present, and the pane settings panel's gather always includes it, so editing the pane's name or font size re-read the document and reset the read-only editor's scroll and caret (`MediaPane` already guarded exactly this). Fixed 2026-11-15: the viewer takes `previous_path := file_path` before `super.apply_settings` and reloads only when the path moved - an unchanged path just re-renders the view, so a font or name edit repaints without touching the document. Test `test_a_name_edit_keeps_the_viewers_scroll` (200-line temp file, scrolled to line 10, a `pane_name` edit leaves the scroll and the text alone, and a genuinely moved `file_path` still loads the new file so the guard is not "never reload"). No headless-observable live surface for scroll position: the test drives the real pane and the panel's gathered dictionary. |
| ✅ | PLUGIN | **A plugin cannot declare the four newest event types** - `plugin_manifest::EVENT_TYPES` listed nine names while the event socket's generic vocabulary (`omp_events.rs`) has ten: `session.shutdown`, `agent.settled`, `turn.finished` and `tool.finished` were absent, so a manifest subscribing to one was refused as unknown and `docs/content/docs/agents/plugins.md`'s claim that the validator's list is the socket vocabulary was false. Fixed 2026-11-15 by removing the second copy rather than adding four names to it: the generic vocabulary is `gpty_core::events::GENERIC_EVENT_NAMES` (one array, with a duplicate-name test), the extension's wire table is `WIRE_TO_GENERIC` and a gdext test pins its generic side against the shared list name-for-name and in order, and the validator's `event_types()` is the three native names (`NATIVE_EVENT_TYPES` - the GUI's own emitters) plus every shared name, so an event the socket emits is one a manifest can subscribe to by construction. The refusal message no longer points at the removed const. Measured with the built CLI: `type = "session.shutdown"` and `type = "tool.finished"` validate (exit 0), `type = "pane.gone"` still exits 1 naming it. Tests: `event_types_are_the_socket_vocabulary` (all thirteen parse, and the count is native + generic), `the_translation_covers_the_shared_vocabulary`, `the_vocabulary_names_each_event_once`; `crates/gpty-core/README.md`'s module table carries the new module. |
| ✅ | GUI | **A per-pane store is rewritten on every settings keystroke** - found 2026-11-15 while adding the pane-directory store: the pane settings panel re-applies its gathered dictionary on every 150 ms debounce tick, and `PaneEnvStore.set_env` wrote the 0600 store each time even when the value was unchanged, so editing a pane's *name* or font size rewrote `user://pane_env.json` at typing speed (`PaneCwdStore.set_dir` already compared first). Fixed 2026-11-15: `set_env` returns before the write when the stored value already equals the cleaned one (`remove_env` already returned when the id was absent), so there is one write per actual change. Test `test_setting_the_same_value_writes_nothing` counts `_write_file` calls through `MockAutoloads.write_count` (the bytes are identical either way, so content cannot prove it): a real change writes once, the same value however padded writes nothing, a changed value writes again, and removing or clearing an absent entry writes nothing. `MockAutoloads` now counts every mocked `_write_file`, so the other stores' write discipline can be asserted the same way. No user-visible failure was involved - no data loss, the write is atomic, and the file is small. |

| ✅ | BUILD | **The defect-class check is a manual release step, so a release can ship an undeclared fix** - found 2026-11-15 while wiring the check into the release; fixed in the same session: `.github/workflows/release.yml`'s `export-release` checkout fetches full history (`fetch-depth: 0`) and runs `./scripts/fix-forensics --check --since "$(git describe --tags --abbrev=0 --match 'v*' HEAD^)"` before the export, falling back to `--check` on the first release. A failure stops the export job before any release archive is created, and the previous tag is computed, so every `v*` tag runs the right range with no version to maintain. |

Measured before in a sandbox: `pwd` in a terminal answered `/home/neilp`, a `cli_view` answered the project directory `/mnt/App/Projects/godot-pty/gpty/godot`; after, both answer the home directory. | ✅ | GUI | **A scheme with fewer than 16 colours inherits the rest of the previous one** - fixed 2026-11-16 (build 0.8.4). `gpty_core::color::palette_from_csv` answers a complete 16-colour array - every valid CSV entry fills its own slot and every other slot is `SYSTEM_COLORS` - and `GptyTerminal.set_palette` assigns that whole array, bumping the generation only when it differs. A short or malformed scheme can no longer paint indices 4-15 with the colours of the scheme before it, and re-applying the current scheme costs no repaint. Tests (gpty-core): `a_short_scheme_keeps_no_colour_from_the_one_before_it`, `a_malformed_entry_keeps_the_default_in_its_slot`, `entries_after_the_sixteenth_are_ignored`. |

## v0.8.5 - Release Repair

The patch v0.8.4 made necessary. Its tag could not be pushed at all: the `tag` ruleset carried a `creation` rule with no bypass actors, and `scripts/check-github-settings` asserted the same wrong rule set, so the gate reported OK while the policy it guards made every release impossible. Publishing then shipped a macOS archive whose FFmpeg alias fix never reached the transport. Both are release-pipeline work, the only kind of change this release carries.

| Done | Area | Item |
|:---:|:---:|---|
| ✅ | BUILD | **The tag ruleset forbade creating a tag, so no release could be tagged** - found and fixed 2026-11-16 while cutting v0.8.4: pushing `v0.8.4` was rejected with "Cannot create ref due to creations being restricted". The `tag` ruleset (26369813) carried the `creation` rule with `bypass_actors: []`, and `creation` restricts ref creation to bypass actors - with none, nobody can create a tag, the admin included; `scripts/check-github-settings` asserted the wrong set in the other direction (`TAG_RULES` *required* `creation`), so the gate passed while the policy it guards made every release impossible. Fixed in the same session: the ruleset keeps `deletion`/`non_fast_forward`/`update`/`required_signatures` - a tag must be signed and cannot be moved or deleted - and drops `creation`, and `TAG_RULES` matches it, so the exact-set comparison catches the drift in either direction. Verified: `./scripts/check-github-settings` prints OK over all 30 checks and the `v0.8.4` tag pushes. |
| ✅ | BUILD | **The macOS archive still carries three real copies of every FFmpeg library** - found 2026-11-16 verifying the published v0.8.4 asset: `zipinfo` shows `libavcodec.dylib`, `libavcodec.63.dylib` and `libavcodec.63.1.102.dylib` as three regular ~9.29 MB files, and the sizes differ (9,294,432 vs 9,294,384), so they are not even dereferenced copies of one file. `macos_libs` skips symlinked source names, but FFmpeg ships the unversioned name as a real file and lipos it into a second canonical, and `actions/upload-artifact` dereferences the `.63` alias on the way to `macos_zip`. Fixed in code the same session, not yet proven: `scripts/bundle-media` gained `normalize_aliases`, which derives `<name>.<major>.dylib` and `<name>.dylib` as symlinks from the canonical `<name>.<major>.<minor>.<patch>.dylib`, replaces a real file sitting at an alias name, and runs both at the end of `macos_libs` and in `macos_zip` after the transported tree is copied into the bundle; the lipo loop now skips the unversioned name so it never becomes a second canonical. Verified offline on a synthetic flattened tree - one real file, two symlinks, `libgpty_gdext.*` untouched - and `bash -n` is clean. The proof is this release's macOS archive showing one real file and two symlinks, now measured: **closed 2026-11-17 on the published `v0.8.5` archive** - `zipinfo -l gpty-v0.8.5-macos.zip` shows `libavcodec.63.1.102.dylib` as the only real file with `libavcodec.63.dylib` and `libavcodec.dylib` as `lrwxrwxrwx` (stored, 25 bytes), and all five libraries follow the shape (5 real files, 10 links, where v0.8.4 had 15 real); the zip shrank 96,084,344 → 83,989,869 bytes, and `scripts/check-macos-minos` still reports 11.0 on every arm64 slice and 10.13 on 15 of 16 x86_64 (the extension itself is 10.12). |

## v0.8.6 - Godot Visual Identity & Close-out

The differentiator release: the interface stops looking like a generic Godot app. Every effect is a setting, subtle or off by default, and display-only.

| Done | Area | Item |
|:---:|:---:|---|
| ✅ | CORE | **The unbounded-queue control asserted the kernel's appetite, not the queue cap** - found 2026-11-21 (build 0.8.6) when the pre-push gate failed twice and 2 of 8 serial suite runs failed on `a_child_that_stops_reading_refuses_input_instead_of_growing_the_queue` with `accepted 288 calls` (once above the writer-queue bound). Cause (`gate-lies`): the send loop yielded between calls, so the terminal task handed chunks to the writer thread, and a raw-mode Linux master write absorbs megabytes into the tty flip buffers before it blocks - the instrumented run showed only `READY` in the grid and no write or backpressure error, i.e. all 288 x 64 KiB were swallowed by the kernel (a fresh probe blocks after ~11 KiB, so saturation depends on the flip-buffer state the earlier output left behind). A cumulative accepted count therefore measured kernel appetite while the queue never filled. The burst is now sent without yielding, so nothing drains the ingress between calls and `STDIN_QUEUE_CAP` is what refuses; the assertion is the cap itself. 20 serial suite runs and the parallel pass are green. |
| ✅ | CORE | **The motion tests sampled tweens by wall clock and read a freed ghost** - found 2026-11-21 by windows-smoke run 44919008575 (build 0.8.6): `test_a_slide_close_exits_toward_the_split_edge` awaited 150 ms against a 200 ms slide; on the runner the timer overshot the tween, the ghost was freed, the `is_instance_valid` assert recorded a failure and the next line read `w.position` - `Invalid access to property ... 'previously freed'` as a script error. Cause (`gate-lies`): a fixed wait assumes the frame budget - one stalled frame expires the timer and the tween together - and GUT continues after a failed assert. The transition tests now poll `PaneTransitions.playing()` to the end state, sample the slide ghost and the crossfade's outbound half frame by frame while the instance is alive (the unchanged rects distinguish out from back), and wait for the ghost's own free because `_finish` clears the entry a frame before `queue_free` lands. GUT: 127 scripts, 1613 tests, all passed. |
| ✅ | GUI | **A pane's font-size setting does not reach text already on screen** - closed 2026-11-17: `PaneBody.font_size`'s setter now calls `_recompute_cell_metrics()` for **every** pane type (a repeated value is a no-op, so the debounced panel does not re-measure per keystroke), and the hook's contract is that an override re-applies the size to every view the type owns, invalidates any height measured at the old font, and tolerates null widgets (a spawn applies settings before `_ready`). It reaches the Inspector's answer and thinking view, the CLI View's output, the code viewer's editor, rendered Markdown and empty state, the file tree's `Tree` (which had never received the setting at all) and empty state, the media pane's placeholder and status, and the wiki editor and preview. `PaneBody.empty_state_font(title, hint, font_px)` is now the one owner of the empty state's title/body offset, and the Reasoning and Wiki `apply_settings` workarounds are deleted (the base drives both). The wiki note list, the graph labels and the media transport's time label stay on the theme/chrome size by decision: index, diagram and chrome, not the pane's text. Tests: `test_a_font_size_change_recomputes_and_a_repeated_value_does_not` (the hook reaches a pane with no `_font`; a repeated size does not re-measure), `test_a_font_size_change_reaches_an_inspector_answer` (theme size and a grown content height), the per-pane `test_a_font_size_change_reaches_…` tests for a rendered file, streamed output, the file tree, the media placeholder and an open note, and `test_a_font_size_applied_before_the_terminal_is_ready_lands_with_its_font` for the spawn-order guard. |
| ✅ | BUILD | **The test harness recursively deletes the working tree when a fixture path is empty** - fixed 2026-11-19: extracted `TestPaths.is_safe_user_path` and `TestPaths.remove_tree` in `godot/tests/helpers/test_paths.gd`, which strictly validates that target paths are real absolute paths inside `TestPaths.user_dir()` and refuses empty strings, whitespace, relative paths (`.`, `..`), virtual schemes (`res://`, `user://`), filesystem roots (`/`, drive roots), and the `TestPaths.user_dir()` root itself. Replaced all seven unsafe local `_remove_tree` copies (`test_wiki_pane.gd`, `test_file_tree.gd`, `test_media_files.gd`, `test_pane_file_sinks.gd`, `test_media_pane.gd`, `test_pane_open_request.gd`, `test_scroll_hints.gd`). | ✅ | CORE | **Executable validation rejects system binaries in user namespaces and containers** - closed 2026-11-19. Cause (`platform-parity`): three copies of the owner check assumed host UID semantics, so a spawn refused an owner the kernel reports as `/proc/sys/fs/overflowuid` (65534) while `geteuid()` is the user. One rule now answers "may this file run": `pty::validate_executable_file` - absolute, regular, not group/other-writable, owned by this user, root, or the overflow UID **only where that UID is outside `/proc/self/uid_map`**, so a host with the identity map `0 0 4294967295` accepts nothing new and an unreadable `/proc` fails closed - and `pty::validate_untrusted_executable` adds the no-writable-ancestor walk for a path saved tile/profile data chose. `transport::validate_gui_binary` and `resolve_omp_binary` now call the file rule instead of their own copies, which also closes the package-manager case those copies refused (a root-owned `/usr/bin/gpty-gui` or `/usr/bin/omp`); the user-chosen `GPTY_GUI`/`GPTY_OMP` keep only the file rule because macOS's `/Applications` is group-writable and must not be refused. Tests: `pty::tests::executable_owner_rule_admits_this_user_root_and_only_the_unmapped_overflow_uid`, `pty::tests::a_uid_map_is_read_as_ranges_and_an_unreadable_one_is_not_evidence`, `transport::tests::socket_validation::gui_binary_in_a_group_writable_directory_is_the_users_choice`, `binary::tests::the_file_rule_is_shared_with_the_pane_spawn_gate`, and `crates/gpty-core/tests/userns_executable.rs`, which re-execs itself under `unshare -Ur` and prints the proof it asserts (`user namespace: euid 0, /usr/bin/sh owned by uid 65534`). Measured: before, `unshare -Ur cargo test -p gpty-core --lib pty::tests::validate_executable_refuses_paths_another_user_controls -- --exact` failed on `validate_executable("/bin/sh")`, and `unshare -Ur ./scripts/gut-check` aborted at its first terminal fixture with `Cannot start the pane's shell: executable belongs to another user (uid 65534): /bin/bash`, which gut-check reports as a run it will not judge; after, that `cargo test` passes, all 21 `pty::tests` pass inside the namespace (PTY spawn tests included), and `unshare -Ur ./scripts/gut-check` runs the whole GUT suite there - 122 scripts, 1552 tests, 1552 passing, `All tests passed!`, exit 0, where the same command previously aborted at its first terminal fixture. `Cause: platform-parity` - the class's control now includes the self-hosting namespace test. |
| ✅ | CORE | **A Windows absolute program path skipped executable validation** - found 2026-11-19 while unifying the owner rule: `pty::validate_executable` waved through any program without a `/`, so `C:\Tools\evil.exe` from a saved tile/profile was never stat'ed - `validate_program`'s separator test knew about `\` and then handed the path to the function that did not. Fixed in the same commit: the separator decision is `path_like(program, windows)`, used by `validate_program` and by `validate_executable_for`, whose platform argument lets `a_windows_absolute_program_is_not_treated_as_a_bare_name` assert the Microsoft spelling on every platform (`path_like`'s own cases pin that a backslash stays an ordinary name byte on Unix). Limit: not exercised on a real Windows host this session - `rust-windows` compiles the test (`cargo check --workspace --tests`) and it runs on any Windows `cargo test -p gpty-core`. |
| ✅ | GUI | **TextRead uncapped buffer read on dynamic files and concurrent writes** - closed 2026-11-20. Cause (`unbounded`): `TextRead.read_prefix` called `FileAccess.get_size(path)` to decide `truncated := size > cap`, falling back to `f.get_as_text()` when `size <= cap`. On dynamic files with 0 stat size (e.g. `/proc/version`) or files concurrently appended to after `get_size`, the read was uncapped on the GUI thread and reported 0 size. Fixed in `godot/scenes/panes/text_read.gd`: the read is always bounded to `cap + 1` bytes via `f.get_buffer(safe_cap + 1)` and decoded with `_decode_utf8_prefix`, completely eliminating `f.get_as_text()`. `f.close()` is called immediately after the buffer read, `truncated := raw.size() > safe_cap`, and `reported_size := maxi(size, raw.size())` so dynamic and growing files report actual read length. Tests: 10 unit tests in `godot/tests/unit/test_text_read.gd` verifying refusal of non-absolute/virtual/missing/directory paths, exact cap and cap + 1 reads, 2 MiB file capped to exactly 1 MiB without `get_as_text`, clean UTF-8 slicing on torn multi-byte sequences at the cap, bounded `/proc/version` reads with non-zero size, and static assertion that `text_read.gd` contains no `get_as_text()` call. All existing tests in `test_pane_file_sinks.gd`, `test_code_viewer_markdown.gd`, `test_viewable_files.gd`, and the 123-script / 1562-test GUT suite pass. |
| ✅ | BUILD | **The Windows GUT gate failed because the fixture helper refused every path, not because of the runner image** - found 2026-11-20 by CI run `44648884305` (attempts 1 and 2) on `1d6f2e5`: `windows-smoke`'s GUT step fails in `test_file_tree.gd` (`test_an_empty_folder_keeps_its_own_sentence` expects `This folder is empty` and reads `""`, lines 81-82; then `[2] expected to equal [1]` and `["a.txt"] expected to equal ["sub"]`), the note save/preview tests (`the save must reach the file`), and `TestPaths.remove_tree` (`remove_tree must succeed on fixture inside user_dir`, `fixture directory must no longer exist after removal`); the suite then aborts with `SCRIPT ERROR: Out of bounds get index '0' (on base: 'Array')`, which `gut-check` reports as an untrustworthy run. Two variables moved since the last green CI (`44541260540`, `80eebf6`, image 20261103.629): the runner image is now 20261111.635, and `f8d4419` added `godot/tests/helpers/test_paths.gd` with the `TestPaths.user_path`/`remove_tree` fixtures `test_file_tree` and the note tests now use. The image was not the cause: `f8d4419` landed *after* the last green Windows run, so the helper had never run on Windows, and the `:188` line in that log is the GDScript backtrace of the expected `push_warning` from `test_a_failed_write_leaves_the_previous_file_intact`, not a failure (the script tallied 7/13 with the file-mode tests pending). Cause (`platform-parity`): `TestPaths.is_safe_user_path` compared `_normalize_path(child)` with `_normalize_path(root)`; on Windows the root arrives from `GPTY_TEST_USER_DIR` as `C:\...\user` (no `/` after the drive) and `path_join` appends `/`, and `String.simplify_path()` keeps the drive separator exactly as written - the root normalised to `C:/...` and every child to `C:\...`, so the prefix compare answered false for every fixture, `remove_tree` deleted nothing, and each fixed-name fixture inherited every earlier test's files (which is why `test_an_empty_folder_keeps_its_own_sentence` read a leftover `note.txt` and `test_wiki_pane` read `alpha.md` where it wrote `note.md`). Fixed 2026-11-20: `TestPaths.normalize_path` unifies the separator (`\` to `/` on Windows) and no longer trims a real path's bytes before `simplify_path`, and `is_safe_path_under(path, root, windows)` is the one bounds rule `is_safe_user_path` and `remove_tree` share; `TestPaths.fresh_dir` deletes a leftover fixture before creating it, and twelve files' fixed-name fixtures use it (`test_file_tree`, `test_wiki_pane`, `test_media_files`, `test_media_pane`, `test_pane_open_request`, `test_pane_file_sinks`, `test_code_viewer_empty_state`, `test_vault_registry`, `test_link_weights`, `test_gdext_ffi`, `test_layout_restore`, `test_title_bar_keyboard`); every test function that indexes a confirmation dialog now asserts the count and returns before indexing (37 functions, 44 reads in `test_wiki_pane`, `test_workspace_cwd_trust`, `test_pane_file_sinks`, `test_palette`), because three `dialogs[0]` reads after a failed count assert were the `SCRIPT ERROR`s (`test_wiki_pane.gd:529/553/574`); and `gut-check` prints Godot's `at:`/backtrace lines with the error it refuses to judge. Tests: `test_a_windows_drive_root_accepts_its_children` (the Windows spelling pinned on Linux - it answered false before the change) and `test_a_posix_root_compares_case_sensitively`. Measured: the same refusal reproduced on Linux by handing the suite a root with a trailing space (`GPTY_TEST_USER_DIR="/tmp/gpty-dirty/user "`), which the old `strip_edges()` compared away - 24 failing tests and three `SCRIPT ERROR`s before, 105/105 for that script after; the full Linux suite passes. `windows-smoke` green on this change (run `44745805901`, job `136320671858`: 123 scripts / 1564 tests, 1545 passed and 19 pending on Windows 2025 - the documented platform gaps, none failed). |
| ✅ | BUILD | **A test that indexes a list a failed precondition never filled still aborts the gate** - fixed 2026-11-20, both halves measured on this tree. (1) *The gate judges what it can*: `scripts/gut-check` treated every `SCRIPT ERROR` as an abort, so one `Out of bounds get index '0'` ended Godot and the verdict was "not trustworthy" with the rest of the suite unrun. It now aborts only when a script/parse error appears **before the first `* <test>` line** - the collector/autoload failure, still 2.3 s from launch to a named FAIL on a full-suite parse error, with the `at:` line - and once a test has started the error is GUT's to attribute: the run finishes, and the verdict splits into "not trustworthy" (a killed run, no Run Summary, a collection failure, a count that does not add up) and "the suite ran and reported failures", which prints GUT's own failing-test block (the region between `= Run Summary` and `Totals`, capped at 80 lines) and the first five `at:` locations. A 64 MiB log cap bounds a repeating error now that the early kill no longer does. Measured with `_dialogs_of` mutated to `[]`: before the guards, 105 tests ran and 31 failed with 4 located `SCRIPT ERROR`s, where the old gate killed at the first; after the guards, the same 31 fail with their own assertion text and zero `SCRIPT ERROR`s. (2) *The shape is gone and gated*: all 44 integer indexes on a call's result across 11 `godot/tests/{unit,integration}` files hoist the call's list, assert its size and `return` when it is short - including the eight dialog reads that only looked guarded, where `assert_not_null(dialogs[0])` ran after the index that could abort - and `scripts/check-test-guards` fails any line that indexes a call's result directly (127 test files, `# guard-ok:` exemption, `ci-check` step 3.3b). Docs: `testing.md` carries the guard rule and the split verdict, `quick-reference.md` the card, and `defect-classes.md` lists the new control under `gate-lies`. Full gate: `./scripts/gut-check` 123 scripts / 1566 tests green, `./scripts/ci-check --fast` green. |
| ✅ | BUILD | **`docs-check` fails on ignored scratch markdown, so any commit waits for a blog draft** - found 2026-11-20 while closing the GUT gate row: the user's `TEMP/` scratch area (ignored by `.gitignore`'s `TEMP*`, home to `gpty-blog-story` drafts and `fix-forensics` baselines) appeared mid-session, and `scripts/docs-check`'s `markdown_files()` walks the worktree, so five drafts with repo-relative links turned every gate run into `21 problem(s)` - and the pre-commit hook runs that gate, so no commit could land without editing or moving someone else's files. | ✅ | BUILD | **An idle window costs 650-760 MB and the renderer is most of it, not the terminal** - measured 2026-11-11 on the shipped v0.8.1 Linux build (Godot 4.7.2, RTX 5070 Ti, Vulkan 1.4.357; `VmRSS` from `/proc`, private sandbox): 647 MB with one pane idle (anon 291, file 250, shmem 106) and 655 MB after every pane is killed, so the terminal is not where the memory is (the two runs differ by less than the run-to-run spread); 30 000 lines pushed through a pane, past the 10 000-row `history_lines` cap, add about 39 MB and then plateau, linearly with the cap. A bare Godot 4.7.2 Forward+ window holding an empty `Control` is 540 MB; the same window on Compatibility (OpenGL3) is 264 MB (the three OpenGL3 figures in this row are the ones that did not reproduce: re-measured 2026-11-13 on the same machine - nvidia 620.70.06 and the Vulkan 1.4.363 loader had both been installed before this capture - a v0.8.1 build measured 366 MB and a v0.8.2 build 363 MB on `--rendering-driver opengl3`, so the 290 MB recorded here is a capture artefact rather than a stack or a code difference, and the README now carries the reproducible row); `gpty-gui --rendering-driver opengl3` on this build is 290 MB warm and 356 MB on a cold cache, against 1076 MB on a cold Forward+ launch - about 430 MB of first-run pipeline compilation that the driver keeps resident in that process, since runs 2 and 3 are 647 MB, so the 1 GB first launch is not a leak and should be documented rather than reported. A post-reboot 2026-11-21 check on the Vulkan/Forward+ debug editor build (1720x1382, two panes with a streamer and btop, grain Subtle) measured 1.09 GiB VmRSS (anon 529, file 409, shmem 175) and did not grow between an idle single pane and the active pair (1.390 -> 1.392 GB in a clean sandbox), so the user-visible 1.03 GB is the cold-launch/debug-build figure above, not pane growth; the shipped-build warm figure remains the comparison. The warm gap is Vulkan host allocations (anon 291 vs 122) plus 78 MB more shmem; the rest is driver code pages shared with the desktop. `godot/project.godot` named no `renderer/rendering_method`, so Forward+ was the implicit default on every platform and Windows pays the same driver tax. This is a decision, not a one-line flip: Compatibility is 2.2x lighter and the app draws a 2D canvas, but the 3D vault graph later in this release needs Forward+. Fix: run both renderers on the same build through `scripts/ui-capture` (both themes, the effect shaders, `draw_bench` at 90x30 and 200x60) and with an 8-pane layout, choose per platform, set it in `project.godot` plus every export preset, and record the chosen numbers in the v0.9.0 performance budget. If Compatibility wins, narrow **The vault graph is a physical 3D map you can push around** to a 2D spring layout in a `SubViewport` instead of dropping it. Closed 2026-11-22: the bake-off ran the same build on both renderers through the capture harness and the memory harness. Measured on the shipped v0.8.5 Linux archive (same machine, `VmRSS`, MiB, warm = a launch in the sandbox): Forward+ 667 MB with one pane, 669 with eight, 667 with zero, 1083 on a first launch, +52 MB for 30 000 lines; Compatibility 310 with one pane, 305 with eight, 363 on a first launch; Mobile 592 with one pane (rejected: only 11% below Forward+). The captures (both themes, `crt`/`grain`, window opacity, the orbital graph, an 8-pane layout) were taken on both renderers; Compatibility renders the orbital map, the opacity veil's alpha and the effects, and only the graph's subtle bloom differs. `draw_bench` (warm, median of three) puts a full repaint at 1.60/3.39 ms on Forward+ and 1.48/3.72 ms on Compatibility at 90x30/200x60, so there is no material repaint penalty; `effect_bench` at 4K on both renderers measured a 237-249 us frame floor with a 0-13 us delta, so it still does not isolate the shader and the v0.9 effect row keeps that work. Decision: Forward+ stays the default on every platform, because the orbital graph's bloom and the v0.8.6 visual identity need it; Compatibility is the documented opt-in (`--rendering-driver opengl3`) and 2.2x lighter warm / 3x lighter on a first launch. `godot/project.godot` now pins `renderer/rendering_method="forward_plus"` explicitly; no export preset carries a rendering-method key, and a future per-platform flip is one verified project-setting override (`rendering/renderer/rendering_method.<os>`, verified on 4.7.2), with the Windows ANGLE/WARP prerequisite owned by the Windows fallback row below. The graph's `auto` rule changes with the decision: it now picks the orbital map on any hardware adapter (Compatibility included) and the planar canvas only on a software rasterizer, superseding the v0.8.5 "Compatibility resolves to planar" rule; `test_graph_mode_auto_follows_the_adapter_not_the_renderer` pins the adapter cases. Harness: `scripts/ui-capture` and `scripts/ui-capture-check` gained `--renderer`; `ui_capture.gd` gained `eight-panes` (4x2 via `grid_cols`/`grid_rows`), `wiki-graph-orbital` and `window-opacity`; `scripts/measure-cost` warms the OpenGL3 arm with a discarded launch and records the eight-pane row (the old 366 MB was a cold GL reading). README's Trade-offs table and the v0.9.0 Performance budget in `testing.md` carry the fresh figures; the budget also records the pending frame/CPU/power/startup/input rows. Tests: the adapter test, the full GUT suite (130 scripts / 1657 tests), the full default-renderer `ui-capture-check` (59 states) and its Compatibility matrix subset (10 states), and `measure-cost --check` (every row OK). The merged-main re-gate passed CI 45062060839 (every job, `windows-smoke` included), CodeQL, Docs and Secret scan on f15c79e. |
| ⬜ | BUILD | **The Windows build has no fallback above native OpenGL 3** - found 2026-11-21 while reviewing the Windows/macOS launch paths: the Windows preset sets `application/export_d3d12=0` and `application/export_angle=0`, while `project.godot` pins Forward+ (Vulkan) as the default and the shipped fallback chain is Vulkan then native OpenGL 3 (Compatibility). Native GL on Windows can be the 1.1 GDI Generic implementation, which cannot run the GLES3 path; D3D12/WARP (software) and ANGLE (GLES3 over D3D11) are both absent from the build. A machine whose driver has no Vulkan therefore either starts on Compatibility or not at all, and the software-rasterizer warning can never name WARP because WARP is not exported. Fix: decide whether the Windows build exports D3D12 (with the Agility SDK) and/or ANGLE, weigh the download size, and verify on a Windows VM/RDP session (WARP) and an Intel driver with no Vulkan; a Windows smoke on a machine without Vulkan should assert the app starts and the `[renderer]` line names the fallback. This informs **An idle window costs 650-760 MB and the renderer is most of it, not the terminal**. The 2026-11-22 bake-off ran the same build on both renderers and measured Compatibility 2.2x lighter (310 against 667 MB warm; 363 against 1083 MB cold), but `project.godot` now pins Forward+ on every platform and the wiki graph's `auto` mode picks the orbital map on hardware. Windows can adopt Compatibility only after this row exports ANGLE (and/or D3D12/WARP) and a Windows smoke proves the GL path; the switch is one `rendering/renderer/rendering_method.windows` project-setting override, verified on 4.7.2 — no export preset carries a rendering-method key. |

| ✅ | GUI | **Dynamic Shaders** - closed 2026-11-21 (build 0.8.6): the closed table is `UiEffects` - `crt` (static scanlines, a phosphor mask and a vignette) and `grain` (film grain in a half-resolution `SubViewport` at 30 Hz) - and its two `preload` paths are the only shaders the app can run; no profile, settings file or plugin names one. `Settings → Appearance → Effects` offers the table and an `Off`/`Subtle`/`Full` intensity (Off by default, Subtle = half the shader's amplitude), persisted as `effect`/`effect_intensity` and broadcast through `apply_to_terminal`; the pane schema (`PaneTypes.TERMINAL_SETTINGS`) accepts only the table's ids and `test_ui_effects.gd` keeps the two lists equal. The orphan `godot/shaders/crt.gdshader` was deleted and its replacement fixes both defects it carried: the scanline term is a UV period computed from the pane's own height and the font's cell height (`rows`), never `SCREEN_PIXEL_SIZE` and never scaled by resolution, and the shader keeps `COLOR` (the ground's tint and alpha) instead of sampling a texture. The lit band adds a little light so the comb is visible on the default black ground, and the gap darkens a lit ground, so both themes read. A pane paints the ground through one `Layer.EFFECT` quad under every band; the pane's own ground rect and the row runs at the default background are skipped while it is active, so text, selection, inverse cells and ANSI backgrounds stay opaque and a scheme's alpha does not compound. Static effects are not evaluated per frame (the layer is redrawn only when the pane changes); grain's budget is declared as the row's measured FBM upper bound (0.29 ms at 1080p, 1.02 ms at 4K) because this environment has no GPU (Godot fell back to GL Compatibility on llvmpipe, where the bench measured a 2.46 ms full-frame software delta at 4K over an 18 ms baseline), and `godot/tests/bench/effect_bench.gd` is the harness that will replace it on real hardware. Glassmorphism's translucency is the shipped window-opacity veil; a backdrop blur reads behind the window and stays in the Future row. Tests: `test_ui_effects.gd` (`test_the_ambient_ids_are_the_pane_schema_values`, `test_every_ambient_entry_declares_a_shader_a_budget_and_a_rate`, `test_an_unknown_effect_falls_back_to_the_default`, `test_the_level_clamps_and_strength_is_half_at_subtle`), `test_pane_effects.gd` (`test_an_active_effect_adds_one_ground_layer_below_every_band`, `test_no_effect_layer_exists_while_the_intensity_is_off`, `test_a_default_background_run_is_skipped_only_while_an_effect_is_active`, `test_the_animated_effect_renders_into_a_half_resolution_buffer`, `test_a_pin_renders_one_buffer_frame_and_stops_the_clock`, `test_turning_the_effect_off_frees_the_layer_and_the_buffer`), `test_settings_manager.gd` (`test_the_effect_and_its_intensity_survive_a_restart`, `test_an_unknown_stored_effect_loads_as_the_default`), `test_workspace_settings.gd` (`test_a_settings_save_broadcasts_the_effect_to_every_terminal`) and `test_settings_panel.gd` (`test_the_effects_section_lists_the_closed_table_and_intensity`, `test_the_appearance_reset_restores_the_effect_defaults`).
