| ✅ | GUI | **Panes animate when they are created, destroyed or swapped** - closed 2026-11-17 as the framework plus three built-ins. `godot/scenes/terminal/pane_transitions.gd` is the registry: each animation declares the transitions it serves (`create`/`close`/`swap`), the channels it touches (`alpha`, `scale`, or the swap's rect pair) and a property schema (`duration_ms`, `scale_from`, `overshoot_pct`), so a new animation is one table entry and appears in the pickers with no settings or workspace code. `godot/scenes/ui/ui_motion.gd` is the policy: `SettingsManager.cfg_motion` is Off/Subtle/Full - Off draws every change in one frame, Subtle eases with no overshoot, Full allows the animation's declared overshoot capped at 3 % (`UiMotion.overshoot`). Settings → Appearance → Motion carries the master switch, one picker per transition (None included) and the selected animation's property rows generated from the schema; every write applies without a restart. Create reserves the final rect first, then fades and scales the wrapper from 0.92 with its pivot at the centre, so no sibling moves; close detaches the tile, grows the siblings, draws the dying wrapper above them and below the divider bands, fades it out and frees it exactly once after the tween (a closed terminal's process therefore lives one transition longer); a position swap records both rects, commits the tile data, lays out, then eases both wrappers between the old and new rects. `PaneTransitions` is skipped on an inactive or hidden workspace, during a divider drag, and while any terminal in that workspace is in its flood cadence (`TerminalPane.under_flood_load`); a layout pass snaps create and swap to final and leaves a close ghost alone (its rect is already final); the launch/profile restore path never reaches the layer. A type switch fades the new pane in over the freed old one. Tests: `test_pane_transitions.gd` (registry shape, default/kind fallback, parameter sanitation and clamping, the overshoot cap, runner guards), `test_pane_motion.gd` (create settles on the exact layout rect, close frees once, one transition through a close-during-create, Off is one frame, a layout pass snaps, swap ends in the swapped rects, hidden-workspace and flood suppression), `test_settings_manager.gd` (roundtrip, damaged store), `test_settings_panel.gd` (pickers, generated rows, debounced edit, reset). |
| ✅ | GUI | **The transition library grows past fade and move** - closed 2026-11-21: three entries joined the registry. `slide` (create/close) is the new `slide` channel: it offsets the wrapper `distance_px` (8-240, default 48) toward the tile's split edge - the manager records that edge on the tile (`edge` = `left`/`right`/`top`/`bottom`, written by `_split_horizontal`/`_split_vertical`, absent on a restored or full-grid tile) and the runner clamps the offset to the grid, so a ghost never paints over the sidebar - then eases to the final rect, and `_snap` restores the position of a snapped create; `pop` is a create/close entry over the existing alpha+scale channels (scale from 0.55 in 120 ms, the 3 % overshoot at Full) and needed no runner code; `crossfade` is the new swap `fade` channel - both wrappers dissolve out where they were drawn, trade rects at the midpoint, and dissolve back in, with the swap snap restoring the base modulate. Every entry keeps the framework's contract: `Off`/`None` draws in one frame, a natural finish lands exactly on the final rect/scale/alpha, and a second transition on the same wrapper takes ownership of the first. The pickers, property rows and storage took no change: all three appear in `Settings → Appearance → Motion` from their registry entry alone. Tests: `test_a_slide_offset_points_at_the_split_edge_and_stays_in_the_grid`, `test_every_create_and_close_animation_declares_a_channel`, `test_a_slide_create_enters_from_the_split_edge_and_lands_on_its_rect`, `test_a_slide_close_exits_toward_the_split_edge`, `test_a_pop_create_grows_from_its_scale`, `test_a_crossfade_swap_dissolves_through_the_midpoint`, and the extended picker and property-row assertions. No new capture here: the v0.8.6 capture row owns the release images. |
| ✅ | CONCEPT | **Reactive Environments** - closed 2026-11-21 (build 0.8.6): `Settings → Appearance → Effects → Reactive effects` is the mapping table - one row per concept, each with Off / Tint (red) / Pulse (accent) / Particles (green), eight rules maximum, no rules by default. `UiEffects.REACTIVE` is the closed cue vocabulary and owns each cue's tone (`DANGER`/`ACCENT`/`SUCCESS`, read from the active palette at draw time), and `cfg_reactive_effects` is sanitized on load (concept-name cap, unknown effect ids dropped). The trigger is the existing concept drain: `_poll_concept_events_for` calls `_flash_concept` for a routed capture and for a notify-only notice, which resolves the concept name and asks the source pane to cue - display only, nothing acknowledged, flushed or published back into the engine. `PaneEffects.flash` enforces the per-pane `FLASH_MIN_INTERVAL_MS` of 334 ms (at most three starts in any second; a trigger inside the window is dropped, not queued) and skips a hidden pane, so an off-screen workspace pays no GPU work; no cue is full-screen (the pane is the flash surface). Tint is a 16 %-peak wash over its pane (80 ms in, 240 ms hold, 400 ms out), pulse a 2 px border at 0.9 fading over 500 ms, and particles a 48-particle `CPUParticles2D` burst whose pool is allocated once and re-emitted with `restart()` (no per-frame allocation; normal blend - no additive pass ships, so nothing here is the "light adds" case). Tests: `test_ui_effects.gd` (`test_sanitize_reactive_keeps_known_effects_and_refuses_the_rest`, `test_sanitize_reactive_caps_the_table`, `test_reactive_for_reads_the_stored_mapping`), `test_pane_effects.gd` (`test_a_cue_is_rate_limited_to_three_a_second`, `test_a_hidden_pane_never_cues`, `test_a_tint_cue_uses_the_danger_tone`, `test_pinning_a_cue_draws_its_final_frame`, `test_particles_are_a_fixed_preallocated_burst`), `test_settings_panel.gd` (`test_adding_a_reactive_rule_stores_and_renders_it`, `test_removing_a_reactive_rule_clears_it`, `test_off_clears_a_reactive_rule`, `test_the_reactive_table_hides_add_at_the_cap`) and `test_settings_manager.gd` (`test_reactive_rules_drop_unknown_effects_on_load`). Screenshot (under Screenshots below): *A pinned Tint cue on its source pane, and the Effects section's mapping rows.* |
| ✅ | WIKI | **The vault graph is a physical 3D map you can push around** - the graph is a deterministic 2D canvas (`vault_graph_model.gd` owns the layout and hit-testing; `vault_graph_view.gd` draws it and already supports dragging a node and a Relayout that re-runs the springs), so it feels like a static diagram next to Obsidian's. Fix: a 3D presentation inside a `SubViewport` (Camera3D, orthographic by default with a free-orbit toggle, one sprite or billboarded label per note, link lines) backed by Godot 3D physics: each note is a body, each link a spring, so a dragged node pushes its neighbours and the map settles when released. Keep `vault_graph_model.gd` the only owner of graph data, link resolution and the semantic hit-test (raycast into the viewport, then delegate to the model); the view owns bodies, springs and camera. Persist node positions in the pane's layout state (the same state that carries `open_note` and `graph`), capped and ignored per missing note. The simulation pauses when the pane is hidden or the viewport is not visible; node and link caps stay as they are; no physics state is ever written to the vault. Tests: a push moves a neighbour and settles; a link stays attached while both ends move; a hidden pane ticks nothing; positions round-trip through the layout; a deleted note is ignored; the graph data is unchanged after an interaction. Shipped 2026-11-18 (build 0.8.5): `godot/scenes/ui/vault_graph_3d.gd` is a `SubViewport`-hosted `RigidBody3D` per node and one spring per drawn edge, with a deterministic per-id depth layer; a drag freezes one body, pushes its neighbours, and rewrites its incident rest lengths on release; the simulation freezes when settled or hidden (a hidden view reports zero ticks) and the initial at-rest map skips a pointless settle pass. Picking stays with `VaultGraphModel`: `hit_test`/`hit_test_edge` run on camera-projected screen positions (the screen-nearest rule the planar canvas and the labels share), not a physics raycast, and labels stay in the screen-space cull rather than a `Label3D` per note. `VaultGraphCanvas` is the shared contract; the planar canvas remains the fallback, `graph_mode` is `auto`/`3d`/`2d` (Compatibility resolves to planar), and `graph_positions` rides the pane's layout state capped at 4000 entries, +/-4096 and 0.1 rounding, with missing notes ignored; a mode switch keeps the arrangement. The cinematic layer is additive/alpha halos, a depth fog that leaves the ground alone, dark-theme glow, a fixed-seed dust pool only while moving, and a vignette; the 3D ground is the 2D palette background behind a transparent viewport so both themes stay exact. Measured on the dev machine (RTX 5070 Ti, Forward+, headless CPU): 52 notes / 312 links build 5.7 ms, force step 0.20 ms, node visuals 0.44 ms, link rebuild 1.2 ms; 2000 notes / 10 000 edges build 170 ms, force step 5.5 ms, node visuals 15.1 ms, link rebuild 15 ms at the fallback 15 Hz; no ticks once settled or hidden. Tests: `test_vault_graph_3d.gd` (push/settle, hidden ticks, link endpoints, round-trip, deleted note, data unchanged, Motion Off, orbit) plus the model, planar, pane and settings suites. Screenshot (under Screenshots below): *The vault graph as a physical 3D map*. |
| ✅ | WIKI | **A workspace switch reopens the orbital graph on its last rendered frame** - reported 2026-11-18 from use: leave the wiki workspace, return, open Graph, and the previous map is drawn as a static background. Cause (`stale-state`): `VaultGraph3D._pause_for_hidden` set `_hidden_paused` and `UPDATE_DISABLED`; the visible branch of `_notification` requested a frame while the flag still held the viewport disabled, and `_resume_from_hidden` (the only clearer) was reached only from a physics frame that is off while settled, so the `SubViewportContainer` kept its last texture. Fixed: the visible branch calls `_resume_from_hidden`, which clears the flag and requests `UPDATE_ONCE` when settled. Test: `test_a_settled_view_repaints_after_hiding` (`godot/tests/integration/test_vault_graph_3d.gd`), whose hidden host is the workspace switch's visibility path. Audit: `vault_graph_3d.gd` is the only app-owned `SubViewport`; the other visibility handlers own no render target (recorded under `stale-state` in `pitfalls.md`). |

| ✅ | WIKI | **The default graph build can steal the keyboard from the note list it replaces** - fixed 2026-11-20: `_show_graph_canvas` reads the focus owner before it hides anything and hands the keyboard to the canvas when that owner is one of the surfaces the canvas replaces (`_is_graph_replaced_surface`: the list, search, note box/toolbar, footer, placeholder, New and Graph), so a build that lands over the list keeps the next keystroke; a surface it does not replace keeps what the user gave it. `_graph_focus_pending` and `request_graph(take_focus)` are gone - the decision is read at land time instead of remembered, which also stops a press whose build lands after the user clicked another pane. Tests: `test_a_default_graph_build_carries_the_keyboard_off_the_list` (fails on `gui_get_focus_owner() == null` with the carry removed, measured), `test_a_default_graph_build_does_not_steal_another_surfaces_keyboard`, and `test_opening_the_graph_hands_the_keyboard_to_the_canvas` now focuses the button the way a click does; `test_title_bar_keyboard.gd`'s read-only wiki case drops its `graph: false` narrowing and settles the default build before the chrome Escape. |
| ✅ | GUI | **Window fit: opacity and inner padding** - closed 2026-11-20: `Settings -> System -> Window opacity` (presets 100/95/90/85/80/75/70/60/50/40 %, stored as `cfg_window_opacity` and clamped 40-100) writes `Window.transparent`/`transparent_bg` and shows the `Workspace`'s full-screen opacity veil (`godot/shaders/window_opacity.gdshader`: a `BackBufferCopy` plus a `blend_disabled` pass that samples the finished canvas, renormalises its premultiplied colour and writes the setting's alpha once), with a new first-child `WindowBg` rect covering every seam (with `transparent_bg` the engine's clear colour is transparent, so an uncovered pixel would turn black at that alpha); a per-surface `Workspace.modulate` was the first implementation and measured framebuffer alpha `0.992` at a 70 % setting because the terminal stacks four opaque surfaces, while the veil gives `0.698` - the window is genuinely at the setting; the row is disabled, not dead, when `WindowChrome.transparency_supported()` (i.e. `DisplayServer.is_window_transparency_available()`) is false, and `project.godot` now carries `display/window/per_pixel_transparency/allowed=true`, because the engine answers unavailable without it even on OpenGL. Measured on this desktop (Godot 4.7.2, RTX 5070 Ti, XWayland): false under the shipped Forward+ renderer here, so the row is disabled on this desktop, and true under `--rendering-driver opengl3` and native Wayland; an in-app capture of the 70 % setting reads framebuffer alpha `0.698` across the app's pixels, while an embedded `AcceptDialog` stayed 1.0 - dialogs, file pickers and popup menus are separate engine viewports and stay opaque, like a terminal's menus. `Settings -> Appearance -> Terminal padding` (0-24 px per side, default 4 in `PaneTypes.TERMINAL_PADDING_DEFAULT`, stored as `cfg_terminal_padding`) is spent on the fit: `_grid_for_size` subtracts `2 x pad` and `_grid_offset` centres the grid inside that box, so the row and column counts always equal what the pane draws; a padding change refits the live grid at once through `_resize_to_fit` (the existing 0.05 s debounce is its retry for a refused backend resize), and the same metrics hook now makes a font-size change refit instead of waiting for a tile resize. Tests: `test_the_fit_spends_the_padding_on_every_side`, `test_a_padding_change_refits_the_live_grid`, `test_a_font_size_change_refits_the_live_grid`, `test_opacity_veils_the_whole_window`, `test_a_window_that_cannot_be_transparent_stays_opaque`, `test_saving_the_opacity_reaches_the_window`, `test_the_opacity_veil_sits_above_every_surface`, `test_the_window_opacity_row_is_disabled_with_a_reason_when_unsupported`, `test_the_opacity_hint_names_the_driver_and_the_way_out`, `test_the_window_opacity_row_offers_the_presets_and_saves_a_pick`, `test_the_terminal_padding_row_writes_and_saves`, `test_window_fit_settings_roundtrip`. The Linux `.desktop` entry, icon and "Open gPTY here" file-manager action ship with v1.0.0's Distribution item, not here. The Settings row is present on every display server (2026-11-20): where `transparency_supported()` is false it is disabled and carries `opacity_hint`'s reason, because hiding it made the shipped feature look unshipped under X11/XWayland. |
| ✅ | GUI | **Colour schemes: import, and light mode that follows the system** - closed 2026-11-20: `gpty-core::scheme` turns a native JSON, Alacritty (TOML and YAML), iTerm2 XML plist, Base16 or legacy 16-colour CSV file into one `Scheme` (16 ANSI entries plus foreground, background, cursor and selection), capped at 16 KiB, with a closed native key set, a strict YAML block-mapping subset, and an iTerm2 reader that refuses an internal DOCTYPE subset, CDATA and any entity but the five predefined ones. `GptyScheme.parse` is the FFI seam and `SettingsManager.import_scheme` copies a validated native file into `user://schemes/`, so the source may move or be deleted. Eleven schemes ship (dracula, gruvbox dark/light, nord, tokyo-night, catppuccin mocha/latte, one-light, solarized dark/light, vivid) with their licences recorded in `LICENSE-EXCEPTIONS.md` section 4. The terminal joins the chrome's existing resolution: `cfg_scheme_dark`/`cfg_scheme_light` are read through `active_scheme_path()`, an empty light slot follows the dark one, and the old `color_scheme` key migrates on load. A pane keeps its own scheme in `PaneSchemeStore` (`pane_scheme`, `file: false`, `ipc: false`). Colour now reaches the cells for real: `Palette` carries the default pair, `set_default_colors` writes it, `CellInfo::from_cell` resolves `NamedColor::Foreground`/`Background` through it, and `_apply_palette` runs unconditionally after `start_shell`, which also closes the v0.9.5 foreground row; a scheme's cursor and selection are drawn at gPTY's overlay alphas. | ✅ | GUI | **The Settings tab bar overflows behind the engine's arrows at the 560 px floor** - fixed 2026-11-20 (build 0.8.6): measured at the floor, the live eight-tab bar needed 561 px of the 504 px it had and hid `Shortcuts` and `About`. Cause (`missed-call-site`): a `TabContainer`'s internal `TabBar` takes its tab styleboxes and font colours as overrides from the **`TabContainer`** theme item, not the `TabBar` item; `UiTheme` registered them on `TabBar` only (only `font_size` was mirrored), so the engine's default box - 10 px a side plus 1 px borders, 20 px per tab - was live and the row was off-brand as well as 57 px too wide. Both types now carry the same `StyleBox` objects and colours, and the engine's 8 px `side_margin` indent is zeroed so the row aligns with the header; the app's 6 px box puts the eight at 401 px of ink + 8*12 = 497 px, 15 px inside the 512 px bar (the card's 544 px less its 16 px inner margins). `test_the_tab_box_is_on_both_types` pins the two registrations, `test_the_live_tab_bar_uses_the_theme_box` pins the forwarding, and `test_all_eight_tabs_fit_the_560x340_floor` pins the clamped card (544 px, bar 512 px) with contiguous rects; `settings-560x340` shows `About` on the row with no arrows. Screenshot (under Screenshots below): *All eight Settings tabs on the 560x340 floor*. |
| ✅ | GUI | **A GPU fallback to a software rasterizer is silent, and its FPS reads as the app's cost** - reported 2026-11-21 from a launch log: Vulkan returned `ERR_CANT_CREATE`, OpenGL 3 `ERR_UNCONFIGURED`, Mesa EGL logged `failed to create dri2 screen`, and the run ended on `Mesa llvmpipe`; `nvidia-smi` agreed with `Failed to initialize NVML: Driver/library version mismatch` (userspace 620.73 against the loaded module), so no GPU context existed at all. The live app measured 80 FPS at 1720x1382; a fresh sandbox at 1920x1080 measured 91-92, and the empty first-run app at 1720x1382 measured 82 with vsync on and 81-85 with it off (12.2 ms/frame) - the renderer's fill cost, not the terminal's paint or the effect's. The log said none of that after the engine's three errors, and the status bar printed a bare `80 FPS` as if the number were the app's. Fixed 2026-11-21: `godot/scenes/ui/renderer_info.gd` (`RendererInfo`) matches the engine's adapter name against the known CPU rasterizers (llvmpipe, softpipe, SwiftShader, WARP, Apple's software renderer); `Workspace._ready` writes one `[renderer]` line naming driver, method, adapter, viewport, device scale, screen refresh, vsync, frame cap and effect, and one `push_warning` saying the frame rate is CPU-bound and to check the driver (the Linux kernel-module sentence is gated to Linux); the status bar appends ` · software` to the FPS (`StatusBar.set_render_note`), and Settings -> About carries a `Renderer:` line. Tests: `test_renderer_info.gd` (10: marker list, vendor dedupe, record format, scale, vsync labels, the platform-gated hint, glow capability), `test_status_bar.gd` (the note rides in the FPS segment and clears), `test_settings_panel.gd` (the About line). Verified on the reporter's session in both states: on the fallback the startup log named `opengl3_es (gl_compatibility) on Mesa llvmpipe (LLVM 23.1.5, 256 bits)` at `1920x1080`, `scale 1.00`, `164.9 Hz`, `vsync on`, `max_fps unlimited`, `effect crt@0`, the warning followed, and the status bar read `91 FPS · software`; after the reboot the same line read `vulkan (forward_plus) on NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5070 Ti` at `1720x1382`, `scale 1.00`, `164.9 Hz`, `vsync on`, `max_fps 165`, no warning was emitted, and the live window measured 155-158 FPS (162-165 in a clean sandbox of the same shape) against 80 on the fallback. Cross-platform review: the warning's Linux kernel-module sentence is gated to Linux; the record carries the window's screen scale (Retina/HiDPI) and its current screen's refresh; `Native` FPS and a pane's follow-the-display cadence read the window's current screen; the 3D graph enables glow only on Forward+ (Mobile ignores it). Remaining: a window moved to another display keeps the cap resolved at launch (its own row); WARP is detected by adapter name because GDScript exposes no adapter-type enum; CI is headless, so the GPU path is verified manually. |

(1) §3 says "The site's home page carries exactly one image: the current release's `vX.Y.Z_1.png`", but `docs/content/_index.md` carries demo clips through the `video` shortcode and no image at all, so the sentence is unexecutable - v0.8.4 left it alone rather than adding a screenshot the page was never built to hold. (2) §8's `git add` list omits `docs/data/cost-history.json` and `docs/static/images/cost-history.svg`, both of which `scripts/measure-cost --record` writes, so following it verbatim ships a README naming the new build while the published ledger and chart still stop at the previous release. Fixed 2026-11-21: §3 now names the blog entry as the only carrier of `vX.Y.Z_1.png` and describes the home page's demo clips instead of claiming a screenshot; §8's staging list includes `docs/data/cost-history.json` and `docs/static/images/cost-history.svg`. `scripts/docs-check`, `build-docs --check` and the full gate pass. |
| ✅ | GUI | **The README and every document it links are readable inside Settings** - closed 2026-11-17: the About tab is a documentation viewer over a generated bundle. `scripts/build-docs` walks the Markdown reachable from `README.md` (64 documents: the root guides and the roadmap, changelog and agents pages, including `SUBPROJECTS.md` through its GitHub blob URL) and packs it into `godot/docs.json` (1.6 MiB), with a byte-for-byte `--check` in `ci-check`, `scripts/build`, the Docs workflow and the workflow path filters. The generator strips YAML front matter, unwraps `<details>`/`<summary>`, drops the README's centered badge header (the renderer shows images as alt text, so it would be a wall of `Image: ...` lines), and rewrites every internal link - relative, blob URL, or same-page `#anchor` - to `gpty-doc:<repo path>#<heading text>`, translating the site's slug to the heading text `MarkdownView.scroll_to_heading` matches. `DocsBundle` validates the bundle and caches it lazily on the About tab's first visit (never at startup); `DocsViewer` renders one document through the existing `MarkdownView`/`GptyMarkdown` pipeline, and the renderer's new opt-in docs mode percent-encodes the `gpty-doc:` halves and refuses a payload over 1024 bytes, so a document can only ever name a bundled key and the shell is never handed the scheme. Navigation is `NavHistory`: the trail is capped at 64, a new page truncates the forward tail, and overflow drops the second entry - never the root - so Back always reaches the README. Back/Forward are `Icons.icon_button` carets with disabled states; a link outside the bundle raises a toast and leaves the page in place. Keyboard shortcuts moved to their own Shortcuts tab (the palette row follows), the app and IPC versions share one `VersionRow`, the repo URL row is gone, and the card grew to `CARD_SIZE` 720x560 with the About tab's `FILL_TAB_SIZE` 720x640, both clamped to the window. Tests: `test_nav_history.gd` (root pinning, cap, forward truncation), `test_docs_bundle.gd` (validation, caps, missing bundle), `test_docs_viewer.gd` (README, link, back, forward, refusal), the doc-mode cases in `markdown.rs`, `test_gdext_ffi.gd`, `test_settings_about_tab.gd`, the eight-tab fit test, and the resized-card assertions in `test_small_window.gd`; the `settings-about` capture state is the visual record. |

## v0.8.7 - Depth, Backdrop & Motion

The finish pass the visual-identity release leaves open: motion gets one switch behind it, the terminal body gets a scheme-owned backdrop, floating surfaces get depth, and a focus change becomes findable. The study book that seeded these rows (`sparks-and-sprites`: prose CC-BY 4.0, code MIT) is a catalogue of presentation mechanics, and only its interface half is adopted here - character animation, procedural generation, flipbooks, input verbs and cursor trails are deliberately out of scope, because gPTY is a reading and orchestration surface, not a game. Motion is allowed where it announces a change - the Getting Started guide, a pane's first paint, a created sidebar row, the tab just opened - and never on text being read: the terminal grid, a streaming Reasoning turn, a note body or source code stay still.

| Done | Area | Item |
|:---:|:---:|---|
| ⬜ | GUI | **A fidelity spike decides the rest of this release: surfaces, mechanisms, budgets** - the rows below were found ad hoc (the empty workspace, the sidebar and Settings arrived from one conversation), and the study book enumerates far more mechanisms than were triaged, so the open question is fit and cost, not names. Do it in two halves, timeboxed, with no feature code. (1) *Surface inventory*: every visible surface from the scene tree and `ui_capture`'s staged states - chrome, overlays, panes, empty states - classified as reading, arrival, state loop or chrome, each with its owner row or a gap; the first pass over the 33 scripts under `godot/scenes/ui` and the pane bodies leaves the concept graph as the only interactive canvas without a fidelity owner (if it is pursued, live routing is a CONCEPT feature, not fidelity). (2) *Probes with numbers*: a per-glyph `RichTextEffect` on a short static title and on a 2k-character note (can a wave or gleam ever be affordable, or is `visible_characters` the ceiling), the reduced-buffer CRT on both renderers (feeds **An idle window costs 650-760 MB and the renderer is most of it, not the terminal**), and pooled event particles at 30 Hz (feeds **Reactive Environments**), each a throwaway `SubViewport` or `draw_bench` run, harness deleted after. Output: every candidate is a roadmap row with a surface, a mechanism, a budget and a stop condition, or an explicit reject with its reason; no committed audit document, no feature code. If the inventory yields nothing new, the rest of the release proceeds unchanged. |
| ⬜ | GUI | **Every animation obeys the Motion setting, including the badge and the keycap** - `Settings → Appearance → Motion` shipped with the pane transitions in v0.8.6 and `UiMotion` reads it there; the animations that still sit outside it are: `agent_badge.gd` starts `_process` from `show_state()` for `working` (one ring turn per 0.9 s, `WORKING_TURN`) and `needs-attention` (a 1.2 Hz sine, `PULSE_HZ`), and `shortcut_guide.gd` runs a 3 s keycap cycle on a wall clock whose only freeze is `pin_animation()` for captures. Fix: extend the existing `UiMotion` owner (a static class beside `UiTokens`) with the duration/amplitude accessors these consumers need; `off` stops the badge's `_process` and draws the phase-0 silhouette (the frame `pin_animation()` pins), freezes the guide's keycap at rest, makes the focus sweep and every text arrival instant, and reduces a toast to its hold time; `subtle`/`full` supply the duration and amplitude. Godot exposes no OS reduce-motion query, so the setting is the promise. Tests: `off` leaves `is_processing() == false` on an animated badge and its capture equals the phase-0 frame; a setting change applies without restart; the guide's pinned frame is identical under `off` and `pin_animation()`. |
| ⬜ | GUI | **Getting Started types each shortcut step in** - the guide's hero chord and caption swap instantly on every step (`_apply_step`, `shortcut_guide.gd:202`), and the surface's own title is literally `Getting Started` (`:249`); a per-step typewriter is the one text effect this surface wants, and the study book's recipe is one integer plus a timer (`Label.visible_characters`, no per-letter nodes). Fix: on each step change reveal `_hero_caption` and the chord at 30-40 characters/s with a 0.6 s ceiling, so the step is legible before the 3 s cycle turns; the title types once on show. A click on the keycap or a spawn shortcut completes the reveal and still performs the action, so "skip the text" can never cost the spawn; `Motion.off` shows every character on frame 0. The atlas cards are not typed - they are read, not performed - and `pin_animation()` pins the completed reveal so a capture cannot catch half a word. Tests: a reveal completes by its deadline; a click mid-reveal spawns exactly once; `off` is complete at frame 0; two pinned runs are pixel-identical. |
| ⬜ | GUI | **New chrome arrives instead of snapping** - three surfaces are arrivals and one is an update, and they currently look the same: the shared empty state (`PaneBody.empty_state` builds `EmptyGlyph`/`EmptyTitle`/`EmptyHint` Labels) appears fully formed, `_sync_pane_rows` inserts a created row fully formed (the `row == null` branch), and a Settings tab switch replaces the card in one frame; a filtered palette list is an update and must stay instant. Fix: one arrival helper on `UiMotion` - a fade on the empty-state host (80-160 ms), a fade on a *created* sidebar row only, and an 80 ms fade on the incoming Settings tab (no movement); at `full`, add a 4 px rise to the empty state and stagger at most three created rows 0.03 s apart, never a restore cascade; `off` draws every surface at its final state in one frame. A renumber, a reorder, an action swap, a palette keystroke, streaming Reasoning and the terminal grid never animate. Tests: a created row fades once and a renumber has no tween; a palette keystroke creates no tween; `off` equals the final frame; rects before and after an arrival are identical (no layout shift); a capture pins the completed state. |
| ⬜ | GUI | **The terminal background can carry a two-stop gradient from its scheme** - the pane paints one opaque ground (`terminal_pane.gd:1177`) and then a rect for every run of same-background cells (`_draw_row_background`, `:1199`), so a scheme is flat by construction; a two-colour vertical gradient is a finished backdrop at the cost of one fill (`sparks-and-sprites`' backgrounds sheet: a gradient is ~free, interpolated per vertex on the GPU). Fix: `Appearance` gets `Backdrop: Flat` or `Gradient` (default `Flat`), with one stop exactly the active terminal background and the other a fixed 6% luminance shift away from it, drawn once behind the grid, and runs at the default background skipping their rect - text, selection and inverse cells keep exact scheme colours, with no shader, no translucency and no renderer dependency. Contrast: the default foreground is checked against both stops at 4.5:1, and the 16 entries are recorded against the flat background for comparison. Tests: a default-background run draws no rect, a non-default run still draws, the endpoints equal the calculated stops, and the full-repaint cost is one extra fill measured on `draw_bench`'s `forced_us` with no per-cell work added. Screenshot: one terminal flat against the same terminal with a gradient. |
| ⬜ | GUI | **Floating surfaces sit above the page: contact shadow and a light edge** - the chrome draws borders and radii only: `ui_theme.gd`'s `_flat()` builds a 1 px border with no shadow, and no app stylebox sets `shadow_size` or `shadow_color` (only the vendored GUT scene does), so a dialog, the command palette, a toast and the pane-settings card have nothing between them and the terminal behind. Fix: one elevation rule (one light direction, top-left) in a new floating-surface `Theme` type variation, not per widget - a soft contact shadow (size 6-10, a new `SHADOW` token in both palettes) plus a 1 px inner top highlight in `SURFACE_RAISED`; no per-pixel blur and no shader (the study book's budget: fake blur is 3-5x the draws, a `StyleBoxFlat` shadow is one item). Light mode uses a neutral shadow, not black at low alpha, so it does not read as dirt. Tests: the dialog, palette, toast and pane-settings styleboxes carry the token; the `palette` capture over a terminal shows the separation; the contrast table still passes. |
| ⬜ | GUI | **A focus change is visible: the new pane's border sweeps in** - `terminal_pane.gd:1282` draws a 2 px `focus_border_color` rect only while the pane has focus (`FOCUS_BORDER_WIDTH = 2.0`) and swaps it in one frame, so a keyboard focus jump across a full grid has no trail to follow. Fix: on focus gain fade the border in from transparent to `TERMINAL_FOCUS` over 120-160 ms (or fade a 2 px accent inset down to the steady width), and fade it out on loss; the grid, text and every rect never move; a second change kills the running tween and starts from the current colour; `Motion.off` draws the final border in one frame. Display only. Tests: a focus change leaves every rect exact; `off` draws in one frame; an interrupted sweep leaves exactly one border; the `focus-sweep` capture state holds mid-sweep. |
| ⬜ | GUI | **The Settings card is a navigation rail with a content column and a live sample** - found 2026-11-21 from daily use: the card is one `TabContainer` (`settings_panel.gd:127`) with eight tabs, and the v0.8.6 floor fix leaves the bar at 497 of 512 px, so a ninth section overflows behind the engine's arrows; the tab labels carry 6 px horizontal and 4 px vertical content margins with `side_margin=0` (`ui_theme.gd:323-333`), which reads as unpadded, while the content's inner margin is `SPACE_LG` (8 px, `_create_tab`). The strip also has no room for the previews the next two rows need. Fix: replace the `TabContainer` with an `HBoxContainer` - a left navigation rail (a `ScrollContainer` of focusable section buttons, the selected row = the current section) and a right content column, both independently scrollable; each section keeps its own scroll offset, its per-section Reset sits in a sticky header, and the rail remembers the last section for the run. Give the content 16 px inner padding and the rail rows 8-12 px; at the design size the preview column is about 220-240 px (the card's design width grows to fit it, e.g. 960x640, while the window clamp still wins below that), and at the 560x340 floor the rail narrows and the preview stacks or hides, never overflows horizontally; a section switch stays instant (the update rule in *New chrome arrives instead of snapping*). Add a shared preview surface on the right for sections that have one: one sample pane mock carrying the active scheme, text and inverse/underline cells, run through `PaneTransitions` for a transition or `PaneEffects` for a shader, plus a sidebar strip when the effect scope includes it; the scheme, Motion and Effects pickers all preview through this one sample (never a second mock), the caption names the selected effect/transition and its static-or-30-Hz rate, and it is hidden and paused on other sections and when the panel is hidden; it is the only preview implementation the next two rows drive. Keep the public deep links (`open_commands`, `open_shortcuts`, the capture states' section names) working through a section-name map, and put a small Back/Forward trail over visited sections (the About viewer's `NavHistory` pattern) beside the title, so a search jump and a manual section pick share one history; Up/Down/Enter move the rail and the first content control follows; every rail row and the sample carry accessible names, and the sample's text description names the selected effect/transition and scope. Tests: section switching and deep links, per-section scroll/reset, the 560x340 floor, preview hidden/paused, the keyboard ring; update `test_settings_panel.gd` (62 tests), `test_small_window.gd`, the `settings*` `ui_capture` states (a rail state and a preview state), and the capture/accessibility audits. This replaces the fixed tab-size row and is the prerequisite for the two preview rows below. |
| ⬜ | GUI | **The Motion pickers name their animations but never show them** - `Settings → Appearance → Motion` offers four animated create/close entries and two swap ones by name, and nothing plays until the next pane spawn, close or position swap, so "Pop" against "Fade and Scale" and "Move" against "Crossfade" is a guess. Fix: a Play button beside each picker (and a replay when the picker changes) plays the selected entry on the rail row's shared preview surface - a scratch `Control` carrying the pane chrome's background, run through `PaneTransitions.create`/`close` (a swap entry plays on two samples through `swap`), so the runner stays the only animation implementation and the preview reads the live Motion level and the same stored params; `Off` draws the final frame, and the sample is never a pane body. Tests: the button plays the registry entry the picker names, a property edit reaches the next preview without a save, and `Off` leaves the sample at its final state. |
| ⬜ | GUI | **The Effects and Reactive rows never show what they play** - `Settings → Appearance → Effects` offers two ambient effects and an intensity, and each reactive rule picks Tint, Pulse or Particles, yet neither picker draws anything: a user choosing Film grain while the intensity is Off sees nothing, and Tint against Pulse is a guess until a concept happens to fire. Fix: the rail row's shared preview surface plays the selected effect at the selected intensity on a sample carrying a terminal pane's ground, in the selected scope (with a sidebar strip when the scope includes it), through `PaneEffects` so the preview is the shipped implementation rather than a second one; a Play button replays it, and each reactive row gets a Test button that plays its cue on the same sample under the same 334 ms rate limit; the effect preview at Off draws the plain ground, the cue previews pin their final frame (as `effect-cue` does), and both previews are new `ui-capture` states so the images repeat. Tests: a preview adds the same effect layer a pane would, `Off` leaves the sample at its ground, and a Test press is rate-limited exactly like a concept fire. |
| ⬜ | GUI | **The ambient effects stop at the pane edge, so the sidebar cannot carry one** - found 2026-11-21 from daily use: `UiEffects.AMBIENT` is configured only by `PaneEffects` on each terminal body, the sidebar's ground is the workspace's `_sidebar_bg` `ColorRect`, and no effect host sits under the sidebar rows, so the Effects picker changes terminal panes alone. The requested scope is panes, panes + sidebar, or sidebar only. Fix: a closed `cfg_effect_scope` (panes, both, sidebar) in Settings → Appearance → Effects, sanitized and reset with the section; the sidebar gets a reusable effect host - the configure/paint/clock half of `PaneEffects` extracted, or a `PaneEffects` mounted on a sidebar ground layer - below the rows and above its ground, using the sidebar's colour, with text and row surfaces opaque. The scope gates the pane layer as well, so "sidebar only" draws no global effect in panes even when a pane's own picker names one; the pane settings panel says the global scope excludes panes instead of silently drawing nothing. In icons mode the host follows the rail, while the flyout is a raised card with its own opaque surface. Static effects redraw only when the sidebar changes; the 30 Hz half-res grain is the only continuous cost; measure both at sidebar size with `effect_bench`, record the run in the v0.9.0 budget table, pin the phase for captures, and keep the existing contrast pairs (the effect is below text, never on it). Tests: the scope vocabulary and reset, the sidebar host on each value, panes off/on, Off frees both layers, contrast unchanged, and `settings-sidebar-effect` / `effect-sidebar` / `sidebar-icons-effect` captures. |
| ⬜ | GUI | **The ambient effect table is two entries, so the picker is a coin toss** - found 2026-11-21 from daily use: `UiEffects.AMBIENT_IDS` is `["crt", "grain"]` and `PaneTypes.TERMINAL_SETTINGS["effect"]["values"]` mirrors it; the transition library already grew to five entries through a registry (row *The transition library grows past fade and move*), while the effect table has no such path. Fix: add at least three closed entries behind `preload` constants - a cheap static one (e.g. vignette or halftone), an animated field at 30 Hz (e.g. aurora or plasma), and one more (e.g. dither or noise) - each with a label, hint, animated flag, rate at most 30 Hz, and a measured `budget_ms_1080p` / `budget_ms_4k` on the bake-off GPU (the v0.9.0 row *The effect frame budgets are borrowed from another shader* is the measurement precedent, and the measured run is recorded in that release's budget table); the hint names whether the entry is static or animated at 30 Hz, and a static effect declares 0 and redraws only on change. Keep the table closed: a profile, settings file or plugin names an id, never a path. The picker, the pane schema test, the preview surface, the sidebar scope and the capture states all read the table, so a new entry needs a shader file, a table row, budgets and a pinned capture; no new settings code. Tests: the id list equals the schema, every entry preloads a Shader and declares its budget and rate, the preview plays each entry, and `effect_bench` runs each on both renderers. |
| ⬜ | GUI | **The sidebar has two positions, not three: open or gone** - found 2026-11-21 from daily use: `Sidebar.WIDTH` is 180 and `COLLAPSED_WIDTH` is 20 (`sidebar.gd:27-28`); `_toggle_sidebar` flips the whole column and the 20 px state holds one `SidebarCollapsedBtn`, so every section is unreachable until the sidebar is reopened. The requested third position is an icon rail - one column of icon buttons (about 44-48 px) that keeps the window-mode menu, Add Pane, Search, Settings, Feedback, Workspaces, Profiles and Panes reachable, with a targeted flyout for the section whose icon was pressed. Fix: a closed `cfg_sidebar_mode` (expanded, icons, collapsed), persisted and reset like the other chrome settings; the header's collapse control becomes a three-state control and `Ctrl+Shift+B` cycles expanded -> icons -> collapsed, each mode naming itself in its tooltip and accessible name. The icon rail reuses the existing section content rather than a second copy: Workspaces/Profiles/Panes open one flyout anchored to the rail, Add Pane opens the existing type grid, Search/Settings/Feedback act as today, and the window-mode dropdown becomes a glyph button with the same popup. The flyout is an overlay beside the rail: it does not reflow the grid, closes on selection/click-outside/Escape and after a workspace/pane action, carries the section header and its Help, clamps at the 560x340 floor, and keeps its content live (the pane list updates while open). Keyboard: the rail is one focus ring, the flyout takes focus and returns it on close; every icon is named and tooltipped. The collapsed 20 px state and the existing per-section folds (`sidebar_folded`) stay exactly as today. Icons mode saves about 132 px at the 180 px default; measure the flyout's overdraw and capture `sidebar-expanded`, `sidebar-icons` (Workspaces and Panes flyouts open), `sidebar-icons-560x340` and `sidebar-collapsed`. Tests: mode persistence/reset and the cycle, each icon opens the right flyout or direct action, selection closes it, click-outside/Escape, the grid rect is unchanged while the flyout is open, the pane list stays live, the 560 floor, focus ring and audits; the expanded and collapsed modes' existing tests stay green. |
| ⬜ | GUI | **The finish pass is captured** - add staged states to `godot/tests/bench/ui_capture.gd` (`backdrop-gradient`, `elevation`, `focus-sweep`, `guide-typewriter`, `chrome-arrival`, `settings-rail`, `settings-preview-motion`, `settings-preview-effect`, `settings-sidebar-effect`, `sidebar-icons-effect`, `sidebar-expanded`, `sidebar-icons`, `sidebar-icons-560x340`, `sidebar-collapsed`) and save `issue-v0.8.7-<slug>.png` under `docs/static/images/`, each listed under Screenshots on this page with a caption that names the row(s) and every numbered box. Pin every animation a capture depends on (`pin_animation()` or the Motion setting's `off` frame) and open the icon flyout on a named section, so two runs are pixel-identical. |
| ⬜ | CORE | **Input reports success after the PTY writer thread exits** - when a pty write errors (EIO once the slave side closes), `PtyHandle`'s writer thread logs and exits, but the terminal task's stdin drain keeps receiving: `write_with_backpressure` returns on the non-`WouldBlock` error and drops that message, the next `send_text` still finds room because the drain keeps pulling, and only the per-message `write_bytes` sees the disconnected writer - so `inject`/`broadcast` answer delivered for bytes that never left the process. The window is the 500 ms `EXIT_DRAIN_QUIET_MS` before a child-exit teardown ends the task, so reachability is low. Fix: close `stdin_rx` on a terminal write error so the refusal reaches the caller, while the read side keeps draining so the exit code and a final capture still land. |
| ⬜ | GUI | **A window moved to another display keeps the frame cap resolved at launch** - found 2026-11-21 while reviewing the Windows/macOS paths: `Workspace._apply_fps_setting` and `TerminalPane.refresh_sync_interval` read `DisplayServer.window_get_current_screen()` when the setting is applied, so they are right at launch, but moving the window to a display with a different refresh rate does not re-run either. With `max_fps = Native` the engine cap is the old screen's rate: with vsync on, 165 on a 60 Hz display is harmless because the compositor holds 60, while 60 on a 165 Hz display caps it at 60 until a settings change or restart; a terminal's follow-the-display cadence keeps the old interval in both directions. Fix: notice the current screen changing (a low-frequency timer, or a window event where the platform raises one) and re-apply `Engine.max_fps` plus each live terminal's `refresh_sync_interval`; an explicit cap is left alone. Tests: a seam that supplies the screen and its rate, asserting the cap and cadence follow a move and an explicit cap does not; no per-frame polling. |
| ⬜ | DOCS | **The v0.8.7 changelog records the finish pass** - release-time: write `docs/content/docs/changelog/v0.8.7.md`, link it from `CHANGELOG.md`, cover the single Motion switch, the Getting Started typewriter, the chrome arrivals, the scheme backdrop, elevation for floating surfaces, the focus sweep, the Settings rail and its previews, the effect scope and the expanded effect table, the sidebar's expanded/icons/collapsed modes, and use the captured images. |

Captures from the November 2026 re-baseline (2026-11-03, build 0.7.2), Linux, 1920×1080 unless noted, taken in a private sandbox with a fresh first-run state by the method in AGENTS.md ("UI/UX assessment method"). Small windows are an embedded `SubViewport`: accurate for layout, not for OS window behaviour. The numbered boxes are drawn in the images; each key says what its box marks. A capture without boxes shows one surface whole - the Add Pane header, the help table it opens, the About tab's shortcut list, and the fixed search strip - and its key names the controls in words instead.

**First launch** - rows: *Shared theme, type scale, colour tokens and UI scale* and the closed v0.7.0 row *First terminal is focused at launch*; boxes 1 and 2 also record the pre-fix Add Pane grid (names in tooltips only) and the window-mode dropdown that read "OS", both fixed and recaptured under *Add Pane, named* below. Box 3 is Close All, destructive and directly under Settings, red at 3.50:1; 4 the status bar's right-hand text at 4.15:1; 5 the first prompt with its cursor visible - focus is where launch left it.

**Concept graph editor** - row: *Settings and Pane Settings are opaque and readable*. Box 1 is the sidebar's Add Pane grid showing through the editor's toolbar; 2 the Settings panel's tabs and concept list showing through the canvas; 3 the app's pane title bar and window buttons showing through the editor's header.

![The concept graph editor on 0.7.2](/images/issue-v0.7.2-concept-editor.png)

**Plugin Install Review** - rows: *Dialogs and profile forms match the app*, *Extend the assessment to the surfaces it did not cover*. Box 1 is the Install button carrying the dialog's focus ring (the review does take keyboard focus); 2 the pinned revision line, in a plain `Label` that cannot be selected or copied.

![The plugin install review on 0.7.2](/images/issue-v0.7.2-plugin-review.png)

**Workspace Trust** - rows: *Dialogs and profile forms match the app*, *Extend the assessment to the surfaces it did not cover*. Box 1 is the Restore button carrying the dialog's focus ring; 2 the spawn plan the dialog names before restoring (`/bin/sh` against the configured `/bin/bash`).

![The workspace trust dialog on 0.7.2](/images/issue-v0.7.2-workspace-trust.png)

**Agent-state badges** - row: *The agent-state badge distinguishes failed from needs-attention by colour alone* (closed). The one capture here taken **after** a fix rather than during the re-baseline: 2026-11-04, build 0.7.2 plus that row, from the `agent-badges` capture state (1280×420, four tiles in one row) - which is also what proves the capture repeats, since a live badge's spinner and pulse are wall-clock driven. Box 1 is `working` (spinner), 2 `completed` (check circle), 3 `failed` (X-circle), 4 `needs-attention` (warning triangle); 3 and 4 are the pair that used to differ by hue alone, and their silhouettes (circle-X against triangle) are what a protan/deutan user reads.

![The four agent-state badges](/images/issue-v0.7.2-agent-badges.png)

**First launch on the design system** - rows: *Shared theme, type scale, colour tokens and UI scale*, *Settings and Pane Settings are opaque and readable* (both closed). Captured 2026-11-04 on the 0.8.0 development build; every chrome colour and size on this screen comes from `ui_tokens.gd` through the one `Theme`. Box 1 is Close All, now `DANGER` at 6.75:1 on the sidebar (was 3.49:1); 2 the status bar's right-hand readout at 6.35:1 (was 4.15:1) in `TEXT_MUTED`.

![A fresh launch on the 0.8.0 development build](/images/issue-v0.8.0-first-launch.png)

**Settings, opaque** - row: *Settings and Pane Settings are opaque and readable* (closed). Box 1 is the card: one opaque `SURFACE` (`#202128`) with a 1 px border, where a terminal's text used to show through a 60 % fill; 2 the tab bar on the token type scale (13 px, accent underline on the selected tab).

![The Settings panel over a terminal](/images/issue-v0.8.0-settings-opaque.png)

**Pane Settings, opaque and explained** - row: *Settings and Pane Settings are opaque and readable* (closed). Box 1 is the card, opaque like Settings; 2 the `Attachment ID` field with the sentence it never had - the id IPC, MCP and agent commands address the pane by - at caption size in `TEXT_MUTED`.

![The Pane Settings card](/images/issue-v0.8.0-pane-settings-opaque.png)

**Save Profile** - row: *Dialogs and profile forms match the app* (closed). Box 1 is a command field: `/bin/bash` fits with room to spare (the 0.7.2 form clipped it to `/bin/ba` in a 69 px field); 2 Save and Cancel grouped at the right edge, with the dialog's own title inside the panel instead of Godot's stock grey frame.

![The Save Profile dialog](/images/issue-v0.8.0-save-profile-dialog.png)

**Workspace Trust** - row: *Dialogs and profile forms match the app* (closed). Box 1 is the spawn plan in a read-only `TextEdit`: selectable and copyable, and a path containing `[b]` cannot be read as markup; 2 Restore and Cancel 8 px apart (the 0.7.2 pair sat ~290 px apart in a 1046 px dialog).

![The Workspace Trust dialog](/images/issue-v0.8.0-dialog-trust.png)

**Empty workspace after Close All** - row: *An empty workspace has an intentional empty state* (closed). Box 1 is the guide: "No panes" and the shortcut that leaves that state, centred on the background token `#1a1a1a` instead of Godot's `rgb(76,76,76)`, and inert to the pointer; 2 the sidebar's pane section, where the `Panes:` heading no longer stands over an empty list.

![An emptied workspace](/images/issue-v0.8.0-empty-state.png)

**UI scale at 125 %** - row: *Shared theme, type scale, colour tokens and UI scale* (closed). The `ui-scale-125` capture state at 1920×1080: box 1 is the terminal text and 2 the sidebar, both scaled by the window's content scale, so a `custom_minimum_size`-bound control grows with the type rather than clipping it. The terminal's own font size stays a separate setting.

![The interface at 125 percent](/images/issue-v0.8.0-ui-scale-125.png)

**Icon buttons** - row: *Pane chrome: one icon-button treatment, signed off by the owner* (closed). Box 1 is the Add Pane grid: eight glyph-only actions with no cell behind them at rest (the pointer is parked, so no hover wash is shown); 2 Settings and Close All, which keep the filled text-button base because an icon and its word are one control; 3 a pane title bar's five actions, glyph-only at rest at the one shared target size.

![Icon buttons](/images/issue-v0.8.0-icon-buttons.png)

**File picker frame** - row: *A FileDialog still wears Godot's stock grey frame* (closed). Box 1 is the app's own header - the picker's title and a close control wired to the picker's cancel - above the engine's own path bar, list and button row, which are untouched; the grey engine title band is gone.

![The workspace-directory picker](/images/issue-v0.8.0-file-dialog.png)

**File Tree, missing root** - row: *The File Tree shows an empty list when its root cannot be listed, and says nothing* (closed). The `file-tree-missing-root` capture state on the fix build (1280×520, two tiles): box 1 is a tree with no root set ("No folder open"), 2 one pointed at a path this machine does not have ("Folder not found"); on the unfixed build both panes were blank trees.

![The File Tree's root states](/images/issue-v0.8.1-file-tree-missing-root.png)

**Pane spawn size** - row: *A fresh pane's child starts at 80×24 and only learns its real size 50 ms later* (closed). The `pane-spawn-size` capture state at 1280×760: the pane's program prints the PTY's own `stty size` before anything can have resized it, so the one line in the image - `41 rows, 129 cols` - is both the size its child was born at and the size it keeps. On the unfixed build the same tile's child started at 24×80 and was resized 50 ms later; a TUI that snapshots its size at startup kept the 80×24 layout until the next resize.

![A pane's child printing the size it was born at](/images/issue-v0.8.1-pane-spawn-size.png)

**Settings → Terminal's scrollbar** - row: *Every scrollable surface has a 0 px scrollbar* (closed). The `settings-terminal` capture state at 1920x1080, on the same 540x560 card the audit measured; its `settings-terminal-150` twin captures the same surface at a 150 % content scale, where the column is 12 device px. Box 1 is the tab's scrollbar column: 8 px of layout at the right inner edge of the tab - 8 px the `ScrollContainer` no longer gives its content - with the thumb as the lighter 6 px strip inside it, inset 1 px from the track and spanning 384 of the column's 441 px because the tab clears its card by only 62 px. On the unfixed build the bar measured (0, 441): no column, no grabber, nothing to drag.

![The Terminal tab's scrollbar](/images/issue-v0.8.1-scrollbars.png)

**Add Pane, named** - row: *Discoverability: pane names, shortcuts and the window-mode control* (closed). The `first-launch` capture state on the fix build at 1920×1080: the sidebar's Add Pane header reads `Add Pane:` beside the new question-mark Help button, and the window-mode dropdown at the top of the sidebar reads `System Frame` where the 0.7.2 *First launch* capture above shows `OS`. Pointing at a tile renames the heading to that type (`Add a Terminal Pane`); a capture parks the pointer, so the default title is what it shows.

![The sidebar's Add Pane header and window-mode dropdown](/images/issue-v0.8.1-first-launch.png)

**Add Pane help** - row: *Discoverability: pane names, shortcuts and the window-mode control* (closed). The `pane-help` capture state: the popup the header's Help button opens, one row per pane type - the glyph the tile draws, what pressing it adds, and the chord that does the same from the keyboard. The chord column is read from the binding registry (`Ctrl+Shift+N/D/T/O`, and the four types with no spawn chord leave it empty), so the table cannot advertise a key that does nothing; the intro sentence says what a press does and that Tab reaches the tiles.

![The Add Pane help table](/images/issue-v0.8.1-pane-help.png)

**Keyboard shortcuts** - row: *Discoverability: pane names, shortcuts and the window-mode control* (closed). The `shortcuts` capture state: Settings → About, the tab the palette's new `Keyboard Shortcuts` row opens, with the table grouped Workspace, New Panes, Panes, Keyboard Focus and Terminal. The card is a fixed 540×560, so the capture shows the top of the list - Workspace, New Panes and the start of Panes - and the tab's own scrollbar carries the rest. Every chord comes from `ShortcutManager.chord(action)` rather than a literal in the panel - `Ctrl+Shift+C` beside *Copy the selection* is the declared `term:copy` the terminal's own key path answers.

![The About tab's keyboard-shortcut table](/images/issue-v0.8.1-shortcuts.png)

**Scrollback return** - row: *A scrolled-back pane returns to live output in one click* (closed). The `scrollback-return` capture state: a pane is scrolled back, so the position readout `[Scroll: 15/260 lines]` and the floating caret-down chevron at the bottom-right both draw; the chevron's tooltip and accessible name read `Return to live output`. No boxes: one surface, controls named in words.

![The scrollback return chevron](/images/issue-v0.8.1-scrollback-return.png)

**Agent-state badges, silhouette weight** - row: *The `working` badge is a thin arc that reads as a dim dot at 12 px* (closed). The `agent-badges` capture state on the fix build (1280x420): left to right `working` (a painted notched ring), `completed` (check circle), `failed` (X-circle) and `needs-attention` (warning triangle, frozen at its dimmest pulse frame). The same four tiles measured 11.7 / 25.2 / 26.5 / 23.9 ink pixels before; after, at full opacity, 58.3 / 48.2 / 51.4 / 48.4. No boxes: one row of badges, named in order.

![The four agent-state badges, redrawn](/images/issue-v0.8.1-agent-badges.png)

**Stop running processes? at 800×500** - row: *A dialog can grow past the window it opens in* (closed). The `running-work` capture state at 800×500: four terminals in one workspace, each given `sleep 600` through the real input path, then the workspace closed - so the card carries the workspace-scoped intro and one line per busy pane, the longest text the app puts in a dialog. The card is bounded by its own limit (552×327: 0.75 of the window less a 24 px edge) and both buttons sit inside it; before the fix the autowrap body was shaped one word per line before its first layout and `popup_centered()` centred that height, which put `Close anyway` below the bottom edge. No boxes: one surface, named in words.

![The running-work confirm inside an 800 by 500 window](/images/issue-v0.8.1-running-work-800x500.png)

**Divider handle** - row: **Resizing a pane is smooth: the resize cursor always shows, no gray band while dragging, no hop on release**. The `resize-hover` capture state at 1920×1080: two terminals on one divider, the pointer resting on the grab band. The accent strip down the divider is the hover highlight - one 12 px band centred on the line, in place of the two 6 px strips whose 1 px wrapper borders left a dead ribbon between them - and both terminal bodies are one colour, the grid's own background, from edge to edge.

![The divider's grab band, highlighted on hover](/images/issue-v0.8.3-divider-handle.png)

**Resize drag** - the same row. The `resize-drag` capture state: the same two terminals with the button still down after a 60 px drag. The left pane has grown by the two whole grid units the preview snapped to and its shell has already reflowed to the new width (the 10 Hz drag throttle), the right pane has given up the space, and no band of a different grey shows between either pane's edge and its cells.

![A divider mid-drag: text reflowed, one background colour](/images/issue-v0.8.3-resize-drag.png)

**Foldable sidebar sections** - row: *The sidebar's three sections cannot be folded*. The `sidebar-folded` capture state at 1920×1080: Workspaces and Profiles are folded to their headers and the Pane list has the height they gave back, while the open `sidebar` state beside it is the same build unfurled. No boxes: one surface whole, named in words. The right-most control of each header is the fold - a down caret while open and an up caret while folded - and the section's own buttons sit ahead of it: add + close-all for Workspaces, save for Profiles, close-all for Panes, which now has a header row of its own.

![The sidebar with Workspaces and Profiles folded on 0.8.3](/images/issue-v0.8.3-sidebar-folded.png)

**Filtered palette, content-sized** - rows: *Settings cards and the command palette keep a fixed height their content does not fill* and *The capture harness compiles before autoloads, so pane-bearing states capture bare pane bodies*. The `palette-commands` state at 1920×1080 on the fix build: the query field sits where a full list puts it, and the card ends at the key-hint row under the last result instead of painting the rest of a fixed 480 px surface. The terminal prompt at the top left is the restored pane behind the overlay, which the harness fix put back into the capture.

![The filtered palette ending at its last row](/images/issue-v0.8.4-palette-filtered.png)

**A short Settings tab sizes the card** - rows: *Settings cards and the command palette keep a fixed height their content does not fill* and *The capture harness compiles before autoloads, so pane-bearing states capture bare pane bodies*. The `settings` state at 1920×1080 on the fix build: the System tab's content ends at `Reset tab to defaults`, and the card ends there too, at the design width and centred; the restoring terminal behind it is visible because the harness compiles its pane scripts again.

![The Settings card fitted to the System tab](/images/issue-v0.8.4-settings-short-tab.png)

**The vault graph as a physical 3D map** - row: **The vault graph is a physical 3D map you can push around**. The `wiki-graph` capture state at 1920×1080 on build 0.8.5: the bundled knowledge vault's 52 notes and 270 links drawn as the physical map, orbited slightly (the Orbit control is on) so the deterministic depth layers show as real parallax. The accent ring marks the open note (`Start Here`), the crosshair at the centre is the keyboard cursor, and the soft halos are the depth cue rather than boxes. The Planar / Orbit / Arrows / Relayout / Fit controls are the graph's one keyboard ring; the status and hint name the gestures.

![The vault graph as a physical 3D map](/images/issue-v0.8.5-3d-vault-graph.png)

**The wiki pane's view buttons and the graph chrome** - rows: *A workspace switch reopens the orbital graph on its last rendered frame* and *The wiki pane's view chrome duplicated its views and started on the wrong one*. The `wiki-list`, `wiki-vault` and `wiki-graph` states at 1920×1080 on build 0.8.5: the list shows **Graph** and **New**; the note editor shows **Notes** and **Graph**; the graph (a new pane's default) shows **Notes** and **Planar** / **Orbit** / **Arrows** / **Relayout**, with no **Physical** or **Fit** control. The graph repaints after the workspace round-trip instead of keeping its pre-switch frame.

![The wiki list showing Graph and New](/images/issue-v0.8.5-wiki-list.png)

![The note editor showing Notes and Graph](/images/issue-v0.8.5-wiki-note.png)

![The orbital graph showing Notes, Planar, Orbit, Arrows and Relayout](/images/issue-v0.8.5-wiki-graph.png)

**All eight Settings tabs on the 560x340 floor** - row: *The Settings tab bar overflows behind the engine's arrows at the 560 px floor* (closed). The `settings-560x340` capture state at 560x340 on the fix build: `System Terminal Appearance Reasoning Concepts Commands Shortcuts About` on one row, no overflow arrows, and the selected tab carrying the accent top edge. The row is the app's own `SURFACE`/`SURFACE_RAISED` box (6 px padding) rather than the engine default, and it starts at the card's inner margin; the last tab ends 15 px before the 512 px bar's right edge.

![All eight Settings tabs on the 560x340 floor](/images/issue-v0.8.6-settings-tabs-560x340.png)

**The branded launch frame** - row: *The launch frame is the engine's default logo on grey* (closed). A desktop capture of the first frames on the fix build (1920x1080, `scripts/screenshot-gpty` with `SCREENSHOT_CAPTURE=import`, which does not draw the pointer): the `icon.png` line-art mark and the `gPTY` wordmark on `#202128`, the same SURFACE the chrome uses, instead of Godot's logo on `#242424`. The 3840x2160 frame is drawn by the engine before any script, so it is one fixed dark picture; Keep Width keeps the mark uncropped at the 4:3 floor.

![The branded launch frame](/images/issue-v0.8.6-boot-splash.png)

**The terminal's colour pair beside the chrome** - row: *Colour schemes: import, and light mode that follows the system* (closed). The `scheme-pair` and `scheme-pair-light` capture states at 1280x760 on the fix build: the four terminals wear the dark slot's gruvbox `#282828` ground in the first and the light slot's solarized-light `#fdf6e3` in the second, while the chrome stays its own `UiPalette` set in both - the two palettes are independent by design, so the light capture's sidebar and title bars are `#f5f5f7`/`#ffffff` and its terminal is cream. The Appearance tab (the `light-settings` state) lists `Dark scheme:` and `Light scheme:` as dropdowns whose first item is the reset (`(built-in palette)` / `(same as dark)`), with no row clipped in the card.

![The dark scheme on the grid](/images/v0.8.6_scheme-pair.png)

![The light scheme on the grid](/images/v0.8.6_scheme-pair-light.png)

**The closed effect table on the grid** - row: *Dynamic Shaders* (closed). The `effect-crt` and `effect-crt-light` states at 1280x760: the CRT ground at Full over red, inverse and underlined text on the default black, then at Subtle on solarized-light; the scanline comb, phosphor mask and vignette live on the ground quad only, so the glyphs, the inverse block and the wrapper chrome stay untouched. `effect-grain` is film grain at Full, pinned to phase 0 so two captures are pixel-identical. `effect-cue` is a pinned Tint cue on its source pane (the 16 % peak, no ambient effect), and `settings-effects` is the complete Effects section: the closed table and intensity, then the reactive mapping rows with their empty state and Add picker. Row: *Reactive Environments* (closed).

![CRT scanlines at Full on the terminal ground](/images/v0.8.6_effect-crt.png)

![The same effect at Subtle on the light scheme](/images/v0.8.6_effect-crt-light.png)

![Film grain at Full](/images/v0.8.6_effect-grain.png)

![A pinned Tint cue on the pane that fired the concept](/images/v0.8.6_effect-cue.png)

![The Effects section with its mapping table](/images/v0.8.6_settings-effects.png)
